- Status: open

### F-059: Missing-CLI addressed requests are silently dropped after one boot warning
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-listener.js` (`handleTrigger` early-returns on `!spawner.claudeAvailable()`; the one-time `cliWarned` notice fires in `start()`)
- Found during: Channels v1.1 adversarial review (2026-07-26)
- Severity: bug (dropped request — requester gets no signal)
- Description: when the Claude CLI can't be resolved on PATH, the listener shows a single "CLI not found" notification at startup, then every addressed/implicit trigger is dropped in `handleTrigger` with only a `diag()` line — deliberately no error reply into the channel (avoids leaking local machine state / spamming the thread). Consequence: a teammate who addresses this operator's agent gets **no response and no indication** the operator's machine can't answer; the request just vanishes (the cursor has already advanced, so it won't re-prompt).
- Proposed resolution: defer — decide a signal that doesn't leak local state: e.g. post a terse channel-visible "operator unavailable" once per channel per outage, or reflect an availability/presence flag on the member so requesters can see the agent is offline before addressing it.
- Resolution (partial, Channels v1.2 2026-07-26): the **presence half shipped** — `agent_presence` + the desktop heartbeat (§8/§18) drive `agentOnline`/`lastSeenAt` on the roster, and the composer warns before you send ("their agent is offline" / "has never connected"). That covers the never-set-up and app-not-running cases. **Still open:** the specific drop this entry names — the app IS running and heartbeating (so it reads as online) but `spawner.claudeAvailable()` is false, so the trigger is dropped in `handleTrigger` with only a `diag()` line and the requester still gets nothing. Presence cannot express "listening but cannot execute".
- Status: open (presence/offline signalling shipped 2026-07-26; the CLI-missing silent drop remains)

### F-060: No post rate limit or metadata size cap on channel messages
- Location: `src/features/channels/**` (`schema.ts` message schema, `server/service-writes.ts` `postMessage`, `app/api/channels/[channelId]/messages/route.ts`)
- Found during: Channels v1.1 adversarial review (2026-07-26)
- Severity: smell (abuse / scale)
- Description: message posts are gated only by channel membership. There is **no per-user/per-channel post rate limit** (an agent in a loop, or a runaway listener, can append unboundedly — each insert takes the per-channel advisory lock, so a hot poster also serializes the channel) and **no overall cap on `metadata` size** (the `summary` field is length-capped, but the free-form `metadata` jsonb blob is not), so a large structured payload rides straight into the row. Not a live incident at current scale, but the abuse surface is open.
- Proposed resolution: defer — add a token-bucket post limit (per `(user, channel)`) surfaced as 429, and a byte cap on the serialized `metadata` in the message schema.
- Status: open

### F-061: Workspace admins have no visibility into private channels (governance gap — deliberate v1 posture)
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/{service-shared.ts (loadVisibleChannel), service-reads.ts (listChannels)}`; RLS member-SELECT policies in `20260725120000_channels.sql`
- Found during: Channels v1.1 adversarial review (2026-07-26)
- Severity: question (governance decision)
- Description: private-channel reads are gated on channel MEMBERSHIP, not workspace role — so a workspace owner/admin who was never invited to a private channel cannot list it, read it, or moderate it (RLS + service both scope to members). This is the intentional v1 privacy posture (a private channel is private even from admins), but it means there is **no admin/governance override** for compliance, offboarding, or abuse review of private channels in a workspace they own.
- Proposed resolution: needs-user-decision — hold as an open decision. If governance wins over privacy: add an admin read/override path (audited, role-gated) or a workspace policy that makes private channels admin-visible. Documented as an open v1 decision, not a fix.
- Status: open (question)

### F-063: `onlineMemberCount` costs 2 extra queries on every channel LIST read and is rendered nowhere
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-reads.ts` (`listChannels` + `getChannel` → `collab.channelMemberUserIds(ids)` + `collab.presenceForWorkspace(ctx.workspaceId)` feeding `onlineCounts()`); exposed at `server/dto.ts:78,135` and `types.ts:114`; the "nothing renders it" note is already in `components/channels-view.tsx:157`
- Found during: Channels v1.2 adversarial review (2026-07-26)
- Severity: smell (waste / scale)
- Description: every channel-list read fans out two extra queries — a workspace-wide presence scan plus a per-channel member fan-out — purely to compute `onlineMemberCount` per channel. **Nothing in the UI renders it:** the channel header derives its "N listening" from the ROSTER read instead, which is exactly why `channels-view.tsx` deliberately keeps presence-realtime events from refetching the channel list. So the list read pays a per-workspace scan on every fetch for a field with no consumer, and it grows with member count × channel count.
- Proposed resolution: defer — either drop `onlineMemberCount` from the list DTO entirely (keep it on `getChannel` if a future header wants it) or make it lazy behind an explicit `?withPresence=1`. If it is ever rendered in the list, it also needs the realtime refetch path the comment currently avoids.
- Status: open

### F-064: Consent expiry is lazy-only — no cron sweep, and an expiring card emits no realtime event
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/consent-service.ts` (`collab.expireStalePending(ctx.userId)` called at the top of create / list / get / decide); `vercel.json` `crons` has no consent entry; `CONSENT_TTL_MS = 24h` (raised from 30 min in desktop v1.4 so the lazy sweep can't evict a parked request; `features/channels/constants.ts`)
- Found during: Channels v1.2 adversarial review (2026-07-26)
- Severity: smell (UX / correctness-at-the-edge)
- Description: a pending consent request past its `expires_at` only becomes `expired` when the operator's NEXT request runs the lazy sweep. Nothing flips it on a timer, so no row is written at the TTL boundary, so **no WAL change and therefore no realtime event fires** — the web consent card sits there with live Allow/Deny buttons until some other fetch happens to sweep it. Correctness is preserved (the sweep runs before every read AND before the de-dupe read, so an elapsed row is never handed back as live), but the surface lies for as long as the page is idle, and the desktop's poll backoff (up to 20s) widens the window on that side too. Desktop v1.4 raised the TTL to 24h (parked requests), so an idle web Pending Requests card can now show live Allow/Deny for up to ~24h before some read happens to sweep it — widening this window and strengthening the case for the cron sweep.
- Proposed resolution: defer — add a `/api/cron/expire-consent` sweep (`CRON_SECRET`-gated, wired in `vercel.json` like `purge-trash`) that flips elapsed pending rows workspace-wide; the resulting UPDATE rides the existing realtime publication, so the card self-clears with no client change. Keep the lazy sweep as the correctness backstop.
- Status: open

### F-067: A failed consent PATCH from the notification-Allow action is silent
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/consent-watcher.js` / `consent.js` (the native-notification Allow/Send handler PATCHes `channel_consent_requests`); web backstop = the pending consent cards at the bottom of the channel transcript (`src/features/channels/components/channel-thread.tsx` + `consent-card.tsx`; the old `pending-requests-panel.tsx` band was deleted in v2.4)
- Found during: Channels consent redesign (Round B, desktop v1.4, 2026-07-27)
- Severity: smell (silent failure — no operator signal)
- Description: when the operator clicks **Allow/Send** on the native consent notification, the desktop PATCHes the consent row to `allowed`. If that PATCH fails (offline, 5xx, token expired, or a lost CAS race that is NOT the settled-decision 409 case), **nothing tells the operator it didn't take** — the notification has already dismissed and the request silently stays `pending`. The **web Pending Requests list is the backstop** (the row is still there, still answerable), but a notification-only operator gets no signal and may believe they approved a spawn that never started. Mirrors the silent-drop shape of F-059.
- Proposed resolution: defer — surface a failed PATCH (re-notify "couldn't record your decision — open Pending Requests", or re-raise the request) instead of swallowing it. The web list already covers the recovery path, so this is a signalling gap, not a correctness one.
- Status: open

### F-068: Per-channel directory is context + a default, not a hard filesystem fence (sandbox-exec is a future option)
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-dirs.js` (`channelDirs` map → spawn `cwd`); `session-spawner.js` (applies the cwd to headless + terminal spawns)
- Found during: Channels directory picker (Round C, desktop v1.4, 2026-07-27)
- Severity: smell (containment-boundary clarity)
- Description: the per-channel working directory sets the spawn's `cwd` (and thus the agent's default read/write root), but it is **not** an enforced filesystem boundary — a spawned agent with Bash/write tools can still `cd ..` or touch absolute paths outside the chosen folder. Actual containment is the **tool profile** (§18 v1.2) + the **two consent gates**; the directory is context and a default, deliberately. Documented as the KEY PRINCIPLE in ENGINEERING §18 "Desktop app v1.4" so no future session mistakes cwd for a fence. (The terminal-mode untrusted-prompt file stays in the sandbox regardless of cwd, so the directory never widens the untrusted-input surface — only where the agent works.)
- Proposed resolution: defer — a true per-directory fence needs an OS sandbox (`sandbox-exec`/seatbelt profile, or a container) wrapping the spawn so the process physically cannot escape the folder. Optional hardening layered on top of the tool profile, not a v1.4 requirement; revisit if operators point untrusted channels at sensitive real directories.
- Status: open

### F-070: Channels v1.5 (first-class tasks + DMs + engagement mode) — deferred debt
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-writes.ts` (`closeTask`/`setTaskMode`/`createDirectChannel`); `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-write.ts` (`opCloseThread`/`opSetThreadMode`); `src/features/channels/components/{channels-view,channels-list-pane,direct-message-dialog}.tsx`; `dopl-desktop-app/main/{targeting,trigger}.js`; `channel_tasks` table
- Found during: Channels v1.5 build + adversarial review (2026-07-27)
- Severity: smell (bundle — several deferred items; item 3 is an open product question, not a bug)
- Description: five items deliberately deferred out of the v1.5 round (first-class tasks, direct channels, engagement mode). Two HIGH DM-invariant bugs + a suppression-binding gap were caught and FIXED in-round (the soft-deleted-DM revive path and the DM membership/visibility immutability enforcement; the `task-reply` verdict binding to `taskTarget === author`); the items below are what was left for later.
  - **1. Threads never auto-close (rot `active`).** SUPERSEDED BY **F-105** (2026-07-31), which re-derives the lifecycle, confirms nothing anywhere auto-closes or expires, and adds the three consequences measured on a live six-open-thread DM — including the one that is not gradual: DM thread inheritance switches OFF at the second open pair-thread. Resolve it there, not here.
  - **2. `set_thread_mode` posts no message → mode change is realtime-invisible.** Unlike `create_thread`/`close_thread` (each posts a `channel_message` that rides realtime → refetch), `setTaskMode` writes only the `channel_tasks` row and posts nothing, so it fires no messages-realtime and does not trigger the threads refetch. The web mode badge is eventually consistent — it updates only on the NEXT `useChannelThreads` refetch (a send, a `create_thread`/`close_thread` echo, or a manual reload). Desktop is unaffected (mode is stamped fresh at each post, §8 v1.5), so this is a web-badge staleness only.
  - **3. `closeTask` lets the TARGET declare `outcome=completed`.** Authorization allows creator OR target to close, and the closer sets `outcome ∈ completed|failed` freely, so the responder (target) can mark their own thread `completed`. By-design under the workspace-trust posture (channel members are trusted teammates, same posture as F-061), but a product may later want the CREATOR to confirm a completed outcome ("responder proposes, requester accepts") rather than letting the responder self-attest done. This is a product question, not a bug.
  - **4. Autonomous auto-continuation not built.** Engagement mode ships only the INTERACTIVE-suppression half (ENGINEERING §18 v1.5 `task-reply`). `autonomous` mode is accepted and stored but inert — no task-scoped standing consent, no per-task `--resume`, no turn caps — so an `autonomous` task's reply just falls through to the normal consent trigger each turn (no auto-continue). Next-round feature work (see ENGINEERING §8 v1.5 engagement-mode + the claude 2.1.220 resume research facts), not a fix.
  - **5. DM revive semantics undocumented in the UI.** Deleting a direct channel is hide-until-reopened (`deleted_at` hides it; a later "New direct message" to the same peer revives the same row with history intact), but the UI labels the action "Delete conversation" with no hint that history survives and returns on reopen. A user may reasonably expect delete to be destructive.
- Proposed resolution: defer — per item: (1) auto-close on a confirmed `task_finished`/`task_failed` from the target, or a TTL/idle sweep, or keep close explicit but surface a "still open" affordance; (2) post a lightweight system message on `set_thread_mode`, or have the threads query also refetch on the messages-realtime tick; (3) needs-user-decision — hold as an open product question (requester-confirms-completion vs responder-self-attests); (4) next-round feature work (standing consent + per-thread resume + turn caps); (5) copy/UX — clarify the delete label or note that a DM reopens with its history.
- Status: open

### F-071: Desktop load-guard — render-process recreate + sleep/wake verification pending
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/load-guard.js`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/api.js` (`resetPool`), `dopl-desktop-app/main/index.js` (`onWake`)
- Found during: Desktop resilience round (never-black window + fast wake recovery, 2026-07-27)
- Severity: smell (verification + edge-case robustness)
- Description: the load guard fixes the post-wake black-window/hung-load bug (watchdog stops a hung load, drops the Chromium pool via `session.closeAllConnections()` and the main-process undici pool via `api.resetPool()`, keeps a local loading screen up, and retries on a 0/2/5/10s backoff). Two residuals: (a) the "never blank" guarantee leans on Chromium **paint-holding** (the local loading/offline frame stays on screen during the subsequent `loadURL` fetch) — verified by design and by the pure reducer's unit tests, but the actual sleep/wake + wifi-change recovery **cannot be tested headlessly** and needs one manual pass (close lid, reopen: window should show the loading screen then content within seconds, never black); (b) `render-process-gone`/`unresponsive` currently **reloads** through the guard rather than recreating the `BrowserWindow` — sufficient for a normal renderer crash (Electron respawns the process on `loadURL`), but a wedged GPU/utility process that survives the reload would need a full window recreate. `resetPool()` swaps the undici global dispatcher for a fresh instance of its **own class** (dependency-free, version-matched) rather than `require('undici')` (only a dev-transitive dep, not bundled) — robust, but a future Node/Electron that renamed the `undici.globalDispatcher.1` global symbol would silently no-op it (the per-request AbortController still bounds any dead socket, so worst case is today's ~minutes recovery, not a hang forever).
- Proposed resolution: defer — (a) Samuel runs the manual sleep/wake + wifi-flip check once against a packaged build and confirms the loading screen appears and content arrives in seconds; (b) escalate `render-process-gone` to a window recreate if a reload-in-place proves insufficient in the field; (c) if the global-dispatcher symbol ever changes, `resetPool` degrades safely to a no-op — revisit only if wake recovery regresses.
- Status: open

### F-072: 2026-07-27 prod CPU incident — read-watermark realtime loop (fixed), reconnect-storm hardening deferred
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-reads.ts` (`readMessages`), `src/features/channels/server/repository.ts` (`updateLastRead`), `src/shared/realtime/use-workspace-tables-realtime.ts`, `supabase/migrations/20260728010000_drop_channel_tasks_from_realtime.sql`
- Found during: prod incident forensics (shared-CPU instance pinned at 100% max for the day vs a ~5-15% weekly baseline; realtime service in a connect/teardown storm; statement timeouts across the board)
- Severity: bug (root cause FIXED in this commit; hardening items deferred)
- Description: `readMessages` bumped `channel_members.last_read_at` to `now()` on EVERY read. `channel_members` is realtime-subscribed (CHANNEL_TABLES), so each bump emitted a postgres_changes event that re-fired every subscribed tab's refetch — which called `readMessages` again: a self-sustaining cross-tab refetch loop, rate-limited only by round-trip latency. Evidence: 86,541 updates on a 6-row `channel_members` table, 2.5M realtime per-WAL RLS evaluations (~5.2 CPU-hours), 2.7M subscription reconciliations, 1.45M realtime.subscription insert/delete cycles (reconnect storm amplification once the DB slowed), 4.39M PostgREST requests. Post-restart churn measured at zero for 60s, confirming no OTHER standing loop. FIXED: watermark is now content-derived (newest message shown, not `now()`) and monotonic at both the service layer and the repository layer (`.or(last_read_at.is.null,last_read_at.lt.<at>)`), so a refetch that shows nothing new writes nothing; `channel_tasks` dropped from the realtime publication (zero subscribers, pure decode cost — migration 20260728010000, applied to prod).
- Proposed resolution: deferred hardening — (a) reconnect circuit breaker in `use-workspace-tables-realtime.ts`: after K consecutive CHANNEL_ERROR/TIMED_OUT cycles, stop resubscribing until visibilitychange/online (today's capped 15s backoff × every hook instance × every tab still hammers a degraded DB); (b) periodic churn check: alert if `realtime.subscription` insert rate spikes; (c) consider dropping `agent_presence` heartbeat UPDATEs to a coarser interval if presence fan-out ever shows up hot.
- Status: open (root cause fixed; hardening deferred)

### F-073: Channels receipts — no delivery/read acknowledgment signal exists
- Location: `src/features/channels/lib/message-receipt.ts`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-listener.js` (would-be ack emitter)
- Found during: Channels v1.6 (message/task differentiation + receipts, 2026-07-27)
- Severity: smell (product gap, deliberately not faked)
- Description: the v1.6 receipt line reports only transcript-provable states (Sent, Accepted-working via task_started, Replied, and the terminal echoes). There is NO "Received" or "Read" state because the responder's desktop never acknowledges delivery, and fabricating one would lie to the sender. A real ack needs a design that respects F-072: per-message ack writes would recreate WAL fan-out on a realtime-subscribed table, so a future ack must be low-frequency (e.g. a coarse per-channel "listener saw up to seq N" watermark, written at most once per poll cycle and monotonic like last_read_at post-fix, or piggybacked on an existing write).
- Proposed resolution: defer — design a coarse listener-seen watermark alongside the autonomous-continuation round; until then the receipt line stays transcript-derived only.
- Status: open

### F-078: Session Window (v1.9) — residual notes + deferred hardening
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-engine.js`, `session-profiles.js`, `session-io.js`, `channel-listener.js`, `renderer/session/**`
- Found during: v1.9 Session Window build + 2 security reviews + fix pass + independent verification (2026-07-28)
- Severity: smell (feature shipped SHIP-verified; items below are deferred, not blockers)
- Description: the SDK-driven session window shipped with all six review blockers (H1 dopl_channel blanket pre-approval, H2 full hard-denies-nothing, H3 subagent delegation, M1 fail-open decision, M2 permissionMode/env, L1 unbound feed) fixed and independently re-verified closed. Residuals:
  - **"Allow for this task" is tool-NAME scoped, not input-scoped.** Approving `Bash` once blesses every later shell command that session; a `Write` grant for path A is honored for path B. Contract-sanctioned (§A.5) and requires an informed click on a watched window, but it widens one click's blast radius. Future: scope task-grants to a normalized command-prefix / path signature, or surface the coarseness in button copy.
  - **Turn cap counts SDK `result` events, not tool calls.** 24 user-turns, not 24 actions; one turn can hold many tool calls (bounded per-tool only by the gate/allow-for-task). Document; consider an action budget.
  - **BashOutput/KillShell hard-denied while Bash gated (full).** Background-shell output is unreadable when Bash is granted — more restrictive than intended, not a hole. Revisit if background shells become a real need.
  - **Own-channel post addressed by slug gates instead of auto-allowing.** `isOwnChannelPost` compares the input `channel` to the session channel id; a slug-addressed own-channel delivery gates (safe direction, minor UX friction).
  - **Subagent gating inheritance never empirically proven.** Mitigated by hard-denying Task/Agent in session mode, so untested-but-blocked; if delegation is ever re-enabled it MUST be proven that a subagent inherits canUseTool + settingSources + disallowedTools first.
  - **Silent-end cards.** Operator End / idle-timeout / cost-cap post no lifecycle echo, so the requester's web card can read "active" until Closed/Reopened/resumed (intended per §A.3; note for watchers).
- Proposed resolution: defer all; none blocks v1.9. Revisit input-scoped grants + the action budget alongside the autonomous-continuation hardening.
- Status: open

### F-09x: Avatar-cache SSRF — DNS-rebind residual
- Location: dopl-desktop-app/main/avatar-cache.js (isSafeAvatarHost / getDataUri)
- Found during: Session Window v2.2 review (2026-07-29)
- Severity: smell (low residual; primary vectors closed)
- Description: getDataUri fetches a member's profiles.avatar_url as the only new remote-fetch surface. Guards enforced: https-only, redirect:'error', raster image/* content-type, declared+actual <=256KB, 4s timeout, bounded pos/neg cache, and now isSafeAvatarHost blocking IP-literal + localhost/.local/.internal internal targets (169.254.169.254 metadata, 127./10./172.16-31./192.168./100.64-127., ::1, fc00::/7, fe80::/10, mapped-v4). RESIDUAL: a PUBLIC hostname that DNS-rebinds to an internal IP is not caught (the guard is on the URL host string, not the resolved IP). Bounded because avatar_url is NOT user-settable (the profile PATCH allowlist excludes it; it comes from Google OAuth), redirect:'error' blocks the 302-to-internal bypass, and the fetch is an image-only, no-exfil GET rendered only in the operator's own window as a data: URI.
- Proposed resolution: defer — if avatar sourcing ever becomes user-influenced, add a resolve-then-check (dns.lookup the host, reject a private resolved IP) or a host allowlist (googleusercontent.com + the Supabase storage host). Until then the sync literal guard is proportional.
- Status: open

### F-079: Server-side DM auto-address + task inheritance — residuals
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts`, `src/features/channels/server/service-writes.ts` (postMessage)
- Found during: cross-user DM delivery-bug fix (2026-07-29)
- Severity: smell (the delivery bug itself is fixed; items below are accepted deltas / follow-ups)
- Description: `postMessage` now stamps `metadata.to_user_id` = the DM peer when a direct-channel post carries no `to`, and inherits the channel's single open first-class task id when an untagged plain message is addressed to that peer. Residuals:
  - **Two extra reads per DM post.** `listMembers` runs on every direct-channel post (peer resolution) and `listTasksByChannel` additionally when the post has no `taskId`. Both are indexed single-channel reads and neither runs for a non-direct channel, but a hot DM now costs 2 queries more than before. Cheapest future fix: carry the peer + open-task set on the already-loaded channel context instead of re-reading them.
  - **`notify_scope='none'` no longer mutes an agent-authored DM ask.** Same accepted delta the web composer took in v1.6, now extended to the MCP post path: an explicitly addressed message deliberately bypasses the mute (an addressed request must never be dropped), so a DM that used to arrive as a silent FYI can now raise a prompt. A per-channel "quiet DM" preference would need its own design.
  - **Inheritance is direct-channel only.** In a channel with three or more members an untagged reply still lands untagged, so a session reply there must pass `task=<id>` explicitly. Widening it needs a participant-pair rule that cannot mis-attribute a third member's turn.
- Proposed resolution: defer all three; revisit the read cost alongside the next channels perf pass (F-063 touches the same read path).
- Status: open

### F-080: Desktop Tier 1 security round (contract v2.9 §C) — residuals
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/sdk-loader.js`, `main/mcp-config.js`, `main/session-engine.js`, `main/session-outbound.js`, `main/session-replay.js`, `main/session-park.js`, `main/session-seed.js`
- Found during: contract v2.9 Tier 1 fixes C1-C7 (2026-07-30)
- Severity: smell (the shipped fixes close the primary paths; the items below are the accepted remainder)
- Description: C1 took the 90-day device token off disk for the SDK path (the bearer now comes from mcp-config's safeStorage cache and is injected into the in-memory `mcpServers` object) and added a tool-BOUND `Read/Grep/Glob` deny over `userData` + `~/.claude*` on every session profile, because a pre-approved tool is shadowed by `allowedTools` and never reaches `canUseTool`. C2 pinned `MCP_URL` and made `writeSpawnConfig` compare the whole serialized config. C3 made `settle` deny every awaited permission, close the push iterator and abort. C4 routed the continuation's counterparty name through `framing.sanitizeName`. C5 pinned `init` as the replay ring's sticky head and split the avatar data URIs onto the `avatars` event. C6 emits a resolving `outbound_gate` + `permission_resolved` pair for an auto-allowed own-channel post. C7 made `startResume` fail restrictive on a stored profile. Residuals:
  - **The HEADLESS/CLI spawn path keeps the plaintext `mcp-spawn.json`.** `session-spawner` still passes `--mcp-config <userData>/mcp-spawn.json`, and `tool-profiles.buildRestrictionArgs` emits no path deny (and nothing at all for `full`), so a headless spawn's pre-approved `Read` can still open it. Fix shape: thread an `extraDenyRules` argument (the same rules `sdk-loader.buildSecretPathDenyRules()` builds) into `buildRestrictionArgs` + `writeScopedSettings`, and emit `--disallowedTools` for `full` too.
  - **`Read(...)`-style path rules on `Grep`/`Glob` are emitted but unproven.** The rules are documented for file tools; unrecognized rules are harmless no-ops on this CLI, so the deny is best-effort for those two and load-bearing for `Read`.
  - **A permissive tool mode can still read the credential dirs via the shell.** The deny binds `Read/Grep/Glob`, not `Bash("cat …")`; under a mode that auto-allows Bash the operator has explicitly granted shell for that session.
  - **safeStorage-unavailable fallback.** `mcp-config` still caches the token as plaintext under the electron-store key when `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` is false (unchanged behavior, now inside the denied `userData` path).
- Proposed resolution: defer; do the headless deny-rule threading with the next spawner pass.
- Status: open

### F-081: Channels vocabulary v3.0 (THREAD / SESSION) — residual `task` identifiers + a component name clash
- Location: `src/features/channels/**`, `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel*.ts`, `packages/dopl-client/src/channel*.ts`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md` §8
- Found during: contract v3.0 Track B (web + MCP + client vocabulary cutover, 2026-07-30)
- Severity: smell (the domain surface is correct; the items below are the accepted remainder)
- Description: the product called ONE exchange a "task", which is wrong (it may be a single message). v3.0 renames the domain to THREAD (one shared exchange in a channel, both members see it) and SESSION (one member's agent run working that thread, on their own machine). Shipped: MCP hard cutover (`create_thread` / `close_thread` / `set_thread_mode` / `list_threads` / `get_thread`, `post thread=`), `@dopl/client` method + type renames, web types (`ChannelThread` / `ThreadStatus` / `ThreadMode` / `ThreadOutcome`), `useChannelThreads`, `thread-panel.tsx`, and the visible copy. Storage names are deliberately untouched (`channel_tasks`, `metadata.task*`, `task_*` kinds, `TASK_*` error codes, `/tasks` routes) with a boundary comment at each mapping point. Residuals:
  - ~~**The `ChannelThread` COMPONENT clashed with the `ChannelThread` TYPE.**~~ RESOLVED in the same round: the component is now `ChannelPane` in `components/channel-pane.tsx` (with its test file), and `channels-view.tsx` imports it under that name. Creating a same-name ambiguity inside the round whose purpose was naming clarity was not worth deferring. ~~**`MessageThread` (the transcript) still has the same shape one level down.**~~ RESOLVED 2026-07-30: it renders the whole channel transcript (every message, session card, activity row), not one thread, so it is now `ChannelTranscript` in `components/channel-transcript.tsx` (with its test file); `channel-pane.tsx` is the only import site, and `thread-panel.tsx`'s doc-comment pointer follows the new filename. Mechanical rename only — no behavior or copy change.
  - ~~**`TaskOverlay` / `SessionGroup.taskId` in `lib/group-thread.ts` keep the `task` spelling.**~~ SPLIT + PART-RESOLVED 2026-07-30: `TaskOverlay` is a pure domain concept with no wire counterpart (the authoritative status/title/mode for ONE thread), so it is now **`ThreadOverlay`**. `SessionGroup.taskId` is KEPT on purpose — it is the `metadata.taskId` wire value verbatim, so it sits on the storage side of the boundary and now carries a BOUNDARY comment saying so; it renames only when storage migrates. The `groupThread(messages, taskOverlays?)` parameter name is likewise kept: the map is keyed by the wire task id.
  - **The server lane keeps `task` throughout** (`service-tasks.ts`, `mapTaskRow`, `TaskCreateSchema`, `service-reads.listChannelTasks`, `TaskNotFoundError`). Intentional per the contract (that lane speaks to storage), but it means a reader crossing from `client/api.ts` to `server/service-tasks.ts` changes vocabulary mid-call-stack.
  - **The `X-MCP-Tool` telemetry labels were renamed with the ops** (`channel_list_tasks` → `channel_list_threads`, etc.), so `mcp_tool_calls` rows split across the old and new label at the cutover date. No consumer reads them yet; a future analytics query must union both.
  - **A NEW INSTANCE OF THIS RESIDUAL SHIPPED, AND IT HAD A FUNCTIONAL COST (2026-07-31).** The desktop's spawn prompt (`dopl-desktop-app/main/prompt-framing.js`) taught the thread argument as **`task=<id>` / `task "<id>"`** in THREE places — the delivery call, the v3.0 VOCABULARY block, and the milestone line. `dopl_channel` has no `task` parameter: the cutover made the agent-facing argument `thread` and kept `taskId` only as the STORAGE key the op folds it into. So the WAKE-V1 thread-tagging fix (which exists precisely so a session's reply is not read as a fresh request on the peer's machine) was **inert on the primary, window-mode path** — the prompt looked correct and named nothing. Fixed in the Q5 round; `test/prompt-framing.test.mjs` now asserts that NO built turn teaches a `task` ARGUMENT anywhere while the `kind="task_*"` post kinds (which are real) survive. **The lesson for the storage migration below:** the residual is not cosmetic wherever the old word sits on an AGENT-FACING surface, because there the two vocabularies are not synonyms — one of them is a parameter that does not exist. Prompt/description text that names a tool argument must be treated as API surface. (A second, independent failure of prompt-only tagging — the agent omitting a correctly-named argument — is why `main/session-outbound-tag.js` now forces the tag in `canUseTool`; see §18.)
  - **A storage migration is the real fix** and is still open: `channel_tasks` → `channel_threads` plus the `metadata.task*` keys. It should be sequenced with the `createTask` atomicity bug (F-070), since both rewrite the same insert path.
- Proposed resolution: defer all; the component rename is the highest-value next slice and is safe to do alone.
- Status: open

### F-083: Channels server audit round (B1-B4) — residuals
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-tasks.ts`, `server/service-writes-metadata.ts`, `server/service-writes.ts`, `server/repository.ts`, `server/repository-messages.ts`
- Found during: six-agent audit server lane (2026-07-30) — see `docs/ENGINEERING.md` §8 "v3.1 additions"
- Severity: smell (all four bugs are fixed; the items below are the accepted remainder)
- Description: B1 made `create_thread` convergent (the initiating message carries a derived `task-open-<threadId>` key and is re-driven on every path that returns the thread), B2 made the calm-terminal flags reserved keys re-stamped only for a thread participant, B3 gated a caller-supplied first-class `metadata.taskId` on `created_by`/`target_user_id` (403), B4 made slug allocation include soft-deleted channels. Residuals:
  - **`create_thread` with NO `client_msg_id` still has no dedup at all.** The convergence rides on the caller's idempotency key, so a retry without one creates a SECOND thread rather than repairing the first, and the abandoned thread row stays behind with no message. Real fix is the PL/pgSQL RPC (or a server-derived key), which should be sequenced with the `channel_tasks` → `channel_threads` storage migration (F-081) since both rewrite the same insert path.
  - **A `client_msg_id` collision from another member still returns THEIR thread DTO.** `findTaskByClientId` is scoped to `(channel_id, client_msg_id)`, not to the caller, so a colliding key leaks that thread's title/creator/target. The post half is now closed (we only re-post when the caller is `created_by`), and the keys are caller-chosen UUIDs in practice, so this is a probe-only leak. Fix shape: scope the lookup by `created_by` and let a foreign hit fall through to the insert's 23505.
  - **~~Legacy `task-{channel}-{seq}` ids are still un-gated for THREADING (only the calm flags are checked).~~ CLOSED 2026-07-31 by the multiplayer wave — see F-110.** The middle path shipped: one shared resolver `isLegacyThreadParticipant` validates the id against its opening message's `{author, to_user_id}` pair, and a non-participant's tag is SILENTLY STRIPPED rather than 403'd (desktop 1.7.16 posts legacy ids for lifecycle events). Calm flags now follow that same tag decision instead of re-running the check. **Kept here, struck rather than deleted, because the paragraph below is the reasoning that shaped the fix.** Original text: A member can still stamp another pair's legacy id on an ordinary message and have it group into their legacy session card. Not extended to a hard gate because the desktop posts legacy lifecycle events whose opener may be unaddressed (pre-v1.6 rows exist in prod: one live `declined` row's seq-N opener carries no `to_user_id`), so a hard gate there would reject real desktop posts. The seq-N pair machinery now exists in `service-writes-metadata.ts` and could be widened once every opener is addressed (the server has auto-addressed DMs since v2.6).
  - **A thread with `target_user_id = NULL` is now writable only by its creator.** `TaskCreateSchema` requires `toUserId` so first-class threads always have a target (prod confirms: zero null-target rows), but any legacy/other path that produced one would refuse the other side's posts.
  - **`isLegacyThreadParticipant` costs one extra read** — only on a post that carries a calm flag AND a legacy id (the desktop's lifecycle echoes), indexed on `(channel_id, seq)`. Same read-cost family as F-079.
- Proposed resolution: defer all; fold the first two into the storage-migration round (F-081) that rewrites the insert path anyway.
- Status: open

### F-085: No server-side revoke for an MCP device token, so desktop sign-out cannot fully revoke
- Location: `src/app/api/auth/mcp-device-token/route.ts`, `src/shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts` (`issueDeviceToken`), `dopl-desktop-app/main/mcp-config.js` (`clearDeviceToken`), `dopl-desktop-app/main/auth-state.js` (`signOut`)
- Found during: Q5 adversarial review (2026-07-31) — item S2
- Severity: bug (security; the local half is fixed, the server half is the open work)
- Description: `signOut()` cleared the Supabase blob and the cookie jar and stopped there. The app holds a SECOND credential the operator never sees: a 90-day `dopl.read`+`dopl.write` MCP device token, cached in electron-store (`mcpDeviceToken` / `mcpDeviceTokenPlain`) and written into `userData/mcp-spawn.json` (mode 600, bearer in the Authorization header). Signing out therefore left full API access to the just-signed-out account sitting on disk for anything that can read the app's data dir — including the next operator to sign in on that machine. The local teardown now exists (`clearDeviceToken` removes both store keys, unlinks `mcp-spawn.json`, and clears the memoized in-process copy), but there is **no server-side revoke to call**: the route is POST-only (mint), and `issueDeviceToken` revokes prior tokens only for the SAME `(user, label)` on the NEXT mint. So the token stays valid server-side until it expires (90 days) or the operator finds it in the web "Connected apps" list. Second, smaller residual: the user-scope `dopl` entry the CLI keeps in its own config still carries the bearer; removing it means resolving the claude binary and running a 25s-timeout child process on a click that must feel instant, and it is the operator's global CLI config rather than app state.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now — done (production-hardening batch 1, A2). `DELETE /api/auth/mcp-device-token` revokes by `label` and/or `tokenId`, `sessionOnly: true` like the mint — a bearer that could revoke device tokens could kill the credential its sibling agents (or the operator's other machines) depend on and delete the row whose `last_used_at` records it, the same privilege-escalation shape as the v1.2 consent/trust gates. `revokeDeviceTokens` scopes every revoke to `(user_id, client_id = dopl_client_device_cli, revoked_at IS NULL)`, so it can only touch the caller's own DEVICE tokens (an agent grant is revoked from Connected apps via `revokeGrant`) and an already-revoked row keeps its original timestamp. Revocation is the existing soft `revoked_at` stamp and `validateAccessToken` checks it BEFORE expiry, so the bearer dies on its next call. Idempotent: unknown / already-revoked ⇒ `{ ok: true, revoked: 0 }`; an empty body is a 400, never a revoke-everything. Desktop: `mcp-config.revokeDeviceToken()` presents the label the machine actually MINTED under (now persisted with the token, so a renamed host still hits the right row), and `signOut()` calls it FIRST — the route authenticates on the cookie jar sign-out is about to clear — bounded at 3s with an outer race (because `getAuthCookie()` can await a refresh before the fetch's own abort starts). Every failure path falls through to the local teardown and names the residual in the diag.
- Residual (kept, deliberate): the user-scope `dopl` entry in the CLI's own config still carries the bearer after sign-out. `ensureMcpConfig` only ADDS that entry when it was confirmed absent, so an entry that exists may be one the operator wrote with their own credential and is indistinguishable from ours from outside; deleting a hand-made global config entry is a worse failure than leaving a bearer the revoke has already made dead (it 401s, and the next sign-in refreshes it via the `lastMintWasFresh` path). Not worth a 25s child process on a click.
- Follow-up (FIX M4, adversarial review of batch 1): `revokeDeviceToken()` returned `true` when there was NO local record, and `signOut()` then logged "+ revoked server-side" for a request it had never sent. "We hold no copy" is not "the credential is dead" — the copies may have been cleared by an earlier sign-out whose revoke failed, or by a wiped store, while the 90-day row stayed live. It now returns `'revoked' | 'none' | 'failed'` and each gets its own sign-out line; a THROW is reported as `'failed'` (the one case where the DELETE may have been sent and its answer lost, so over-warning is the safe direction). It deliberately does NOT guess a label and fire a blind DELETE: the label is the only selector and it lives in the record we just failed to find, so a renamed host would get a 200 having revoked nothing — the same false confidence one layer down.
- Tests: `src/app/api/auth/mcp-device-token/route.test.ts` (auth matrix through the REAL `withUserAuth` — cookie ok, full-write bearer 403 `SESSION_REQUIRED`, read-only bearer 403, unauthenticated 401, per-method for POST and DELETE; selectors; idempotence), `src/shared/auth/mcp-oauth-device-token.test.ts` (revoke scoping + idempotence + de-dup + DB-error propagation, and that `validateAccessToken` refuses a revoked-but-unexpired row while the same row un-revoked still validates), `dopl-desktop-app/test/device-token-revoke.test.mjs` (DELETE + minted label, the bound, every failure path, and the signOut call order).
- Status: open (kept one cycle — landed; delete on the next prune)

### F-091: The realtime wake ships a whole `channel_messages` row to deliver ~36 bytes of signal (SQL written, NOT applied)
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/realtime.js` (`onInsert`, `wakeChannelId`), the `supabase_realtime` publication on `public.channel_messages`
- Found during: Q8 egress diet (production hardening batch 1, A3)
- Severity: smell (pure waste; no correctness or security impact — and F-072 blast radius, since every published byte is a byte a read-triggered write would multiply)
- Description: the desktop's push transport is deliberately WAKE-ONLY — it reads `channel_id` out of the INSERT payload and then refetches over the authed long-poll, which re-runs the server-side visibility gate. The web's `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime` ignores the payload entirely (its handler takes no arguments). Yet `channel_messages` is published to `supabase_realtime` with NO column list, so every INSERT fans out the full row — prod average 881 bytes, max 4 468 — plus Realtime's per-column type metadata, to every subscriber in the workspace, to communicate one uuid. Metered as egress on a project already at 182% of the free tier.
- Proposed resolution: needs-user-decision — a publication column list is the whole fix and it is a PROD DDL statement, so it is written here rather than applied. Nothing in the app changes: `wakeChannelId()` is the single extraction point and is indifferent to which other columns arrive (pinned by `dopl-desktop-app/test/realtime.test.mjs`, which asserts the wake id is identical for a full row and a narrowed one).

```sql
-- Narrow the realtime wake payload on channel_messages. Run as one transaction:
-- ALTER PUBLICATION ... SET TABLE replaces the ENTIRE table list, so the
-- DROP + ADD pair is the only correct way to change ONE table's column list.
BEGIN;
ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime DROP TABLE public.channel_messages;
ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE public.channel_messages
  (id, channel_id, workspace_id, seq, created_at);
COMMIT;

-- Rollback (restores the full-row publication):
-- BEGIN;
-- ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime DROP TABLE public.channel_messages;
-- ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE public.channel_messages;
-- COMMIT;
```

- Why exactly those five columns (verified against prod, 2026-07-31): `id` is the primary key and `channel_messages` is `REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT`, so Postgres REQUIRES the replica identity in any column list that publishes UPDATE/DELETE; `workspace_id` is the subscription `filter` both clients use AND is read by the RLS policy `channel_messages_member_select`; `channel_id` is the wake routing key and the policy's other input. `seq` + `created_at` are ~60 bytes kept for diagnosis (which insert woke us); dropping them is safe. Dropping `body` + `metadata` is where the win is.
- Expected effect: ~1.6 KB → ~0.6 KB per insert per subscriber (~62%), and the row body — the part that grows with agent output, up to 4.5 KB today — stops being fanned out at all.
- Verify after applying (do not skip — Realtime evaluates RLS against the WAL record, and a policy input that stopped being published fails CLOSED, i.e. silently no wakes). **Start the desktop app with `DOPL_WAKE_BYTES=1`** — since FIX L7 the byte measurement is opt-in (it JSON-stringified every payload on the hot wake path to feed a counter nothing decides on), and without the flag the state line reads `wakeBytes=off` and the insert line carries no `bytes=`. Then post one message and confirm (1) `listener.log` shows `realtime insert … bytes=` roughly a third of its previous value on the receiving Mac, (2) the web transcript still live-updates in an open tab, (3) `wakes=`/`wakeBytes=` in the transport state line keep advancing. If wakes stop, roll back with the block above — no app deploy is involved either way.
- Status: open (SQL not applied)

### F-092: The 60s client abort was a TRANSPORT choice, and two residuals it leaves behind
- Location: `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/shared/api/sse-keep-alive.ts`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/mcp-config.js` + `main/mcp-cli-entry.js` + `main/sdk-loader.js`, `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-await-budget.ts`
- Found during: Q9 (production hardening batch 1) + its adversarial review — items M1 / H1
- Severity: was HIGH (the wake primitive did not work for any client we cannot configure); now smell + two residuals
- Description: every long MCP call died at exactly 60.0s — `dopl_channel(op="await")`, but also `op="list"` and `dopl_kb` reads, so it was never await-specific. Claude Code aborts each non-GET fetch to a `type: "http"` server after `max(server.timeout ?? 60_000, 60_000)` ms, and that timer bounds **time-to-response-headers**. `/api/mcp` built its transport with `enableJsonResponse: true`, which makes the SDK withhold the entire response — headers included — until the tool handler returns, so the 60s header bound became a 60s WHOLE-CALL bound. The first round mistook it for a hold-length problem and shortened 240s → 215s, which fixed nothing.
- Resolution: `enableJsonResponse` dropped. The transport's default SSE path returns its `Response` synchronously after dispatching the message (the SDK defers the handler onto a promise chain), so headers flush at t≈0. This required no client configuration, which is the point: terminal Claude Code, claude.ai connectors, Claude Desktop and third-party clients are all fixed and none of them are ours to configure. Compatibility is not narrowed — the transport already 406s any POST whose `Accept` omits `text/event-stream`, in BOTH modes. `src/shared/api/sse-keep-alive.ts` wraps the response and emits an SSE comment every 15s so a ~215s silent-but-open stream is not killed by an intermediary's idle timeout.
- Residual 1 — **NOT VERIFIED AGAINST PRODUCTION.** The streaming behaviour is verified by unit test and by reading the SDK, not against Vercel. Two things must hold there and were not observed: headers really do flush before the first body byte, and nothing imposes a post-headers idle timeout under ~215s. Verify on the next deploy with a real long `op="await"` from a terminal Claude Code session with NO per-server `timeout` set (that is the configuration the fix exists for). If it fails, the fallback is the per-server `timeout` path, which is already in place for the desktop's own entries.
- Residual 2 — **PER-SERVER `timeout` BLAST RADIUS.** The desktop writes `MCP_CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS` (`290_000`, `main/mcp-config.js:73`) on every Dopl MCP entry it owns: `mcp-spawn.json` (`mcp-config.spawnConfigBody`) and the SDK path (`sdk-loader.js:92`). That value applies to EVERY call to the `dopl` server, not just `await`: a genuinely hung short op hangs a session for up to ~290s instead of 60s before it aborts. Accepted for now — the hold is the only op that can legitimately take minutes, and a 60s cap on it breaks the wake outright — but it is the reason the transport fix is the real one and these entries are belt-and-braces. If Residual 1 is confirmed in production, consider dropping the per-server timeouts entirely rather than keeping two mechanisms. (The third writer this residual used to name, `main/mcp-cli-entry.js`, was DELETED — see F-096. `dopl-desktop-app/test/mcp-client-timeout.test.mjs` pins the relation `AWAIT_HOLD_CAP_MS + AWAIT_HOLD_MARGIN_MS <= CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS`, not a literal.)
- ~~Residual 3 — the `sdk-loader.js` comment claiming `/api/mcp` "answers with enableJsonResponse".~~ **RESOLVED** (verified 2026-07-31): `main/sdk-loader.js:156-160` now describes the buffering as PAST behaviour and names the streaming fix; `enableJsonResponse` survives in `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts` only as the comment explaining its absence, pinned by `src/shared/api/sse-keep-alive.test.ts:159` (`expect(routeCode).not.toMatch(/enableJsonResponse/)`).
- Status: open (residuals 1-2)

### F-093: Q7/Q9 landed with no finding entries, and the §2 file-size cap was never enforced on `src/**`
- Location: `docs/REFACTOR-FINDINGS.md` (this file), `eslint.config.mjs`
- Found during: production-hardening batch 1 adversarial review — items H2 / L1
- Severity: smell (process); the lint half is real drift
- Description: two separate gaps in the same round. (a) The Q7 (thread-linkage self-verification) and Q9 (deadline chain) rounds changed the MCP surface and the await budget and recorded NOTHING here, so the only account of why those numbers are what they are lived in code comments — exactly the state this file exists to prevent. (b) ENGINEERING.md §2 states a 500-line hard cap, and only `dopl-desktop-app/eslint.config.js` ever enforced it. The root config had no `max-lines` rule at all, so `src/**` and `packages/*/src/**` grew past it silently — `src/shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts` reached EXACTLY 500 during this round with nothing to say so.
- Resolution (a): F-092 above covers Q9's transport finding; Q7's residual is already tracked under F-083 (legacy `task-<uuid>-<seq>` ids stay caller-settable with no participation check) and the tool-side comments were corrected in this round to stop claiming `metadata.taskId` is "the only honest source" — `taskTitle` is the server-stamped half, and a fabricated tag renders titleless, which is the tell.
- Resolution (b): the rule is wired into `eslint.config.mjs:34-39` at the same severity and options as the desktop config (`max-lines` 500, `skipBlankLines:false`, `skipComments:false`), with the pre-existing offenders listed explicitly at `:47-69` rather than hidden behind a blanket ignore. **New code is capped; the backlog stays visible.** DO NOT ADD TO THAT LIST — split the file instead.
- **The backlog, RE-DERIVED 2026-07-31** (measured with `find` + `wc -l` over `src/**` and `packages/*/src/**`, not copied forward — this entry absorbs the old F-041, which had gone stale). Two things the measurement showed:
  - The exemption list is still EXACTLY the set of files over the cap — no unlisted file has crossed 500, so the rule is holding on new code.
  - But FOUR exempted files GREW after the rule landed, which is what an unconditional exemption buys: `src/features/channels/lib/group-thread.test.ts` 837 -> **983**, `packages/mcp-server/src/server.ts` 781 -> **876**, `src/features/channels/lib/group-thread.ts` 561 -> **621**, `src/features/channels/components/channels-view.tsx` 534 -> **540**.
  - Current sizes (**re-measured 2026-07-31 after the multiplayer wave; the numbers in parentheses are what this line said before**): `src/shared/supabase/types.ts` **2793** (was 2290 — GENERATED by the Supabase CLI, not a refactor candidate), `group-thread.test.ts` 983, `packages/mcp-server/src/server.ts` **911** (was 876), `src/features/skills/components/skill-view.tsx` 759, `packages/dopl-client/src/client.ts` **698** (was 695; see F-110 residual (n) — the `ChannelAgentsClient` extraction did not reduce it), `src/features/knowledge/server/seed-fixtures-data.ts` 670, `group-thread.ts` 621, `src/shared/layout/settings-modal/sections/plans-billing.tsx` 566, `src/features/billing/server/webhook-handler.test.ts` 542, `channels-view.tsx` 540, `src/features/ontology/server/repository.ts` 538, `src/features/workspaces/server/invitations.ts` 534, `src/features/teams/server/repository.ts` 509, `src/features/billing/components/upgrade-modal.tsx` 506.
  - `src/shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts` is deliberately NOT exempted and now sits at **498** — it shrank rather than growing, so enforcement held on the one file this rule was written for.
- Proposed resolution: refactor the list down one file at a time, outside a hardening round. `types.ts` should get a permanent exemption comment (generated); the rest are ordinary splits — `skill-view.tsx` (extract the editor/save-chain hook + header controls) and `invitations.ts` (extract the accept/join sub-flows) are the two that already have a split scoped, inherited from the pruned F-041. Consider making the exemption a size CEILING rather than an off switch, so an exempt file cannot grow further.
- Status: open (rule landed; 14-file backlog outstanding, 4 of them growing)

### F-094: The middleware ran a NETWORK auth call on every request, and two smaller poll/publication residuals (Q12)
- Location: `src/proxy.ts` (+ new `src/proxy.test.ts`, `src/proxy-claims.test.ts`); `dopl-desktop-app/main/consent-watcher.js` → new `main/consent-cadence.js` (+ `test/consent-cadence.test.mjs`); the `supabase_realtime` publication
- Found during: Q12 request-volume diet (production hardening batch 2, B4), triggered by the 2026-07-31 self-DDoS incident — auth traffic went 70/min → 1,500/min for 30 minutes, starved GoTrue until the Google OAuth code-exchange failed DNS and every server-rendered page threw
- Severity: was HIGH (the app could amplify a normal traffic bump into its own outage); now closed for the middleware, two residuals below

**The amplifier, and why it was the whole story.** `proxy.ts` called `supabase.auth.getUser()` on EVERY matched request. That is a network round-trip to GoTrue, which costs ~5 Postgres queries, on a matcher that covers every page and every API route — ~200k requests/day for 8 users. `pg_stat_statements` on prod confirms the shape: the GoTrue user lookup (`SELECT users.aud, users.banned_until, …`) has **2,555,472 calls**, and the per-request session setup (`set_config('search_path', …)`) **6,855,730**. The Postgres time is small (3.6 min total) — the failure was never CPU, it was GoTrue's own request/connection capacity, which is exactly what the incident exhausted.

**The fix, and the finding that matters most.** `getUser()` → `getClaims()`, which verifies the access token LOCALLY. **This is only safe because of what this project's tokens actually are, and that was verified against prod, not assumed:** `GET https://<ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json` returns exactly one key, `{"alg":"ES256","kty":"EC","crv":"P-256","kid":…}`, and the anon key is the new `sb_publishable_…` format — there is no legacy HS256 shared secret left. Asymmetric + `kid` + WebCrypto present is precisely the branch where `auth-js` fetches the JWK once (cached process-wide in `GLOBAL_JWKS`, `JWKS_TTL` = 10 min) and then runs `crypto.subtle.verify`. Signatures ARE still checked. `src/proxy-claims.test.ts` proves it against the REAL client with REAL generated ES256 keys: a valid token verifies with the JWKS fetch only, 25 consecutive verifications cost 1 request total, and a tampered signature / an attacker-key re-sign is REJECTED without falling back to the network. **If this project ever reverts to a symmetric signing key, `getClaims()` silently starts network-calling `getUser()` again** (auth-js's own fallback for `alg: HS*` or a missing `kid`) — the diet quietly disappears but nothing breaks or becomes unsafe. That degradation is pinned by a test too.

**Cookie refresh is preserved and this was the trap to avoid.** `getClaims()` with no argument runs `getSession()` first, and `getSession()` is what does the rotating-cookie refresh — within `EXPIRY_MARGIN_MS` (90s) of expiry it POSTs `token?grant_type=refresh_token`, the new session goes through the `@supabase/ssr` storage adapter, and the `setAll` callback rebuilds `supabaseResponse` with the rotated cookies. So the network call now happens ~once per session per hour instead of once per request, and sessions do not die after ~1h. Pinned by `src/proxy.test.ts` (rotated cookies land on the response; a failed refresh still bounces to `/login`).

**SECOND TRAP, and it is a live landmine for anyone else adopting `getClaims()` in this repo:** `getClaims()` only converts `AuthError`s into `{ data: null, error }`. `auth-js`'s `validateExp` (`lib/helpers.ts`) throws a **plain `Error`** — `"JWT has expired"` / `"Missing exp claim"` — and `getClaims()` **re-throws it at the caller**. `getUser()` could never do that. Unwrapped in middleware that is a 500 on every server-rendered page, i.e. the exact failure shape of the incident. `proxy.ts` therefore wraps the call in try/catch and treats a throw as "no session" (fail-closed, same branch a failed `getUser()` took). `getSession()`'s 90s pre-expiry refresh normally keeps this unreachable, but it keys off the stored `expires_at`, so a blob whose `expires_at` is absent or disagrees with the token's own `exp` reaches it. **Do not "simplify" that try/catch away.** Pinned in both test files.
- Behaviour preserved exactly: `src/proxy.test.ts` is a 62-case route matrix (authed page / public page / API route / static asset × valid token / near-expiry + refresh / no session / verification throw) covering every `PUBLIC_ROUTES` entry, the `dopl_at_` bearer bypass, the S-8 lowercase 308 and all five of its exclusions, the OG/Twitter crawler passthrough, and the `config.matcher` static-asset exclusions asserted against the real exported value. `proxy.ts` is load-bearing (OAuth + MCP + the desktop sign-in bridge route through it) so the change is 1 call site + 2 renamed reads; nothing else moved.
- Expected effect: the GoTrue round-trip disappears from the hot path entirely. Steady-state cost per page view goes from 1 network call + ~5 Postgres queries to 0, with one JWKS GET per process per 10 minutes (edge-cached, no Postgres) and one refresh POST per session per hour.

**~~RESIDUAL 1 — `withUserAuth` still does a network `getUser()` per API request.~~ RESOLVED** (Q11, verified at HEAD 2026-07-31): `src/shared/auth/with-auth.ts:398` calls `supabase.auth.getClaims()`, and `:354-376` carries both traps in-file (the `validateExp` plain-`Error` re-throw, and `getSession()`'s rotating-cookie refresh). The middleware and the route wrappers now read the same way.

**Successor observation, NOT a residual of this entry and deliberately not filed:** `proxy.ts:64-65` records that every SERVER COMPONENT still asks GoTrue over the network via `shared/supabase/server.ts` -> `getUser()`, and that the two paths can DISAGREE. Worth a look in a round that owns that file.

**RESIDUAL 2 — the consent watcher was a CPU/wakeup problem, not a request problem; the brief's premise was half wrong.** `consent-watcher.js` ran `setInterval(tick, 2000)`, but `tick()` iterates `records` — with no pending consent request (the overwhelmingly common state) it makes **zero** network calls. It was 1,800 wakeups/hour of `auth.isSignedIn()`, which is a synchronous `safeStorage` decrypt + JWT decode, and whose cookie probe is already single-flighted behind a 30s cooldown (F-088). Real request volume was bounded by the per-record ladder, not by `TICK_MS`. Fixed anyway, because a flat timer that ignores whether there is anything to do is the wrong shape: the scan is now self-scheduling and adaptive (`main/consent-cadence.js`, extracted at the §2 500-line cap and pure enough to be `require`d straight into a `node --test` file — no source slicing). 3s for 60s after a wake / registration / decision / `poke()`, otherwise it sleeps until the soonest record is actually due, clamped to [3s, 30s]; with no records it sits at 30s. A global ceiling of 60 polls/trailing-minute enforces the F-072 "no unbounded poll loops" rule — a capped poll is skipped, never settled or dropped, so throttling can never lose a request. The single-flight `inFlight` lock and the settled-set replay guard are untouched. Clock is injectable (`start({ clock })`) and the scheduler is driven by a fake clock in `test/consent-cadence.test.mjs`.
- Arithmetic, before → after (pinned as executable assertions, not prose): idle (no pending request) **1,800 wakeups/hour → 120, and 0 requests/hour either way**. One record parked a full hour with a decision made on another surface: **141 → 116 requests** (−18%), and the first minute got FASTER (20 polls vs 12) because that is when a decision actually lands. The per-record ladder outside the active window moved 10s/20s/60s → 15s/30s/60s. Nothing waits on the ladder in normal use: every in-app decision path already calls `poke()`.

**RESIDUAL 3 — exactly ONE of the 23 published tables has no subscriber, and the publication is NOT where the CPU went.** Grepped every subscriber in the tree — the 8 `useWorkspaceTablesRealtime` call sites (`CHANNEL_TABLES`, `CONSENT_TABLES`, `PRESENCE_TABLES`, `KNOWLEDGE_TABLES`, `ONTOLOGY_TABLES`, `WORKFLOW_TABLES`, `SKILL_TABLES`, `CHAT_TABLES`) and the desktop's single `postgres_changes` subscription on `channel_messages` (`main/realtime.js:298`). Union = 22 tables. The publication holds 23. The orphan is **`public.clusters`**: the table is alive and server code reads/writes it (`features/clusters/server/service.ts`), but nothing subscribes to it — it was published by `20260419000000_realtime_canvas_brain.sql` for the legacy canvas, which is gone.
- **The honest sizing: pruning it will barely move the needle.** `pg_stat_statements` shows where the ~7.2h actually went — `realtime.list_changes` (`SELECT wal->>… `, the apply_rls decode) at **2,968,450 calls / 386.6 min (6.44h)** and the subscription-lookup query (`with sub_tables as (select rr.entity from pg_publication_…)`) at **1,830,653 calls / 56.3 min (0.94h)**. That is the POLLER, which runs whenever any subscription is open, times the WAL records it has to decode and RLS-evaluate. `clusters` holds 7 rows and is near-zero churn, so dropping it removes decode work that was already near zero. Do it as hygiene, not as a fix.
- Two things that WOULD move the needle, both noted and neither actioned here: (a) `channel_consent_requests` is **`REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`** — every UPDATE ships the entire OLD row through wal2json in addition to the new one. It is probably deliberate (Realtime needs the old row to evaluate a subscription `filter` on UPDATE/DELETE), so this is an observation to check, NOT a change to make blind. (b) F-091's column-list narrowing on `channel_messages`, still unapplied.
- Proposed resolution: needs-user-decision — prod DDL, same protocol as F-091. Written here, NOT applied. There is direct precedent for both the change and its wording: `supabase/migrations/20260728010000_drop_channel_tasks_from_realtime.sql` did exactly this for `channel_tasks` after the 2026-07-27 CPU incident. Land it as a migration rather than a console statement so the publication stays reproducible.

```sql
-- supabase/migrations/<ts>_drop_clusters_from_realtime.sql
-- Q12 request-volume diet: `clusters` was added to the realtime publication by
-- 20260419000000_realtime_canvas_brain.sql for the legacy canvas, which has since
-- been deleted. Server code still reads and writes the table, but NOTHING
-- subscribes to it — the 8 useWorkspaceTablesRealtime call sites and the desktop
-- listener cover 22 other tables and not this one. Publishing it is pure logical
-- decoding + per-subscription RLS cost. Re-add deliberately if a subscriber ships.
ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime DROP TABLE public.clusters;

-- Rollback:
-- ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE public.clusters;
```

- Verify after applying:
```sql
-- 1. 22 rows, and `clusters` is absent.
SELECT tablename FROM pg_publication_tables
WHERE pubname = 'supabase_realtime' ORDER BY 1;

-- 2. Live subscriptions are unaffected (nothing ever subscribed to `clusters`,
--    so this set must be identical before and after).
SELECT entity::text, count(*) FROM realtime.subscription GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 2 DESC;
```
Then open the Canvas / clusters UI and confirm it still loads and saves — it refetches through the service layer and never relied on a realtime event, so nothing should change. If anything in the cluster UI stops updating live, roll back with the one-liner above; no app deploy is involved either way.
- Status: open — **Residual 3 only** (the `clusters` publication SQL above, written and NOT applied; owner decision, prod DDL). Middleware CLOSED, Residual 1 CLOSED (`with-auth.ts:398`), Residual 2 CLOSED. The `getClaims()` narrative above is kept deliberately even though the work landed: it is the reason the swap is SAFE (asymmetric ES256 + `kid`, verified against the prod JWKS) and it names the two traps — both are conditions a future change could silently break.

### F-097

**`POST` and `DELETE` on `/api/auth/mcp-device-token` disagree about an invalid `label`.** `BodySchema`'s `readLabel` silently falls back to the default `"Dopl Desktop CLI"`, while `RevokeSchema` **400s** on the same input. So a label the mint quietly rewrote — e.g. a hostname pushing it past 120 chars — can never be revoked by the client that sent it. Found while fixing the revoke verdict (which now parses the server's `revoked` count and reports `no-match` instead of claiming a 0-row revoke succeeded). Reconcile the two schemas: either both coerce or both reject.
- Status: open. **Server-side — needs a push.**

### F-098

**The web consent card cannot name the tool profile that actually bounds the session.** Under a `read_only` or `dopl_only` profile the SDK's `disallowedTools` (plus the credential-path deny rules) fence the session at the tool-binding layer, where no permission axis can reach — so the bypass option's old copy, "Auto approving every command on this machine", was simply false. The COPY half is fixed and verified 2026-07-31: `src/features/channels/components/permission-preset-row.tsx:52` reads "Auto approving every command the tool profile allows", carried verbatim into `renderer/session/session-labels.js` and pinned in both suites.

**Correction to this entry (2026-07-31):** it claimed no `toolProfile` reaches `src/features/channels/` "at all". That is wrong — the channel MEMBERSHIP preference is plumbed (`types.ts` `AgentToolProfile` / `myAgentToolProfile`, `server/dto.ts`, `server/service-reads.ts`, `constants.ts` `AGENT_TOOL_PROFILE_LABELS`, rendered by `components/channel-settings-popover.tsx`). What has none of it is the CONSENT REQUEST: `ChannelConsentRequest` carries no profile field and `components/consent-card.tsx` references none, so the card can refer to "the tool profile" but cannot say WHICH one bounds this session. The desktop status strip already names it via `permissionPostureText(toolMode, messageMode, profileLabel)`.
- Status: open — plumb the profile onto the consent-request DTO so the card states the real blast radius instead of gesturing at it. **Needs a server push.**

### F-099

**The Q1 narration sweep on the channel WRITE ops. Code CLOSED 2026-07-31**, verified at HEAD (this supersedes F-095, now pruned): `opCloseThread`'s thread title, `ch.name` at 14 sites, `profiles.display_name` at ~10 sites (neutralized at the SOURCE in `memberLabel`, so a call site added later cannot reintroduce it), and `metadata.taskId` all route through `neutralizeInline` / `inlineOr`, with `UNTRUSTED_THREAD_HEADER` on the close confirmation. Charset rules added for `channels.name` / `channels.topic` in `src/features/channels/schema.ts`; `channel-ops-write.ts` split at the §2 cap.

**The one fact from that round to carry forward, because it is still true and is what makes the narration layer load-bearing:** a non-UUID `metadata.taskId` is stored **VERBATIM** — `resolvePostMetadata` runs its lookup + participation gate only inside `if (isUuid(callerTaskId))` (`service-writes-metadata.ts:236-245`), and the route's `metadata` is a bare `z.record(z.string(), z.unknown())` with no length, charset or newline rule on any value. Nothing on the way IN bounds it; only the renderer does.

**DELIBERATELY LEFT, with reasons** (do not "fix" these without re-reading them):
- **Caller-argument echoes stay raw**: `opOpen`'s 409 `"${name}"`, `close_thread`'s `${summary}` (2000 chars of the agent's own prose — neutralizing would clip and de-punctuate an operator-facing outcome for no threat), `threadLinkageNote`'s `${askedThread}`, and `${ref}` throughout the read ops. None round-trips through storage where a peer can reach it. `threadId` is the EXCEPTION and IS neutralized — it round-trips through the legend, which renders peer-set `taskId`.
- **`set_thread_mode` gets a span but NO header**: the route allows it to the creator only, so a success means the caller typed the title. The span stays because a tool must not depend on a remote authz check for a local rendering property; the header would be framing a string against its own author.
- **Message BODIES stay as they are** — indented, under `UNTRUSTED_BODY_HEADER`. That is the disclaimed zone by design, not an oversight.
- **No header on channel-NAME-only echoes.** A name is now a bounded span inside an unambiguous sentence; a header on every write confirmation and every error line would train agents to skip headers. `op="list"`, where names are discovered in bulk, does carry one.
- ~~**The other MCP tools were NOT swept.**~~ **CLOSED by F-101** (same night) — and the guess in this bullet, "lower reach, the caller's OWN workspace objects", was wrong on two counts. See F-101.

**MIGRATION WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED — the only open item in this entry:** `supabase/migrations/20260731100000_channels_name_topic_bounds.sql` (CHECKs on `channels.name` / `channels.topic`). **Read its header before applying — unlike the display_name migration it does NOT close a reachable hole.** `20260725130000` revoked INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `public.channels` from `authenticated`+`anon` and dropped every write policy; VERIFIED against prod 2026-07-31 (`information_schema.table_privileges` returns SELECT/REFERENCES/TRIGGER only), so the service layer is the sole writer and both its zod schemas are now bounded. The CHECK is there for the NEXT writer (a route added without the schema, a repair path calling `supabaseAdmin()` directly — the DM self-heal does). Pre-flight is in the file; run 2026-07-31 returned **3 rows, 0 violations** (longest name 16, longest topic 38), so it adds clean today.

- Status: open — **migration only** (written, NOT applied; owner decision). Code closed and verified at HEAD; the cross-tool sweep closed as F-101.

### F-100

**The N-party wave shipped copy that asserted false things, and the falsehoods were all the same shape: a note telling an agent or an operator what happened.** Fixed 2026-07-31 (uncommitted). A confidently wrong note is worse than silence — each of these produced a specific wrong action.

All four fixes are CLOSED and verified at HEAD. Two standing rules came out of them and are worth carrying forward, because both had been restated wrongly in three files:

- **The implicit-trigger rule keys on MEMBER COUNT, not `is_direct`** — `targeting.js` fires on `memberCount === 2` + membership and never reads `is_direct`. It now has ONE home, `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-addressing.ts`, which all three MCP files read, each fact cited to the file that owns it. What makes the caller's OWN post safe to call unheard is the LOOP BRAKE plus every MCP post being agent-authored, NOT the channel's size.
- **A threaded post must WAIT, not re-post.** A first-class (uuid) thread tag feeds the counterparty's live session or reopens their settled one, so telling an agent "nobody was woken, re-post with `to=`" manufactures a DUPLICATE request — a second consent prompt and a second run, the 1.7.14 incident shape. A LEGACY (`task-<channel>-<seq>`) tag routes to no session and keeps the plain note. (F-105 is the same failure reached from the other side: inheritance silently declining to tag at all.)

The other two (H2a's `await` "not for you" wording; H2b's `direct` flag so the outbound card names the DM recipient) are wording/plumbing, pinned by `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts` plus 6 desktop assertions.

**REPORT-ONLY, an owner call and deliberately untouched:** `op="members"` lets an agent walk any PUBLIC channel it was never invited to and dump member display names and **emails** (`formatMemberLine` falls back to `m.email`; `listChannels` ORs `visibility.eq.public`). This is route/web parity rather than a new trust boundary, but it is newly enumerable BY AN AGENT in one call. See the round's report for the exact reach.
- Status: open — **the `members` reach only**, an owner/product call rather than a bug. Code closed and verified at HEAD.

### F-101

**The narration rule was written as a `dopl_channel` rule; it is a `dopl_*` rule, and the widest-reach site was in no tool file at all.** F-099 closed the channel work and deferred "the other MCP tools" on the guess that their reach was lower because they render "the caller's OWN workspace objects". Both halves of that guess were wrong. Closed 2026-07-31 (uncommitted). What shipped:

- **ONE definition, MOVED not copied.** `neutralizeInline` / `inlineOr` / `INLINE_TEXT_MAX` live in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/narration.ts`; `channel-shared.ts` **re-exports** them, so there is exactly one implementation. Guarded two ways: function IDENTITY (`expect(channelNeutralize).toBe(neutralizeInline)` — a copy passes a behavioural test and fails this) and a source scan asserting `narration.ts` is the only file in `src/tools/` declaring `function neutralizeInline`. **Keep both guards**: they are what stops a later round from re-forking the helper.
- **`workspaces.name` / `.description` — the highest-reach untrusted string in the product, and the main reason to keep this entry.** Set by whoever OWNS a workspace, and a workspace enters your directory the moment you accept an invitation or a join link — so the author is not "another member of your workspace", it is "the owner of a workspace you joined", who need share no other context with you at all. It reaches the two most trusted surfaces in the protocol: the MCP **`instructions`** block, and the **`_dopl_status` footer on EVERY successful tool response** (where `active_workspace: "${name}"` sat inside double quotes, so one newline bought a second, invented `_dopl_status` key). Six sites in `server.ts` neutralized; the footer carries the immutable `id=` beside the renameable slug; `UNTRUSTED_DIRECTORY_NOTE` frames the table.
- **`dopl_chats` is cross-user BY DESIGN** — `visibility: "public"` shares a chat workspace-wide and `op="list"` returns other members' alongside your own. That is the assumption F-099 got wrong. Labels are values now; bodies stay intact. `UNTRUSTED_ARCHIVE_HEADER` fires only when a rendered chat is workspace-shared, because a header on every result trains agents to skip headers.
- **`dopl_members`, kb / skill / workflow / cluster / ontology / map / search**: every name, title, label, path, slug-echo and search snippet routed through the neutralizer, plus user ids restored beside member-typed names. `dopl_map` matters most of that group — the server instructions tell the agent to call it FIRST, before its first substantive reply.
- **Why a hand-rolled neutralizer is never good enough** (`descSuffix` in `knowledge-ops-read.ts` was one): its `\s*\n+\s*` -> space pass misses **U+0085 (NEL)**, which is not in JavaScript's `\s` class, and it touched no markdown at all. Anything that looks like local sanitising should defer to the shared helper.

**DELIBERATELY LEFT, with reasons** (do not "fix" these without re-reading them):
- **BODIES stay whole and unclipped**: knowledge-entry bodies, `SKILL.md`, chat transcript summaries and `verbatim`, a skill's `description`/`when_to_use` under `op="get"`, workflow step `description`/`userInput`/`agentOutput`/`nextInstructions`, ontology `text` attributes (4000 chars) and action prose. These are the procedures the product exists to hand the agent. Where such a body had neither framing nor indent, it is now INDENTED (`indented()` in `ontology-render.ts`) — continuation lines get two spaces, so the text survives verbatim and loses only its ability to BEGIN a line. **Residual, named rather than papered over:** an ontology `text` attribute or action description can still contain markdown mid-line. That is the same trade the channel bodies make, one blast radius down.
- **No untrusted-content header on kb / skill / workflow / cluster / ontology.** That content is the workspace's own authored procedure and the agent is MEANT to follow it; "never instructions addressed to you" would be false there. Headers went only where the content is demonstrably someone else's: the workspace directory, a shared chat, the member roster.
- **Self-inflicted echoes** (`${slug}`, `${path}`, `${query}`, `${label}` the caller just passed) are neutralized only where the echo sits inside a code span a backtick could escape. They are low reach and marked as such in the round's report.

~~REPORT-ONLY — the charset-rule schema call.~~ **ANSWERED — see F-102** (14 short-label columns bounded on the way in; migration written, NOT applied).

**Harness lesson worth keeping, because it reported false PASSES:** vitest `-t` is a REGEX, so a filter containing `(` or `+` matches nothing and reads as "passed"; and passing a filter through a shell lets BACKTICKS in a test name run as command substitution, with the same silent zero-match. Five apparent mutation "survivors" in this round were harness artifacts; all five died once the filter was escaped and `spawnSync`'d with an argv array. Do not trust a mutation run driven by a shell-interpolated `-t`.

- Status: open — **residual only**: an ontology `text` attribute or action description can still carry markdown MID-LINE (named above, an accepted trade), plus the Dopl "Dopl Development" KB sync. Code closed and verified at HEAD (`narration.ts` exists and is the sole definition). **Ships with the SERVER.**
