### F-102

**F-101's closing report-only call, answered: the short-label columns are charset-bounded on the way IN, and the DB half turned out to be load-bearing rather than defence in depth.** F-101 left the question open with "`workspaces.name` is the one worth doing first, on reach alone". It is done, along with the rest of the short-label class. Closed 2026-07-31 (uncommitted, **no DDL applied**).

- **ONE definition.** `SAFE_LABEL_RE` + `safeLabelMessage` + `safeLabel(subject, max)` + `safeOptionalLabel(subject, max)` live in `src/shared/lib/safe-label.ts`; `features/channels/schema.ts` (`CHANNEL_TEXT_RE`) and `app/api/user/profile/route.ts` (`DISPLAY_NAME_RE`) import it. Three copies of a charset rule drift, and the copy that drifts is the one that stops rejecting.
- **14 columns bounded in zod**: `workspaces.name`+`.description`, `clusters.name`, `knowledge_bases.name`, `skills.name`+`.folder`, `workflows.name`, `workflow_steps.title`, `teams.name`, `chats.title`+`.project`, `chat_folders.name`, `ontology_clusters.name`, `ontology_objects.name`. (`app/api/clusters/[slug]` PATCH had NO schema at all — hand-rolled `typeof body.name === "string"` — and parses `ClusterNameSchema` now.)
- **THE FACT THAT MAKES THE UNAPPLIED MIGRATION MATTER.** F-101 framed the DB half as a cheap second layer. For eight of the twelve tables it is the FIRST layer: `authenticated` holds INSERT/UPDATE on all of them **and** each carries a permissive `public` write policy — `clusters_editor_update`, `knowledge_bases_editor_update`, `skills_editor_update`, `workflows_editor_update`, `workflow_steps_editor_update`, `ontology_clusters_editor_update`, `ontology_objects_editor_update`, `teams_admin_write`. Any workspace **editor** can PATCH those names straight through PostgREST with the anon key and never touch a route, so the zod bound is unreachable for them. Only `workspaces`, `chats` and `chat_folders` are service-role-only (the `channels` case). **Until the migration is applied, eight tables are bounded only where the writer chooses to go through a route.**

**DELIBERATELY LEFT, with reasons:**
- **Every `description` / `overview` / `purpose` / `subtitle` / body / transcript / `SKILL.md` / `whenToUse` / `learnings`.** Prose, legitimately multi-line, rendered as bodies under framing that says what they are. `workspaces.description` is the ONE exception and is argued at its constraint — it renders as a one-line summary in the MCP directory table, so it was already being read as a label.
- **The ontology labels nested in JSONB** — `attributes[].label`, `template[].label`, `relationships[].label`, `methods[].name`. They render into narration, but they are not columns: a CHECK means walking a jsonb array on every write, and `ontology_objects` is editor-writable, so a zod-only bound would be exactly the fence-beside-an-open-gate this work closes. **OPEN.**
- **`workspaces.description` is edited in a `<textarea rows={3}>`** in both `workspace-settings-form.tsx` and `create-workspace-dialog.tsx`, so Enter is now a validation error in a control that invites it. **OPEN, one-line follow-up:** make those two single-line inputs so the affordance matches the rule.

**MIGRATION `20260731110000_short_label_charset_bounds.sql` — WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED.** 14 CHECK constraints named `*_charset_check` (`chats_title_check`, `chat_folders_name_check`, `teams_name_check`, `skills_folder_len` already exist as length-only and are left alone). Pre-flight run against prod 2026-07-31: **all 14 columns, 0 violations of any kind** — 0 control, 0 zero-width/bidi/separator, 0 untrimmed, 0 over-length. Verified three independent ways, the last being a `bool_and(<predicate>)` dry-run per constraint, which is exactly what `ADD CONSTRAINT` validates. **Trap worth knowing:** copying the `[\u200B-\u200F\u2028-\u202F\u2060-\u206F\uFEFF]` class between tools as LITERAL characters can collapse U+2028..U+202F into an ordinary ASCII space, turning the class into "matches any name with a space in it" and reporting a whole table as violating. That happened during this work and produced a false alarm on 7 of 9 workspaces. The file stores the `\uXXXX` escape form and carries a five-assertion sanity check to run before trusting any count.

Tests: **+331** in `src/shared/lib/safe-label.test.ts`, table-driven (17 bounded fields × 11 rejected classes + 7 legitimate names). **Mutation-verified in BOTH directions**, and the second direction is the point: narrowing the rule to ASCII fails 126/127 acceptance cases, because a rule that rejects `Müller's Team` or `研究` would be worse than no rule. Keep that half of the suite.

- Status: open — **three items**: (1) migration `20260731110000` written and NOT APPLIED (owner decision, prod DDL); (2) the jsonb-nested ontology labels; (3) the `workspaces.description` textarea affordance. Code closed and verified at HEAD (`src/shared/lib/safe-label.ts`; `ClusterNameSchema` on the cluster PATCH). Dopl "Dopl Development" KB sync still pending.

### F-104: An agent could not establish WHO or WHERE it was, and three surfaces answered from three unreconciled sources
- Location: `packages/mcp-server/src/server.ts` (the `_dopl_status` footer, `current_workspace`, `buildInstructions`); `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/members.ts:opWhoami`; `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/ontology-ops-read.ts:opAnchor`; `packages/mcp-server/src/factory.ts` (boot); `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`; `src/shared/auth/with-mcp-transport-auth.ts`; `src/shared/auth/mcp-oauth.ts:validateAccessToken`
- Found during: live incidents, 2026-07-31 — two agents in one channel spent a round disagreeing about which of them was which; a second could not tell whether its counterparty was a different machine or a different account on the same machine
- Severity: bug (identity confusion corrupted real cross-user work, and the surface actively invited it)
- Description:
  - **Three sources for one fact.** The boot status ping's `user_id` reached ONLY `dopl_channel`. `dopl_members(op="whoami")` re-derived the caller from `GET /api/workspaces/me`, whose `userId` is `string | null`; when it came back null the op still printed `## You in <workspace>` and a role and identified nobody, while the channel roster in the SAME connection was confidently marking "you" off the other id. `dopl_ontology(op="anchor")` used neither.
  - **The footer answered half the question.** `_dopl_status` rides every successful response and the instructions tell the agent to read it, and it named only the workspace. An agent that needed its own id had to go looking, and what it found disagreed.
  - **`op="anchor"` was the strongest identity claim with the weakest backing.** It rendered `You are anchored to this object.` over `ontology_objects.name` — member-typed text — with no caller id, no `SECURITY:` framing, and no test, while `buildInstructions` told every agent to call it for "my/me" requests. `op="claim_anchor"` lets any agent on the connection re-point the link it reads from. An agent reporting an object name as its own identity was doing what the surface invited.
  - **Locus was unrepresented.** `validateAccessToken` selected `id, user_id, scopes, access_expires_at, revoked_at` and dropped `client_id` / `client_name`, so the transport could not tell a desktop device token from a connector grant. `readRuntimeHeader` was resolved at `/api/mcp` and forwarded to the loopback but never given to `bootServer`. Both facts were in hand and discarded; `userId` was discarded too and then re-fetched over a loopback by `bootServer`.
  - **A stale finding, corrected.** An earlier note said `memberDisplay` renders name + email and DROPS the user id. That was true until commit `6641e59` (2026-07-31) rewrote it to always append `` (`<userId>`) ``. Any plan resting on it is out of date.
- Resolution (2026-07-31): ONE identity record — `CallerIdentity` in `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/identity.ts` — resolved once at boot from the credential that is authorizing the request (the transport's id wins over the ping's), rendered by one set of functions and nowhere else. The footer leads with `caller: id=… · runtime=…`; `whoami` is the full answer plus `LOCUS_NOTE`; `current_workspace` states the caller on the branch where no footer fires and now prints workspace ids; `op="anchor"` states the caller's id first and frames the object as re-pointable context; `op="list"` marks `· you` the way the channel roster does. `describeCredential` (`src/shared/auth/mcp-credential.ts`) classifies on `client_id`, never on the caller-supplied `client_name`.
- **What it deliberately refuses to claim** (this is the load-bearing half): a credential label names where the credential was MINTED, never where the session RUNS; an absent runtime stamp renders `unstamped`, never `external`, because a desktop spawn on an older build is also unstamped; and a peer's MACHINE is stated as not knowable, with "do not assert it either way", while a peer's ACCOUNT is decidable by user id.
- Privacy fence: a peer stays name + immutable id + membership. No credential, hostname, or runtime is exposed for anyone but the caller, asserted by tests in `members-identity.test.ts` and `ontology-anchor.test.ts`.
- Pinned by `identity.test.ts`, `members-identity.test.ts`, `ontology-anchor.test.ts`, plus additions to `server.test.ts` / `factory.test.ts` / `mcp-oauth-device-token.test.ts`. Mutation-verified: **12 reverts, 25 test failures, 0 vacuous.**
- Residual: `dopl_channel`'s `memberRef` still collapses the caller to the bare literal `"you"` on a message line, dropping the id in that slot (deliberate there, and the roster prints it — but it is the one place the "a name never travels without an id" rule is relaxed). `src/features/mcp-connect/skill-template.ts` was updated with a "Who am I" section but still omits `dopl_channel` / `dopl_search` / `dopl_members` from its tool list, which is a pre-existing drift from the server instructions.
- Status: resolved (residuals noted)

### F-105: Nothing ever closes a thread, and three mechanisms that key on thread status degrade as open threads pile up
- Location: `src/features/channels/server/service-tasks.ts:191` (`closeTask` — the ONLY writer of `status='closed'`) and `:204` (the creator-OR-target gate); `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts:73-88` (`resolveInheritableTask`, the exactly-one-open rule) and `:256` (`metadata.taskMode` stamp); `src/features/channels/server/repository-tasks.ts:71-82` (`listTasksByChannel` — no status filter, no limit); the four copies of the await stop-rule — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-read.ts:131`, `channel-ops-write.ts:428`, `channel-ops-threads.ts:119`, `channel.ts:71`; `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-effects.js:24-26` + `main/session-ipc.js:107`; `supabase/migrations/20260727150000_channel_tasks.sql:12-25` (the lifecycle CHECKs)
- Found during: live observation, 2026-07-31 — ONE DM channel holding SIX open threads, two of them `mode='autonomous'` left over from calendar tests hours earlier
- Severity: bug (the accumulation is silent, and past two open threads in a pair it changes routing behaviour, not just tidiness)
- Description: the lifecycle and the facts first, because two of the consequences below are not obvious from the entry that recorded the root fact.
  - **Lifecycle.** `channel_tasks.status` is `open | closed` only, defaulting to `open`, with `CHECK ((status='closed') = (outcome IS NOT NULL))` and `outcome IN (completed, failed)`. `createTask` inserts `open`. The ONLY transition to `closed` is `closeTask` (`service-tasks.ts:191`), authorized to the thread's **creator OR its target** (`created_by` / `target_user_id`; anyone else gets `TaskForbiddenError`) — reachable as `dopl_channel(op="close_thread")` and `PATCH /api/channels/[channelId]/tasks/[taskId] {op:"close"}`. `reopenTask` (web-only, same authorization) flips it back.
  - **Nothing auto-closes, and there is no TTL. Verified, not assumed:** the table has no expiry column at all (`closed_at` is stamped BY the close, never by a sweep); `vercel.json` carries exactly three crons (`purge-trash`, `oauth-cleanup`, `reconcile-seats`) and none touches `channel_tasks`; there is no `/api/cron/expire-*` route; and on the desktop every terminal path EXCEPT the operator's explicit close IPC (`session-ipc.js:107` -> the reducer's `close_task` branch) deliberately leaves the row open — `session-effects.js:24-26` says so in as many words for operator End and the turn/cost caps, and an idle session PARKS rather than ending. So a thread closes only when a human clicks close in the session window, or an agent chooses to call `close_thread`.
  - **1. The await stop-rule is keyed on a condition that a finished exchange never reaches.** All four copies of the rule teach "STOP and report to your operator when the thread is closed or failed, or when nothing has come from that member for ~30+ minutes". Because a completed exchange stays `open`, the thread half of that disjunction effectively never fires, and what actually ends a wait is the silence timer — i.e. the agent burns a full ~30-minute timeout per finished exchange, re-arming ~3.5-minute holds against a thread that ended long ago. The rule exists to produce bounded listening; unclosed threads make it produce the timeout it was written to avoid.
  - **2. `list_threads` grows without bound and is never ranked.** `listTasksByChannel` selects every row for the channel, newest first, with no status filter and no limit; `listChannelTasks` maps all of them; `opListThreads` renders all of them under an `N threads` header. An agent looking for the thread to continue picks from a list that is mostly dead exchanges, and the only signal separating them is the status word on each line.
  - **3. THE SHARP ONE — DM thread inheritance switches OFF at the second open pair-thread.** `resolveInheritableTask` is deliberately all-or-nothing: it returns a thread only when EXACTLY ONE open thread exists for the `{author, peer}` pair (`candidates.length === 1`), because with 2+ candidates a guess would attach the turn to the wrong card and route it to the wrong session window. Correct on its own terms — but it means that in the observed six-open-thread DM, every untagged addressed message inherits NOTHING, lands with no `taskId`, and therefore reads on the peer's machine as a NEW request rather than a continuation. That is the duplicate-request shape F-100 (H3) was written to prevent, and unlike the other consequences it is not a gradual degradation: it flips off at the second open thread and stays off until the extras are closed.
  - **4. A stale `mode` is inherited by whatever runs in the thread next.** `mode` lives on the thread row; `service-writes-metadata.ts:256` stamps `metadata.taskMode = task.mode` onto every message that resolves into it, and `dopl-desktop-app/main/trigger.js:290` uses that value as the spawned session's mode. A thread left `autonomous` from an earlier test keeps handing `autonomous` to every later session in it. Today the blast radius is small — v2.5 made the inbound gate universal and removed autonomous auto-continuation (`session-reducer.js:305-308`; the queue/hold decision is `autoInbound(s)`, the permission axes, not the thread mode) — so this is a stale LABEL plus a live input to whatever autonomous is next wired to (F-070 item 4 tracks that as feature work). Worth naming because the leftover rows in the live channel were exactly this.
  - **5. Closing is never prompted.** The protocol line tells an agent to close "when the GOAL is done, not per hop" (`channel.ts:63`) and that is the only reinforcement anywhere; nothing asks at the moment an exchange actually ends. The web surfaces a close only through the session window's operator action, and a completed exchange that ended by any other route (End, cap, park, crash, or an agent that simply stopped) leaves the thread open with no affordance in front of anyone.
  - **Relationship to F-070.** F-070 item 1 recorded the root fact ("threads never auto-close, rot `active`") as one bullet in a five-item v1.5 deferral bundle. This entry supersedes it with the measured consequences; item 1 there now points here.
  - **Relationship to F-109 — the CURSOR half of consequence 1 is fixed; the THREAD half is not.** F-109 makes `close_thread` report the seq its lifecycle echo landed on, so a requester that closes and then re-arms has a true cursor instead of a guessed one. That removes the other way this exchange-ended story went wrong (the guess skipped a delivered reply outright) but changes nothing here: `closeTask` is still the only writer of `status='closed'`, so a completed exchange still stays `open` and the stop-rule's "thread is closed or failed" disjunct still effectively never fires. Do NOT read F-109 as resolving any part of this entry — the four mechanisms below remain the open question.
- Proposed resolution: needs-user-decision — four plausible mechanisms, deliberately NOT chosen here because they trade off differently and are not mutually exclusive.
  - **(a) Auto-close on a terminal lifecycle event.** Close when the target posts `task_finished` / `task_failed` for the thread. Cheapest and most precise (the signal already exists and is already thread-tagged), but it hands the outcome to the responder — which is F-070 item 3's open product question — and today's caps deliberately post `task_failed` with `capped:true` while leaving the thread resumable, so this would need to distinguish a real finish from a cap.
  - **(b) A TTL / idle sweep.** A `CRON_SECRET`-gated route on the `purge-trash` pattern that closes threads with no message for N days. It is the only option that closes threads nobody will ever revisit — but it invents an outcome for an exchange nobody adjudicated, and it must respect F-072: a sweep that writes to a realtime-published table on a timer is exactly the shape that caused the CPU incident (`channel_tasks` is NOT in the publication today, which makes this cheaper than it looks — see `20260728010000`).
  - **(c) An operator prompt.** Surface "N open threads" and a close affordance in the web channel pane / on the session card at end-of-session. No invented outcomes and no schema change; relies on a human noticing, so it fixes the tidiness and consequence 3 but not the agent-side stop-rule.
  - **(d) Teach agents to close what they opened.** Make the close a stated obligation at the point the agent finishes, not only in the protocol preamble, and consider having the requester's own stop-rule check whether IT should close. Costs nothing but a description change and ships with the server; least reliable on its own, best as a rider on (a) or (c).
  - Whatever is chosen, consequence 3 argues for also making `resolveInheritableTask`'s all-or-nothing miss VISIBLE (it currently degrades silently) — a post that could have inherited but did not is the one moment the pile-up is provable.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — STILL REAL, BUT CONSEQUENCE 5 IS RESOLVED AND THE ENTRY WAS CARRYING IT AS OPEN.** Option (c)+(d) shipped on 2026-08-04 as **PROPOSE-THEN-CONFIRM**, and nothing in this entry said so: `service-tasks-propose.proposeTaskClose` gives an agent a terminal act that mutates nothing, `channel-description.ts:46` now makes proposing the protocol's own closing instruction ("you never close a thread yourself"), and `/api/cron/stale-threads/route.ts` sweeps 14-day-idle threads and posts **the same** `closeProposed` marker under a colliding `client_msg_id`, so one prompt per thread forever. Option (b) was considered and deliberately declined in that route's own docblock — a cron is further from the human than the agent is, and it would fire on exactly the threads with the least evidence. **What is still true, verified line by line:** `closeTask` (now `service-tasks.ts:337`, not `:191` — this entry's Location line is stale) remains the ONLY writer of `status='closed'`, nothing auto-closes, `repository-tasks.ts:71-82` still lists every thread with no status filter and no limit (consequence 2), `service-writes-metadata.ts:135` still returns a thread only on `candidates.length === 1` (consequence 3, the sharp one), `:440` still stamps `taskMode` and `trigger.js:289` still reads it (consequence 4), and all four copies of the stop-rule still key on "closed or failed" (consequence 1) — but that disjunct now has a mechanism that can actually fire it, which it did not have when this was written.
- Status: open (question) — **narrowed to consequences 1-4;** the "closing is never prompted" half is closed

### F-106: Three ways the await/wake primitive defeated itself, all fixed — the residuals only
- Location: `src/features/channels/schema.ts` (`AwaitQuerySchema`), `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/await/route.ts`, `src/features/channels/server/service-await.ts` (`AwaitHoldOptions`), `service-reads.ts` (`pollChannelMessages` / `hasNewMessages`), `repository-messages.ts` (`listMessages` / `hasMessagesAfter`), `service-tasks.ts:128-144` (the self-target guard), `server/errors.ts` (`TaskSelfTargetError`), `http-mapping.ts`; `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-wake-guidance.ts` (NEW), `channel-ops-read.ts:opAwait`, `channel-ops-write.ts:opPost`, `channel-ops-threads.ts:opCreateThread`, `channel-errors.ts`, `channel.ts`, `server.ts:909`; `packages/dopl-client/src/channel-types.ts` + `channel.ts`
- Found during: live cross-machine channel work, 2026-07-31 (all three observed in prod, none found by a test)
- Severity: bug
- Description: three independent defects that all landed on the same primitive — the requester's own session coming back for the peer's reply. (1) **A caller's own posts popped its own await.** The hold was author-blind, so a `task_progress` milestone or a close echo posted while the await was armed ended it on the caller's own echo — twice, on exactly the multi-step work the hold exists for. (2) **A thread could be addressed to its creator.** `createTask` accepted `toUserId === ctx.userId`; only `created_by` and `target_user_id` may post into a thread, so the one member allowed to answer was the one who asked. It rendered as a live request, the peer's listener logged `verdict ignore`, and it could never be answered. (3) **The tool promised a wake it could not deliver.** `post` / `create_thread` / both `await` branches each ended with "that call can keep running after your turn ends, and its result will wake you" — a CLIENT behaviour the server cannot observe, said unconditionally. Measured false: an external session was told it, armed the await, and the ~215s hold returned in-turn.
- Resolution (2026-07-31, uncommitted): (1) opt-in `excludeAuthor` on the await stack, always-on for the MCP path and NOT sent by the desktop listener (which needs its own rows); the existence probe carries the same filter as the row read, or a hit the page drops spins the hold. (2) `TaskSelfTargetError` → 400 `CHANNEL_TASK_SELF_TARGET`, placed after the addressee-membership check and **before** the `client_msg_id` short-circuit so a retry cannot be handed the stored dead thread as a success. (3) `channel-wake-guidance.ts` is the one module deciding what may be claimed, off `CallerIdentity.runtime` (F-104's record, now passed whole to `registerChannelTool`): stamped → don't arm at all; unstamped → the honest hold-fact plus the wake as a client conditional. All stated in ENGINEERING.md §8 (the await sub-bullets and the v3.1 thread list). Tests: `channel-wake-runtime.test.ts` (11), `channel-await-author.test.ts`, `service-await.test.ts`, `schema.test.ts`, `channel-addressing-rule.test.ts`, and `service-tasks-idempotency.test.ts` (split out of the over-cap `service-tasks.test.ts`).
- Residuals, all deliberate and none scheduled: **(a)** `.neq("author_user_id", x)` also drops NULL-author rows (SQL `<> NULL`). No writer produces one today and the exclusion is only ever the await's, but `channel_messages.author_user_id` IS nullable — if system-authored messages are ever added, this filter silently swallows them. **(b)** The exclusion also drops a SIBLING session on the same account from an MCP await; intended (that traffic is "own" from the channel's point of view), but it is the one case where "own" and "mine" differ. **(c)** Headless desktop spawns carry the `desktop-session` stamp too (`main/mcp-config.js` `spawnConfigBody`), so they get the session-window branch's "replies are fed as new turns" wording, which describes the window path more exactly than a headless `claude -p`. Don't-await is correct for both, so the text is not split — revisit only if the headless path stops being fed. **(d)** `post to=self` remains unguarded server-side (the receiving desktop classifies it as noise); the MCP `self_target` arm in `opPost` is unreachable by construction.
- **Follow-on (F-109, same day).** Defect (3) established what the tool may not CLAIM and stopped there, which left an external session with the honest answer "you may simply not be woken". F-109 gives that caller an actual wake — a background-shell poll (`scripts/dopl-channel-wait.sh`) whose TASK COMPLETION is a wake the harness already delivers — appended to `HOLD_FACT` as one conditional sentence so it rides the same single-module discipline. It extends this resolution; it does not alter it, and residuals (a)–(d) below are untouched. Connectors still have no wake (F-109 residual (d)).
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done) — residuals (a)–(d) are recorded, not queued
- Status: resolved (residuals noted); Dopl "Dopl Development" KB sync pending

### F-107: The desktop auth-cookie host check strips a leading dot, and is safe only because APP_HOST is a subdomain
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/auth-cookies.js:64` (`isOurAuthCookie`), `:44-50` (`APP_HOST` + the comment that states the reasoning), `dopl-desktop-app/main/config.js:4` (`APP_URL` / `DOPL_APP_URL`)
- Found during: session-end doc pass, 2026-07-31 (reading FIX S3's jar-pinning fence)
- Severity: smell (config-coupled latent hazard; not exploitable at today's configuration)
- Description: the predicate is `String(c.domain || '').replace(/^\./, '') === APP_HOST`. Chromium reports a host-only cookie's domain as the bare host and a DOMAIN cookie's as `.suffix`, so stripping one leading dot and requiring an exact match rejects `.usedopl.com` (normalizes to `usedopl.com`) against today's `APP_HOST` of `www.usedopl.com`. That is the whole of FIX S3's fence: the name half alone let a sibling subdomain's domain cookie into both readers, and the host half is what pins the jar to our own origin. **The safety is a property of the CONFIG, not of the check.** `APP_HOST` is derived from `DOPL_APP_URL`, an env var. Point it at the apex and `.usedopl.com` — settable by ANY subdomain — strips to an exact match and passes, so the fence widens from one host to every subdomain of the apex, silently, with no test failing and no code changed.
- Proposed resolution: defer — make the check independent of which host it is configured for: reject any cookie whose RAW domain starts with `.` when `APP_HOST` has no subdomain (an apex), or compare host-only and domain cookies on separate branches rather than normalizing them into one string. Either way the invariant ("only a host-only cookie for our exact origin is ours") should be asserted by a test that feeds a `.apex` domain cookie against an apex-configured `APP_HOST`.
- Status: open

### F-108: Desktop tests that grep source text pin today's SHAPE, not behaviour
- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/test/` — 79 of the 83 files that `readFileSync` a module read a `.js` SOURCE file, and 49 assert over that text with a regex (`assert.match` / `.test(SRC)` / `exec(readFileSync(...))`). Highest-value examples: `sdk-mcp-token.test.mjs` (a HIGH security fix — the device token leaving the SDK path's disk surface — pinned by regexes over `sdk-loader.js` / `mcp-config.js` / `session-engine.js` source), `session-addressee-truth.test.mjs` (e.g. `assert.match(readFileSync(M("session-io.js")), /direct: s\.direct === true/)`, an exact source expression), `main-audit-window-budget.test.mjs` + `main-audit-resume-budget.test.mjs` (window/resume budgets read out of `session-park.js` / `session-engine.js` text)
- Found during: session-end doc pass, 2026-07-31
- Severity: smell (debt marker — nothing is currently broken)
- Description: a regex over source proves a string is present, not that the behaviour it implies happens, so a subtly broken rewrite that keeps the phrasing survives the suite. This class has already produced one worked example: `session-preset-start.test.mjs` says in its own header that its previous version regex-matched the reducer's source for `toolMode: 'manual', messageMode: 'ask'` and was therefore worthless, and it was converted to drive the reducer. The CSS/HTML half was separately hardened by `test/helpers/source-probe.mjs` (audit R3) after slice-based assertions were found to pass VACUOUSLY — that fixed the vacuity, not the shape-vs-behaviour gap. The remaining files are mostly main-process modules that CAN be driven directly (the desktop suite already does it elsewhere), so the barrier is effort, not architecture.
- Proposed resolution: defer — convert opportunistically, highest-value first (`sdk-mcp-token.test.mjs`, being the one guarding a security fix), when each module is next changed for another reason. Do NOT schedule a sweep: a mass rewrite of passing tests buys nothing and risks losing the assertions that are load-bearing.
- Status: open

### F-109: Six ways a two-agent exchange lost information it already had — all fixed, the residuals only
- Location: `src/features/channels/schema.ts:242` (`MessageReadQuerySchema.thread`), `server/repository-messages.ts:34,67` (`listMessages` `threadId` + the collapsed cursor builder), `server/service-reads.ts:213,225` (`readMessages` + the watermark guard), `server/service-tasks.ts:219,230` (`TaskCloseResult` / `closeTask`), `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/messages/route.ts`, `.../tasks/[taskId]/route.ts`; `packages/dopl-client/src/channel-types.ts:198,236` + `channel.ts` + `client.ts` + `index.ts`; `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-await.ts` (NEW, the §2 split), `channel-ops-read.ts:90` (`opRead`), `channel-ops-threads.ts:213` (the echo line), `channel-wake-guidance.ts:71,83`, `channel.ts:68-71,78,254`; `scripts/dopl-channel-wait.sh` (NEW); `dopl-desktop-app/main/queued-notice.js` (NEW), `main/channel-post.js:43`, `main/trigger.js:342,372`
- Found during: a live two-agent cross-machine stress test, 2026-07-31 — one exchange, six distinct information losses, none of which a test would have found
- Severity: bug
- Description: six defects with one shape. In each case the information the agent needed EXISTED — on the server, in the transcript, or in the deferring process — and the surface did not hand it over, so the agent guessed, re-derived, or waited on something that had already happened.
  1. **Isolating one exchange meant paging the whole channel.** The transcript is thread-tagged (`metadata.taskId`) and nothing could read on that tag. A peer agent burned five paged reads over ~135 messages to pull out 14 of them, and the one-shot `limit=200` read that would have avoided the paging overruns its own output budget.
  2. **The close echo's seq was guessable and got guessed.** Closing a thread posts a lifecycle marker, so it moves the cursor, and only `closeTask` knew where. A requester closed a thread, assumed last-known-seq + 1, armed `await` one past that, and **silently skipped the peer's main deliverable** — the reply was already below the cursor, so the hold waited for something that had arrived. `openingSeq` had solved the identical problem at the other end of a thread since v1.8; the close had no mirror.
  3. **`seq` was documented as a per-channel counter and is not.** The identity sequence is on the TABLE, so a channel's seqs are gappy. A live census asked for "seqs 1-190" and got 143 real messages — an agent that reads a range as a count concludes it lost 47 messages.
  4. **Nothing told an agent what to do with a deliverable bigger than the 16000-char body cap**, and nothing told it to drain its inbox before shipping one. A scope correction landed 14 seconds after a deliverable went out; ~250 words of that deliverable were already wrong.
  5. **An external session had no wake at all.** F-106 (3) established that the tool must not PROMISE a wake it cannot observe, and stopped there — leaving the honest answer "you may simply not be woken". Measured since: MCP-call backgrounding did not engage on the caller it was tried against (6+ in-turn ~215s holds), while background-TASK completion demonstrably does wake an idle session.
  6. **A deferred desktop trigger was invisible inside the thread it deferred.** Both defer points already posted the `RESEND` bubble, but `postResult` carries no metadata and therefore lands OUTSIDE every thread, so the requester's thread showed nothing for 2m13s (against 15s for the first thread) and "queued" was indistinguishable from "ignored".
- Resolution (2026-07-31, uncommitted): (1) `?thread=<id>` on the message read, threaded schema → repository → client → `opRead`; a FILTER, not a lookup (any non-empty string, legacy `task-<channelId>-<seq>` ids included, no `channel_tasks` check, `[]` on no match), and it deliberately moves NO read watermark — the watermark is content-derived and monotonic, so a filtered read would mark unrelated older messages seen. (2) `closeTask` → `{ thread, echoSeq }` through the route, `@dopl/client` (`ChannelThreadClosed`) and `opCloseThread`, which prints the seq or says nothing; additive on the wire like `openingSeq`, so an older deployment reads as `null`. (3) §8's seq text corrected at the source (`ENGINEERING.md`, the `channel_messages` bullet + the append-RPC's "no gaps") and taught agent-side in the new `CONVENTIONS:` block. (4) that same block carries the dopl_kb-and-reference rule for large deliverables and the read-before-final-deliverable rule. (5) `scripts/dopl-channel-wait.sh` — env-driven `curl` loop on the await route, header-only token, `X-Workspace-Id`, exits 0/2/3 — plus ONE conditional sentence appended to `HOLD_FACT` so it reaches all four unstamped call sites from one place. (6) `main/queued-notice.js` posts one `task_progress` per episode, `client_msg_id = queued-<taskId>` (thread-keyed, so the peer's resend under a new seq dedupes against it), legacy ids skipped, fail-soft; `postTaskEvent` grew an `opts` parameter and a boolean return to support it. **§2 split it forced:** `channel-ops-await.ts` off `channel-ops-read.ts` (480 → 271), on the seam that file had drawn twice already — `await` is the only op there that loops.
- **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, flagged rather than buried:** a close whose echo post fails no longer throws. Previously the caller saw a 5xx for a close that HAD landed, and its retry re-closed; it is now reported as `echoSeq: null`. Every error the close ITSELF raises (not a member, not creator-or-target, unknown thread) is unchanged and still throws.
- Tests: `channel-thread-scope.test.ts` (12, NEW — both ends of the scoped cursor), `repository-messages.test.ts` (7, NEW — the collapsed cursor builder), `schema-query.test.ts` (10, NEW — the read/await/consent query schemas, moved out of the over-cap `schema.test.ts`), the thread-scope and echo describes in `service-reads.test.ts` / `service-tasks-lifecycle.test.ts`, and `dopl-desktop-app/test/queued-notice.test.mjs` (12, NEW). Green at time of writing: root channels suite **444**, MCP `channel*` **172**, desktop queued-notice **12**.
- Residuals, all deliberate and none scheduled:
  - **(a) No index backs the thread filter.** `channel_messages` carries `(channel_id, seq)` and `workspace_id` only — nothing functional on `metadata->>'taskId'` — so a scoped read is a filter over the channel scan. Correct at today's volumes; the fix if channels grow is a functional index, not a schema change.
  - **(b) `await` has no thread filter, and the fact lives in four strings.** `channel-ops-read.ts:125` and `:145` (the empty and trailer renders), `channel.ts:78` (the `read` op line) and `channel.ts:254` (the `thread` param describe) all state it. If a thread-scoped hold is ever built, those four move together or the tool starts lying about itself.
  - **(c) There is still no local queue behind the queued notice.** Pickup rides the peer's resend loop exactly as before; the notice closes the silence, not the latency. **A real deferred retry at the settle site is the follow-up** — the two defer sites (`trigger.js:342` engine/same-thread, `:372` headless/per-channel) already know everything a retry would need.
  - **(d) claude.ai connectors remain wake-less.** The scope matrix after this batch: desktop woken (v1.9, replies fed as new turns), terminal-with-background-shell now has a real wake, connector has none and cannot have one from here — there is no shell to run the poll in. That cell stays open; do not read `BACKGROUND_TASK_HINT` as having closed it.
  - **(e) `packages/dopl-client/src/client.ts` is 696 lines** (was 695; F-093's re-derived backlog measured it there). ENGINEERING.md §2's table had it at 592 — ~100 lines stale before this batch touched it — and is corrected, with a pointer added to F-093 as the live measurement. The scheduled per-domain method-group extraction is unchanged and unstarted.
- Cross-references: **F-106** — same primitive, next layer; this adds the tier-1 external wake its resolution deliberately stopped short of, and none of its residuals (a)–(d) are affected. **F-105** — the close echo's seq gives the requester a TRUE cursor at the moment an exchange ends, which is the cursor half of the stop-rule story; the thread half (nothing ever sets `status='closed'`, so the stop-rule's "thread is closed" disjunct effectively never fires) is untouched and stays the user's product call.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done) — residuals (a)–(e) are recorded, not queued
- Status: resolved (residuals noted); Dopl "Dopl Development" KB sync pending

### F-110: Multiplayer shipped with four ways an N-party room was still a two-party assumption — all fixed, the residuals only
- Location: **schema** — `supabase/migrations/20260731120000_channel_agents.sql`, `20260731130000_channel_task_participants.sql` (both NEW, both applied live as remote `20260731224658` / `20260731224726`). **server** — `src/features/channels/server/service-participants.ts` (NEW, `isThreadCurator` / `mayRemoveIdentity`), `service-agents.ts` + `agent-names.ts` + `repository-agents.ts` + `repository-participants.ts` + `agents-dto.ts` + `service-writes-agents.ts` (all NEW), `service-writes-metadata.ts` (`mayWriteThread`, `isLegacyThreadParticipant`, the owner bridge), `service-tasks.ts` (`seedThreadParticipants`), `schema-agents.ts`, `errors.ts`, `http-mapping.ts`, `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/agents/**` + `.../tasks/[taskId]/participants/route.ts`. **MCP** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-description.ts` + `channel-schema.ts` + `channel-agent-refs.ts` + `channel-ops-agents.ts` + `channel-law.test.ts` + `channel-post-linkage.ts` (all NEW), `channel.ts`, `channel-errors.ts`, `channel-ops-threads.ts`, `channel-ops-write.ts`, `identity.ts`, `server.ts`. **desktop** — `main/session-pool.js` + `session-team.js` + `channel-agents.js` (NEW), `listener-io.js` (the durable `teamAgents` store), `targeting.js`, `session-history.js`, `session-store.js`, `session-spawner.js`, `session-engine.js`, `session-park.js`
- Found during: three adversarial per-lane reviews of the multiplayer wave, 2026-07-31 — after the build was green and before it was committed. **None of the four was found by a test; all four are the same mistake in four costumes** — a rule written for two parties, then handed N.
- Severity: bug
- Description:
  1. **A participant join was gated on CHANNEL membership, which is not authority over one of the channel's threads.** `mayWriteThread` says a thread WITH a participant set is a breakout room whose set decides who may post. So in a private channel `{A,B,C}`, C could write itself into A↔B's thread and then post into it — including a `task_failed` carrying the calm-terminal flags (`declined`, `interrupted`, …), which re-stamps the outcome rendered on A's and B's cards. That is exactly the forgery the reserved-key strip and the legacy-id gate exist to prevent, reached through the front door. The defence that "participants are only seeded when a create asks for them" was true of `createTask` and said nothing about the join route.
  2. **THE LAW was OFF until a roster read succeeded, and OFF FOREVER if it 404'd.** `targeting.classify` disables the implicit two-member trigger on `entry.teamAgents === 0`, and that count was published only by `channel-agents.noteRoster` — which runs only on a successful read. Listener loops start live before reconcile, so the window between boot and the first successful roster read had the law disarmed, and a channel whose roster read failed had it disarmed permanently. The failure mode is not "nothing happens": it is the implicit trigger firing in a room that has team agents, which is the storm the law exists to prevent.
  3. **THE LAW promised something false, and contradicted itself two paragraphs later.** `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION` told agents that addressing a human never spawns their agent. That is true only when `as_agent` is passed — an OPTIONAL parameter, default off — so the default behaviour of the documented action was the opposite of the documentation. The same description then stated the correct rule in its `post` bullet, so the tool disagreed with itself in one string. `channel-law.test.ts` had a negative guard that should have caught it and did not: it grepped whole LINES for three exact phrases, so a paragraph carrying one true clause and one false one passed.
  4. **`create_thread` with a workspace-but-not-channel participant orphaned a live thread and lied about it in both halves.** Participants resolved through `resolveMemberOr`, which reads the WORKSPACE roster, so a workspace member who was not in the channel passed resolution and failed at the route. The thread row is inserted BEFORE the set is seeded, so the thread existed — and the error said "No thread was opened". Separately, `join_thread`'s description claimed admitting somebody notifies them; nothing notifies anybody.
- Resolution (2026-07-31, uncommitted):
  1. **THE CURATION RULE** (`service-participants.ts`): only a thread's `created_by`, its `target_user_id`, or an existing `kind:"user"` participant may ADD a row — everyone else 403 `TASK_FORBIDDEN`, **including a caller adding only themselves**, because drop-in join is a product decision nobody made. The curator check runs BEFORE `assertIdentityBelongs` so the 400/201 split cannot be used to probe membership. Ejecting is narrower than admitting: self and own-agent always, anyone else creator/target only. A thread's set can now only ever grow by the decision of someone already inside it, so a thread nobody inside it curated stays a two-party thread forever.
  2. **A DURABLE PER-CHANNEL COUNT.** `listener-io.js` keeps a `{channelId: count}` map in electron-store under the key `teamAgents`; `channel-listener` seeds `entry.teamAgents` from it at boot alongside `rosterKnown: false`, so the law is armed before the first roster read. A null roster read KEEPS the last known count — **UNKNOWN is not EMPTY** — instead of resetting to zero.
  3. **The rule is keyed on `as_agent` in BOTH places** (the law block and the `post` op bullet, plus `to_agent`'s param prose): *with* `as_agent` + `to=` the person is notified and no agent starts; *without* it the same `to=` is the operator's REQUEST and triggers their listener. "There is no third case." `channel-law.test.ts` now splits on `(?<=[.!?])\s+` and requires any sentence pairing "the other side" with "starts/triggers" to be KEYED on `as_agent` — **plus a meta-test that feeds the guard the exact false sentence that shipped and asserts it is caught.** Do not delete that meta-test when refactoring the scanner; it is the only thing proving the scanner has teeth.
  4. **Channel-scoped resolution** (`channel-agent-refs.ts` — `listChannelAgents` / `listChannelMembers`, explicitly not `resolveMemberOr`), two new bad-request classifications (`CHANNEL_PARTICIPANT_NOT_MEMBER`, `CHANNEL_AGENT_NOT_IN_CHANNEL`) plus a new `ForbiddenKind` classifier, and **honest post-insert error text** that says a thread MAY have been opened, tells the caller to check `list_threads` before retrying, and explains that a same-`client_msg_id` retry re-posts the opener but does NOT re-seed the set. `join_thread`'s false clause is deleted and replaced with its denial: "Admitting somebody NOTIFIES NOBODY."
- **Also in this wave, and load-bearing for anyone reading §8:** the legacy-id gate (F-083 bullet 3 / audit Q10) is CLOSED — one shared resolver `isLegacyThreadParticipant`, tag STRIPPED on failure and never 403'd (desktop 1.7.16 compat), calm flags following the same tag decision — which is what unblocked the session history fence. `pairRows` widened afterwards and **only** for `bind === 'room'`; pair mode is byte-identical. Both corrections are made at source in ENGINEERING.md §8.
- Tests: `service-participants-curation.test.ts`, `service-participants.test.ts`, `service-agents.test.ts`, `agent-names.test.ts`, `repository-agents.test.ts`, `repository-participants.test.ts`, `schema-agents.test.ts`, `service-writes-metadata-agents.test.ts` / `-participants.test.ts` / `-thread.test.ts`, `agent-display.test.ts`, `composer-commands.test.ts`, `mention.test.ts`, `rooms.test.ts`, `agent-chips-bar.test.tsx`, `rooms-sidebar.test.tsx` (root); `channel-law.test.ts`, `channel-ops-agents.test.ts`, `channel-agent-addressing.test.ts`, `channel-locus.test.ts` (MCP); `channel-agents.test.mjs`, `session-pool.test.mjs`, `session-team.test.mjs`, `session-engine-slot.test.mjs`, `session-history-room.test.mjs`, `spawner-slot-release.test.mjs`, `team-addressing.test.mjs`, `prompt-framing-team.test.mjs` (desktop). Suite totals in the gate section above.
- Residuals, all verified against the code, none scheduled:
  - **(a) Agent owners are not curators — a PRODUCT CALL, not an oversight.** `isThreadCurator` tests `row.kind === "user"` only. Owning an agent that is in the set does not make you able to change the set, for the same reason `mayWriteThread` refuses to infer a human's write access from an agent they own. If the operator wants owner-curation, it is a one-line widening and a deliberate one.
  - **(b) Add and eject are ASYMMETRIC.** Any existing user participant may admit; ejecting a third party is creator/target only. Justified (admitting grows a room you are in, ejecting takes something from someone who was invited) but it means a participant can grow a room past what the two original parties would have chosen and cannot then shrink it.
  - **(c) `create_thread` cannot attribute its opening message, and the fix cannot land alone.** `as_agent` is REFUSED there on purpose — an agent-attributed opener classifies as notify-only escalation and would never wake the responder. Making it work needs `TaskCreateSchema.authorAgentId` **and** a desktop `classify` change in the same change; either half alone produces threads nobody answers.
  - **(d) `to_user_notify` is UNBUILT — and it is not in the reserved-key strip either.** Zero implementation; the only two occurrences repo-wide are `docs/MULTIPLAYER-PLAN.md` and a comment in `targeting.js`. **`resolvePostMetadata` does not strip it**, so a caller can set it today and it persists verbatim — harmless only while nothing reads it. **Whoever builds the consumer must add the strip in the SAME change**, or the key is spoofable on day one. This is the missing piece behind the desktop's `!isDirect` narrowing on agent→human escalation (ENGINEERING §18): the server stamps the same `to_user_id` for an explicit `to` and for the DM auto-address, so inside a DM the wire cannot tell an escalation from an ordinary peer reply.
  - **(e) `join_thread`'s member arm still resolves against the WORKSPACE roster.** `resolveParticipantOr` calls `resolveMemberOr` (which reads `listWorkspaceMembers`) for `user:` refs, unlike `create_thread`'s channel-scoped path. Harmless as it stands: `service-participants` runs `assertIdentityBelongs` before `findParticipant` and `insertParticipant`, so nothing is written before the 400, and the 400's text is accurate. It is an inconsistency between two resolvers for the same concept, not a hole.
  - **(f) `create_thread`'s idempotent retry re-posts the opening message but never re-seeds the participant set.** Seeding happens only after a successful `insertTask`; both retry branches (stored task, unique-violation race) call `postOpeningMessage` and skip seeding. So a create that failed mid-flight leaves a thread whose set must be repaired with `join_thread` — which is what the new error text now tells the caller to do, but the asymmetry itself is unfixed.
  - **(g) `pool.listActive` rows are not round-trippable for agent-keyed sessions.** `claim()` stores only `channelId`/`taskId`, never `agentId`, so for an agent key `taskId` is `''` and `slotKey(row)` cannot reproduce `row.key`. Latent today: the only `claim()` call site is the headless spawner, which never passes an `agentId`, and team sessions go through the engine registry rather than the pool. **Amplifier: `session-spawner.listActiveSpawns` has ZERO consumers** — the accounting surface this would corrupt is currently dead code. Fix the row shape before anything reads it.
  - **(h) The window budget can silently evict a summoned team shell and leave the server row `active`.** `session-park.evictIdleShell` selects any session with `state.parked === true`, `operatorTouched !== true` and no pending inbound — a freshly summoned shell matches all three — and settles it `interrupted`. Nothing calls `channelAgents.setStatus(..., SUMMONED)` on eviction, so the peer's agent chip keeps showing `active` for an agent that is gone. Partial mitigation only: the next addressed message re-summons it.
  - **(i) Dismissed teardown is unclaimed.** Dismissal stops an agent STARTING (three independent gates) and tears nothing DOWN. `reconcileChannelInner` never inspects dismissed rows and never settles a session; `settle` is exposed only to `session-park` for LRU eviction. A dismissed agent with a live session keeps it until some other pressure evicts it.
  - **(j) The `claudeSessions` map is now unbounded.** It was keyed by `channelId` (bounded by the channels you are in) and is now keyed by `sessionKey` — `(channel, thread)` today, `(channel, agent)` once the pool is used for team sessions. `getSessionId`/`setSessionId`/`clearSessionId` only add or delete one key at a time, and `session-store.pruneRecords` touches a DIFFERENT electron-store key (`sessionRecords`). Nothing prunes it.
  - **(k) The permission preset is CHANNEL-keyed, so one session can consume another's single-use arm.** `channel-prefs.js` stores `map[channelId] = {tools, messages, at}` and `takeArmFrom` deletes on read; `trigger.js` consumes it by channel id. Pre-existing (H2, 2026-07-31) and correct when a channel had one session — **amplified by N**, because the first of several agents in a room takes an arm a human armed for a different one.
  - **(l) Three COMMITTED charset migrations are NOT applied to prod.** `20260731090000_profiles_display_name_bounds`, `20260731100000_channels_name_topic_bounds`, `20260731110000_short_label_charset_bounds` are all in git and all absent from the remote migration history. This wave's two migrations ARE live. Unrelated to multiplayer; noticed while verifying the ledger and recorded here because nothing else tracks it.
  - **(m) The desktop code is built and NOT released.** `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` still reads **1.7.16** and is unmodified in the tree — no 1.7.17 bump, no notarize run. The live two-machine smoke in `docs/MULTIPLAYER-PLAN.md` cannot run until it is, and residuals (h), (i) and (k) are exactly what that smoke should be watching for.
  - **(n) Two §2 / cost notes, recorded rather than acted on.** `mayWriteThread` adds an **unconditional** `listParticipantsByTask` round-trip to every UUID-tagged post, including every legacy two-party thread that will never have a set. And the `ChannelAgentsClient` extraction (`client-channel-agents.ts`) justifies itself by the 500-line cap while leaving `packages/dopl-client/src/client.ts` at **698, two lines MORE than the 696 it started at** — the method bodies left, the import + `extends` + docblock came back. An extraction that cites the cap must be measured against it.
- Cross-references: **F-109 / F-106** — the same primitive one layer out; none of their residuals are affected. **F-083** — its bullet 3 (the open legacy-id gate) is closed by this wave and should be read as discharged. **F-093** — `client.ts` is 698 now, not 696; its re-derived backlog needs the new number.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done) — residuals (a)–(n) are recorded, not queued. (d), (g) and (l) are the three a future session is most likely to trip over.
- **STALE, corrected 2026-07-31: residual (m) said the desktop "still reads 1.7.16 and is unmodified".** It reads **1.7.17** (`7940d97`), and this wave itself was committed as `4e1252d` — so "uncommitted" throughout this entry means "uncommitted at the time of writing", not now. What survives from (m) is the part that mattered: the version is cut, **no notarize run has happened, and nothing is released**. See F-111 residual (n) for the new problem the bump created.
- **RESIDUALS RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — NINE OF FOURTEEN ARE OBSOLETE, ONE IS FIXED, FOUR SURVIVE.** Do not work this list without reading this bullet first; most of it describes deleted files.
  - **OBSOLETE — the subject is gone (channels rollback §1, F-141).** ~~(a) `isThreadCurator` tests `kind === "user"`~~, ~~(b) add and eject are asymmetric~~, ~~(e) `join_thread`'s member arm resolves against the workspace roster~~, ~~(f) an idempotent `create_thread` retry never re-seeds the set~~ — `service-participants.ts` and `repository-participants.ts` are deleted and there is no participant set to curate, admit into, resolve for or re-seed. ~~(c) `create_thread` cannot attribute its opening message because `as_agent` is refused there~~ — `as_agent` is deleted (`channel-schema.ts:29`); there is no attribution to make. ~~(h) the window budget can evict a summoned team shell and leave the row `active`~~ and ~~(i) dismissed teardown is unclaimed~~ — `channel-agents.js` and `session-team.js` are deleted; nothing summons, dismisses or sets an agent status.
  - **FIXED.** ~~(n) first half — `mayWriteThread` adds an unconditional `listParticipantsByTask` round-trip to every UUID-tagged post~~ — it collapsed back to the synchronous `isThreadParticipant` (`service-writes-metadata-thread.ts:34`) when breakout rooms were rolled back, so the round-trip is gone. (n)'s SECOND half stands and got worse: `packages/dopl-client/src/client.ts` is **731** lines, not 698, and the per-domain extraction is still unstarted (ENGINEERING §2 carries the number).
  - **(d) `to_user_notify` — CLOSED 2026-08-06 by F-151.** It is stripped in `resolvePostMetadata` and never re-stamped. The residual's own instruction ("the consumer and the strip must ship in the SAME change") is what kept it open for six days: rollback §1 deleted the consumer, so the instruction could never be satisfied and the key would have stayed caller-settable on a promise nobody could keep. Stripping alone is the honest end state for a documented-reserved name with no writer and no reader.
  - **(g) STILL RECORDED, NOW UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION.** `claim()` (`session-pool.js:71-82`) still stores only `channelId`/`taskId`, so `slotKey(row)` still could not reproduce an agent-keyed `row.key` — but there is no longer any producer of an `agentId`, and `listActiveSpawns` still has ZERO consumers. Fix the row shape before anything reads it; nothing does.
  - **(j) STILL REAL, and the only unbounded map left.** `session-spawner.js:49` keys `claudeSessions` by slot key, `getSessionId`/`setSessionId`/`clearSessionId` touch one key at a time, and `store.pruneRecords` prunes a DIFFERENT electron-store key. Not fixed here because the fix is a retention POLICY, not a guard — `prunableKeys` deliberately protects records that still hold a resume id, so choosing when to evict decides when a session stops being resumable. Give it the `prunableKeys` treatment: one pure policy, called from `session-engine.init` beside the record prune.
  - **(k) STILL REAL, DE-AMPLIFIED.** `channel-prefs.js:68` still stores `map[channelId]` and `takeArmFrom` still deletes on read. The "first of several agents in a room takes an arm meant for another" amplifier went with named agents; what is left is the original single-session H2 shape. Re-keying the arm to a slot touches the same surface F-119's `adoptsConsent` single-setter pin guards, so it is not a drive-by.
  - **(l) STILL OPEN AND UNVERIFIABLE FROM THE TREE.** All three charset migrations are present in `supabase/migrations/`; whether they are applied is a question for the remote migration history, and the 2026-08-06 pass had no database access. Treat it as a CHECK to run, not a fix to make.
  - **(m) MOOT.** `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` reads **1.9.0**. The version question this residual and F-111 (n) both tracked belongs to whatever ships next, not here.
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **residual list corrected 2026-08-06 (F-151): live residuals are (g), (j), (k), (l) and (n)'s size half; everything else is obsolete, fixed, or moot**

### F-111: A room where nobody could just TALK, an @-mention that was decoration, and a breakout room two agents were deaf inside — all fixed, the residuals only
- Location: **schema** — `supabase/migrations/20260731140000_channel_agent_engagement.sql` (NEW, applied live), `20260731150000_drop_channel_agents_engaged_idx.sql` (NEW, applied live — drops the index the previous migration created). **server** — `src/features/channels/schema.ts:167-200,230-250` (`MessageIntentSchema`, `MAX_ADDRESSED_AGENTS`, `toAgents`), `server/service-writes-agents.ts:105-108,147-152,160-165,207-215,328-347`, `server/service-writes-metadata.ts:349-361,382-386,398`, `server/service-thread-handshake.ts` (NEW), `server/service-writes-members.ts` (NEW), `server/service-agents.ts:173-176,203,240-256`, `server/repository-agents.ts:117-127` (+ `clearEngagementByEngager`), `server/errors.ts:196-203,242`, `server/http-mapping.ts:74-78`, `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/agents/[agentId]/route.ts:43-45`. **web** — `lib/mention.ts:190-259`, `lib/composer-mode.ts:108-129,152-156,246-268,317-330`, `lib/agent-engagement.ts` (NEW), `lib/thread-agents.ts` (NEW), `components/message-composer.tsx:161,430`, `components/agent-chips-bar.tsx:202-203,246,347,427-436`, `constants.ts:105`. **MCP** — `channel-handshake-key.ts` + `channel-render-agents.ts` + `channel-post-notes.ts` + `channel-ops-open.ts` (all NEW), `channel-description.ts` (THE LAW), `channel-schema.ts`, `channel-addressing.ts:85`, `channel-ops-threads.ts:120-126,236-242`, `channel-ops-await.ts:296-313`, `channel-render.ts:278-288`, `channel-law.test.ts:257,319-332,351-393`. **desktop** — `main/channel-engagement.js` + `channel-roster.js` + `channel-threads.js` + `legacy-threads.js` + `realtime-agents.js` + `session-shell.js` + `session-state.js` + `session-greeting.js` + `targeting-window.js` (all NEW), `main/channel-agents.js:262-340,448,460`, `main/targeting.js:67-73,194-202`, `main/realtime.js`, `main/listener-messages.js:55,66`, `dopl-desktop-app/test/live/**` (NEW tier)
- Found during: live use of the multiplayer wave (F-110) on 2026-07-31, immediately after it committed — the operator's own first-run flow. **None of the six was found by a test, and the suite was green through every one of them.** Two were found by reading THE LAW's own sentences back against the code they describe.
- Severity: bug
- Description: six defects with one shape — **the model was written for a room of AGENTS and the room is actually full of PEOPLE.** Every one of them is a case where the product's stated law had no way to express something a human obviously wanted to do.
  1. **TWO HUMANS COULD NOT TALK WITHOUT WAKING AN AGENT.** In a DM the server auto-addresses every post to the peer, so "sounds good" hit the implicit two-member trigger and spawned an ASSIST session. There was no wire shape for "this is people talking" — "nothing acts unless addressed" could not say it, because in a DM everything IS addressed.
  2. **`@handle` WAS DECORATION.** The multiplayer wave shipped mention autocomplete and an `@`-styled token in the transcript, and **nothing resolved those tokens at send time**. The operator's core flow — summon an agent with `/new-agent`, type "@quartz do X" — sent a plain unaddressed message and reached nobody. The feature looked complete from the UI and was inert on the wire.
  3. **A SUMMONED AGENT WAS DEAF TO THE PERSON WHO SUMMONED IT.** `routeAddressedAgent` opened with a blanket `m.authorUserId === myUserId` exclusion — a fossil of the two-party ASSIST model, where the only trigger was a PEER asking and "a message I wrote is not a request to me" was true. Under TEAM, the agent runs on the author's own machine and the author is the person it is most listening for, so every conjunct pointed at this machine and the route dropped the message on the floor. **Defects 2 and 3 compose:** even once mentions resolved, the operator's own "@quartz do X" would still have gone nowhere.
  4. **A BREAKOUT ROOM WAS A ROOM TWO AGENTS WERE DEAF INSIDE.** Team sessions key `(channel, agent)`; `feedLiveSession` looks up `(channel, task)` with no `agentId`. So a thread-tagged post **never** reached a peer agent's session, and THE LAW's own published sentence — *"inside a thread everyone in it hears everything: no tagging between participants"* — was false on every desktop. This is the core two-agent feature and it had never worked.
  5. **THE LOOP BRAKE WAS DEFEATABLE BY ONE WORD.** Engagement keyed on `authorKind`, which §8 v1.1 already documents as **caller-assertable** (`PostableAuthorKindSchema` accepts it from anybody). An agent-token caller posted `{toAgent:"quartz", authorKind:"user"}`, quartz was engaged for 60 minutes, and the agent then drove it with untagged lines carrying the same assertable label — two agents sustaining each other with **no human at any hop**, which is precisely the loop the brake exists to break. **A test pinned the old behaviour as INTENDED** (`service-agents-engagement.test.ts:11-19` records that it "used to pin the other way round"), so the suite proved the hole rather than catching it.
  6. **AN AGENT COULD NOT SEE THAT IT HAD BEEN CO-ADDRESSED, WHICH IS THE PRECONDITION FOR THE HANDSHAKE.** `read`/`await` rendered nothing about agent addressing, and `AWAIT_UNNAMED_NOTICE` tested `to_user_id` alone — a field the owner bridge stamps from the **FIRST** addressed agent's owner. So on "@quartz @onyx work on X", onyx's machine woke to a message naming its own agent by handle and was told *"NONE of the messages above NAMES you as its addressee"*: instructed, in writing, not to act on the job it had just been handed. Separately, two agents handed the same instruction — one holding a channel SLUG, one holding the UUID — minted DIFFERENT `thread-open-` keys, hit different entries of the partial unique index, and would have opened TWO threads for one instruction.
- Resolution (2026-07-31, uncommitted on `7940d97`):
  1. **`intent: "chat" | "request"`** on the post. `chat` skips the DM peer auto-address entirely (the peer is never resolved, not resolved-and-discarded) and is refused 400 `CHANNEL_CHAT_ADDRESSED` with a human `to`. **There is no default and nothing writes or compares `"request"`** — the feature is negative branching on `=== "chat"`, and `metadata.intent` is stamped only when supplied, so every existing post is byte-identical. `intent` joined the reserved-key strip list. `chat` + agent mentions is ALLOWED by design: tagging an agent is not requesting a person, and chat+`toAgents` is the composer's primary shape for giving an agent work. Web composer is two-mode, **Chat default**; Request keeps subject + recipient and opens a thread.
  2. **`extractMentionedAgents`** resolves `@handle` out of the composed body at send time against the CHANNEL roster — backtick-blanked, whitespace-anchored (an email can never be a mention), case-folded, deduped, capped — and sends `toAgents`. **MULTI-ADDRESS with it:** `toAgents[]` → `metadata.to_agent_ids[]`, `to_agent_id` retained as a compat mirror for installed desktop 1.7.17 (which reads only the scalar), cap `MAX_ADDRESSED_AGENTS` = 8 enforced on the **deduped merge** of `toAgent`+`toAgents` ⇒ 400 `CHANNEL_TOO_MANY_AGENTS`. `MAX_DERIVED_AGENTS` now imports the same constant — as literals they diverged, and `toAgent` + eight made NINE addressed agents where the ninth was engaged, woken, truncated out of the handshake derivation, and then 403'd out of the thread it had been woken for.
  3. **`myOwnAgentSpoke(m, myUserId)`** = own account AND not `humanAuthored` — identity alone cannot tell my typing from my agent's output; `author_kind` can. Applied to the ADDRESSED and ENGAGED lanes only. **Deliberately NOT applied to the thread lane** (see 4).
  4. **A THIRD ROUTING LANE, order addressed → thread → engaged**, ahead of `feedLiveSession`. Participation is read from an authenticated `GET /api/channels/<id>/tasks/<threadId>` and **never from stamped metadata** — a participant list on a message is a claim, and this decision starts work on the machine. Cached in `main/channel-threads.js` (TTL 5 min, fail-backoff 30s, LRU 100, single-flight). Inside a thread the participant set is the standing consent; **outside one the loop brake is unchanged**. Three things replace the brake in that lane: the human-curated server-side set (no self-join, owning an agent confers no curation), a self-echo filter, and the inbound gate. **ENGAGEMENT** is the other half — `engaged_at`/`engaged_by`, 60 min, TTL applied CLIENT-side and only RECORDED server-side, refreshed by acting via a LOCAL sliding window rather than a PATCH-per-message (which is the F-072 anti-pattern verbatim).
  5. **The brake keys on `ctx.source === "agent"`** — derived from the credential, with no field a caller can set — and it returns FIRST. **The `authorKind` / `author_agent_id` checks stay as defence in depth**: they catch an operator's COOKIE session posting on an agent's behalf, and installed 1.7.17, which labels agent replies `authorKind:"agent"` with no `author_agent_id`. Knock-on, intended: MCP posts are agent-credentialed, so `to_agent` over MCP addresses, wakes and routes but no longer ENGAGES.
  6. **`agentAddressTag`** renders `` · to agents `handle` (`id`) `` on `read` and `await`, degrading to the bare id rather than to silence; `AWAIT_UNNAMED_NOTICE` now also asks whether any addressed agent is mine, and **fail-soft is preserved** (an empty roster makes the notice fire exactly as before rather than suppressing on a guess). `normalizeHandshakeKey` rewrites `thread-open-…` keys onto the RESOLVED channel uuid inside `opCreateThread`, and a malformed key is refused rather than repaired; a rewrite is always reported in the result. **`service-thread-handshake.ts` then DERIVES the participant set server-side from the instruction's own server-stamped `to_agent_ids`** — because a rule an agent has to remember is a rule the third model that reads it will break. Every miss seeds nothing, silently.
  - **THE LAW rewritten** for chat / engagement / loop-brake / handshake, and its negative guard **joined by a second, general one**: *"a sentence must not promise the READER an effect the reader cannot cause."* That is the rule that would have caught four of this session's six agent-facing lies, and its teeth-test pins the exact false sentence that shipped.
- Tests: `service-writes-intent.test.ts`, `service-writes-multi-agent.test.ts`, `service-writes-members.test.ts`, `service-agents-engagement.test.ts` (+ `.fixtures.ts`), `service-agents-disengage.test.ts`, `service-tasks-handshake.test.ts` (+ `.fixtures.ts`), `service-tasks-handshake-room.test.ts`, `service-thread-handshake.test.ts`, `composer-mode.test.ts`, `mention.test.ts`, `thread-agents.test.ts`, `agent-engagement.test.ts`, `agent-engagement-chip.test.ts`, `agent-chips-bar-engagement.test.tsx` (root); `channel-handshake-key.test.ts`, `channel-agent-address-render.test.ts`, `channel-thread-participants.test.ts`, `channel-ops-participants.test.ts`, `channel-law.test.ts`'s second guard + teeth-test (MCP); `channel-engagement.test.mjs`, `channel-threads.test.mjs`, `session-greeting.test.mjs`, `session-posture-copy.test.mjs` (desktop). **Green: root 1880 (111 files), `packages/mcp-server` 413 (31), `packages/dopl-client` 48 (3), desktop 1722. `tsc --noEmit` clean in root + both packages; `npx eslint src packages` 0 errors (1 baseline `proxy.ts` warning); `dopl-desktop-app` `npx eslint .` clean.**
- **NEW TEST TIER, recorded because it is not in any suite total above: `dopl-desktop-app/test/live/`** (5 files, ~1187 lines) — a live contract harness run by `npm run test:live`, deliberately `.js` so the default `node --test 'test/**/*.mjs'` cannot pick it up and a credential-less run is a clean exit 0. It posts real messages through the real API and feeds each returned message through the REAL desktop decision modules **twice — once as sender, once as peer** — which is what makes it a one-Mac cross-account test. Throwaway `harness-<stamp>` channel, real DMs in `FORBIDDEN_CHANNEL_IDS`, PASS/FAIL/**SKIP** where SKIP means the target deployment lacks that capability.
- Residuals, all verified against the code, none scheduled:
  - **(a) `to_user_notify` is STILL unbuilt and STILL not in the reserved-key strip.** Re-verified this wave; unchanged from F-110 residual (d) and repeated here because this wave touched `resolvePostMetadata` and did not fix it. The strip list is exactly `to_user_id`, `summary`, `runtime`, `appVersion`, `to_agent_id`, `to_agent_ids`, `author_agent_id`, `intent`, then the five calm flags, then the four `task*` keys — **`to_user_notify` is in none of them**, so a caller can set it today and it persists verbatim. Harmless only while nothing reads it. **THE CONSUMER AND THE STRIP MUST SHIP IN THE SAME CHANGE, or the key is spoofable on day one.**
  - **(b) An owner missing from the loaded roster reads as UNKNOWN, not offline, so a chip can briefly over-promise.** `agent-chips-bar.tsx:202` uses `members.find(...)`, which yields `undefined` during roster load or for an owner who has left the channel; `deriveAgentEngagement` short-circuits `owner && !owner.agentOnline`, so the row falls through to `"engaged"` and `isListening()` returns true. Deliberate (`AgentOwnerPresence`'s docblock says `undefined` means WE DO NOT KNOW and is not to be read as offline), and the safe default for a transient load — but the chip shows fully-listening for an agent whose machine may be gone.
  - **(c) Web disengage is OWNER-ONLY while the server also allows the ENGAGER.** `canDisengageAgent` requires `isAgentOwner`, and its own docblock calls this "DELIBERATELY NARROWER THAN THE SERVER". Mitigated by copy — a non-owner engager is told "You engaged it. Only its owner can disengage it here." — so the state is explained rather than hidden, but the API affordance has no UI.
  - **(d) `@parked-agent`: the web will not resolve it, the server will.** `mention.ts` filters to `summoned | active` via `isAddressableAgent`; `resolveChannelAgent` applies **no status filter at all** and says so in a comment. Neither side is wrong on its own; which one is right is an **unmade product call** (does addressing a parked agent wake it, or is parking a mute?). Decide it before either side is "fixed".
  - **(e) A chat post carries NO `summary`, so a peer's consent prompt is built from the raw body.** `SendOptions` deliberately dropped `toUserId` and `summary` — they are the two fields that turn a chat post into an addressed one, and a live wire nobody drives was the shape the bug came back through, so deleting them makes it a type error. Correct as a design call. **The consequence is downstream:** an agent-addressed chat message still reaches the owner's machine, and `trigger.js:87` falls back to `truncate(m.body, 120)`, so the operator's Accept card shows a body slice instead of a one-line intent. The fix is not to re-add `summary` to chat; it is to give the mention path its own summary derivation.
  - **(f) Mentions typed in REQUEST mode are silently inert.** `message-composer.tsx:161` resolves them only when `mode === "chat"`; the request payload never touches the roster and the mode's help copy says nothing about it. A user who types "@quartz" into a Request gets no address and no notice.
  - **(g) The 9th mention is dropped client-side with no explanation.** The cap is 8 and `extractMentionedAgents` simply `break`s; the composer's helper line lists the ≤8 resolved handles and there is no cap copy anywhere. The server WOULD have refused a 9th with `CHANNEL_TOO_MANY_AGENTS` — the client silently prevents the caller from ever seeing that error, which is the worse of the two behaviours. **Related source defect: `mention.ts:222` says a "tenth" handle is dropped. With the cap at 8 it is the NINTH** — `schema.ts:186` and `service-writes-agents.ts:138` both say ninth, so `mention.ts` is the odd one out.
  - **(h) Two agents in one thread on the AUTO message posture have no SHORT bound.** On the default manual/ask posture every hop needs an operator Accept. With both sides on auto there is no human in the loop and the pair ping-pongs until a per-session bound stops it — turn cap (24), cost cap, or idle timeout. **Finite, and nothing makes it short.** Recorded as the honest bound rather than described away; it is the same trade the auto posture makes everywhere else, but it is the first place where TWO machines compound it.
  - **(i) `channel-threads.js`'s cache has NO invalidation — only TTL, backoff and LRU.** `channel_task_participants` is deliberately outside the realtime publication, so there is no doorbell. A participant REMOVED from a thread may still be fed for up to 5 minutes. Bounded in the safe direction only because the server refuses that agent's WRITE immediately, so the worst case is a turn that runs and cannot post — which is wasted spend, not a leak.
  - **(j) `channel-law.test.ts`'s bullet ceiling does not measure bullets.** The test named `keeps the law to at most 12 BULLETS` counts non-blank LINES including the `THE LAW OF THIS ROOM` header, so the real ceiling is **11** and the law is at 11 today. A twelfth bullet fails a test whose name says it should pass. Rename it or count `- ` prefixes; do not raise the number to make room.
  - **(k) The second law guard's GENERAL FORM lives in a docblock, not in an assertion.** What it enforces is a narrow `ENGAGES` × `HUMAN_KEYED` regex pair. The general rule — never promise the reader an effect the reader cannot cause — is the one worth keeping, and today only a human applies it. Do not read the docblock as coverage.
  - **(l) The desktop RESTATES `ENGAGEMENT_TTL_MS` and disagrees with the web by 1 ms on the clock-skew arm** (web `age > -TTL` exclusive, desktop `-age <= TTL` inclusive). Restating is correct and unavoidable (separate package, own release cadence); the 1 ms is cosmetic and is recorded so nobody re-derives it as a bug. **What IS worth fixing at source:** migration `20260731140000`'s header says the TTL is applied "on the desktop", which is half the truth — the WEB's `lib/agent-engagement.ts` is what drives the chip. The `…150000` migration states it correctly.
  - **(m) `test/live/` matches no eslint config block.** `dopl-desktop-app/eslint.config.js` matches `main|scripts|renderer/**/*.js` plus `test/**/*.mjs`; `test/live/*.js` matches neither, so 1187 lines (largest file 383) get no `max-lines` and no `languageOptions` — the one tree in this repo with a zero-exemption cap has a corner with no cap at all. The `.js` extension is load-bearing (it keeps the tier out of `npm test`), so the fix is a config block, not a rename.
  - **(n) THE REWORKED BUILD CARRIES THE SAME VERSION STRING AS THE SHIPPED ONE.** `package.json` was bumped to 1.7.17 for F-110 (`7940d97`) and F-111 changed routing, engagement and the doorbell on top of it **without a further bump**. So `app-version.js` / `version-skew.js` / the `X-Dopl-App-Version` header cannot distinguish a machine running F-110's desktop from one running F-111's — and this wave is precisely the one where two machines behave differently (one routes thread posts and honours engagement, the other does not). **Bump before the two-machine smoke**, or the skew observation is blind for the run it matters most in.
- Cross-references: **F-110** — this is its next layer and its residual (m) is corrected in place above; (a) here is (d) there, re-verified and still open. **F-109 / F-106** — same primitive further out; none of their residuals are affected. **F-093 / F-041** — ENGINEERING §2's over-cap table was reconciled against the `eslint.config.mjs` exemption list in this pass (it listed 5 of 14; `server.ts` read 612 against 915 on disk), so §2 is a measurement again rather than a pointer. **F-072** — the two places this wave could have re-created the read-amplification shape and deliberately did not: engagement refresh is a LOCAL sliding window instead of a PATCH per inbound message, and the thread participant read is TTL-cached, backed-off, capped and single-flight.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done) — residuals (a)–(n) are recorded, not queued. **(a), (h) and (n) are the three a future session is most likely to be hurt by**: (a) becomes a security hole the day someone builds the consumer, (h) is a spend risk the moment an operator flips both postures, and (n) makes the live smoke unable to tell the two builds apart.
- **RESIDUALS RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — SEVEN OF FOURTEEN ARE OBSOLETE, TWO ARE FIXED, FIVE SURVIVE.** This entry's whole subject — a room of NAMED AGENTS reached by `@handle` and held open by ENGAGEMENT — was deleted by rollback §1, so most of what follows describes files that are not in the tree.
  - **OBSOLETE — the subject is gone.** ~~(b) an owner missing from the loaded roster reads as UNKNOWN, so a chip over-promises~~ and ~~(c) web disengage is owner-only while the server allows the engager~~ — `agent-chips-bar.tsx` and `lib/agent-engagement.ts` are deleted. ~~(d) `@parked-agent`: the web will not resolve it, the server will~~, ~~(f) mentions typed in REQUEST mode are silently inert~~, ~~(g) the 9th mention is dropped client-side with no explanation (and `mention.ts:222` says "tenth")~~ — `lib/mention.ts` is deleted and `toAgent`/`toAgents` are `z.never()` (`schema.ts:209`), so there is no mention to resolve, drop or miscount. ~~(i) `channel-threads.js`'s cache has no invalidation~~ — that module is deleted with the thread routing lane. ~~(l) the desktop restates `ENGAGEMENT_TTL_MS` and disagrees with the web by 1 ms~~ — engagement is gone from both sides; `engaged_at`/`engaged_by` survive only as historical columns the DTO does not map (`service-agents.test.ts:201`).
  - **(a) `to_user_notify` — CLOSED 2026-08-06 by F-151.** See F-110 residual (d): stripped in `resolvePostMetadata`, pinned in `service-writes-metadata-attribution.test.ts`. This residual was the SECOND recording of the same fact and it changed nothing; that is the pattern F-151 is about.
