  - **(j) THE BULLET CEILING — CLOSED 2026-08-06 by F-151.** The test was worse than this residual described: by 2026-08-06 its NAME said "at most 12 BULLETS" while its assertion said `<= 8` over non-blank lines, and the slice still included the `THE LAW OF THIS ROOM` header — so the enforced ceiling was 7 rules against a name promising 12. It now counts `- ` prefixes with the ceiling of 8 that was already written down. Fixed the way the residual asked (count bullets), not the way it warned against (raise the number).
  - **(m) THE UNLINTED TIER — CLOSED 2026-08-06 by F-151.** `test/live/**/*.js` has its own `eslint.config.js` block with the same `max-lines` and `no-redeclare`, and the `.js` extension is untouched because it is what keeps the tier out of `npm test`. Worth recording how it aged: the residual measured 1187 lines and the tier was at **1681** when it was finally capped — the gap grew 40% while the finding sat open. `test/eslint-config-coverage.test.mjs` now asks ESLint itself whether EVERY source file in the tree is capped, so the next uncovered directory fails a test instead of waiting for a reader.
  - **(h) STILL REAL.** Two agents in one thread on the AUTO posture still have no SHORT bound. Named agents are gone but the shape is not: a thread is two members, each with their own session, and both postures are the operator's to flip. Finite (turn cap 24, cost cap, idle timeout); nothing makes it short.
  - **(k) STILL REAL.** The second law guard's GENERAL form — *never promise the reader an effect the reader cannot cause* — still lives only in a docblock. What is asserted is a narrow `OTHER_SIDE` × `STARTS` × `KEYED` regex triple (`channel-law.test.ts`), and its `KEYED` alternation was rewritten when `as_agent` died. Do not read the docblock as coverage.
  - **(n) MOOT.** `package.json` reads **1.9.0**; the 1.7.17-vs-1.7.17 skew this described cannot recur for those builds.
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **residual list corrected 2026-08-06 (F-151): live residuals are (h) and (k); (a), (j) and (m) are FIXED; everything else is obsolete or moot**

### F-112: Every in-thread agent update was silently undeliverable — the desktop refused the one kind its own prompts ask for
- Location: **desktop** — `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-agents.js:230` (the blanket `if (!m || m.kind !== 'message') return '';`, now `MILESTONE_KINDS` + `routeAddressedAgent`), `main/channel-deliver.js` (unchanged — `selfEcho` already covered this path), `main/targeting.js:64` (deliberately UNCHANGED). **The prompts that asked for the refused kind:** `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-description.ts:67`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/prompt-framing.js:265-269`.
- Found during: the 2026-08-01 two-agent live run, analysed in `docs/NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §2 F1. Both machines ran 1.7.19 (server-stamped `appVersion`), so this was never "the fix is not installed".
- Severity: bug
- Description: `routeAddressedAgent` opened with a blanket kind refusal sitting in FRONT of all three lanes — addressed, thread and engaged — while the MCP tool description and this app's own spawn prompt both instruct every agent to log progress as `kind="task_progress"`. **The product asked for a kind the product then refused to deliver.** Across seq 340-368 of that run the correlation was 100% predictive in both directions: every undelivered post was a `task_started`/`task_progress`/`task_finished`, every delivered one was a `message`. Nine posts queued for the peer agent — three carrying `metadata.to_agent_ids` — started nothing, and both agents concluded the transport was broken. **A green suite of 1780 tests said nothing about it, because every desktop fixture constructs `kind: "message"`** (`test/helpers/channel-agents.mjs` `msg`).
- Resolution (2026-08-01, uncommitted): the blanket refusal is replaced by a two-way split under `MILESTONE_KINDS` (`task_started` / `task_progress` / `task_finished` / `task_failed`, restated from the server's `PostableMessageKindSchema`). A milestone is **not a turn**, so it gets only the two lanes somebody had to ASK for — **ADDRESSED** (`to_agent_ids` names one of my agents) and **IN THREAD** (it lands in a breakout room that agent participates in, same participant fence) — and **not the ENGAGED lane**: an unaddressed milestone in the main room reaches nobody, because milestone chatter is a stream, not a request. `'message'` behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged, and `system` / an unknown kind is still refused whole. **`targeting.classify` was deliberately NOT widened** (it still answers `'ignore'` at `targeting.js:64`), or every peer milestone would raise a consent card.
  - **The brakes were verified against this path rather than assumed, and all four hold because `engagement.humanAuthored` is FALSE for every non-`message` kind:** `myOwnAgentSpoke` (my own account's milestone never routes in the main room), `channel-deliver.selfEcho` (an agent's own `task_started`/`task_finished` never echoes into its own session — by `author_agent_id` when stamped, and for ALL of my agents when it is not), `selfAuthored` (a milestone is never gate-bypassed, so the inbound gate still holds every hop), and `noteAgentActed` (a milestone never slides the 60-minute engagement window). The auto-posture ping-pong bound of F-111 residual (h) is therefore unchanged.
- Tests: `dopl-desktop-app/test/channel-milestone-kinds.test.mjs` (NEW, 15 tests, the first fixtures in this tree that construct task_* kinds — **7 of the 15 fail against the pre-fix gate, verified by reverting it**) and live check **9** in `test/live/checks-room.js` (`an ADDRESSED kind="task_progress" is delivered`, with the unaddressed twin as its control — verified FAIL pre-fix / PASS post-fix against the real API). **Green: desktop 1797, `npx eslint .` 0 errors, `npm run test:live` 8 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped.**
- **§2 SPLIT, in the same change:** `test/live/checks.js` was 566 lines BEFORE this check and 650 after, so it was split on the registrar/sibling pattern — `checks.js` (65, the ordered CHECKS list and the surface `run.js` imports), `checks-room.js` (394, checks 1-5 and 9), `checks-thread.js` (142, checks 6-8), `checks-shared.js` (118, the verdict constructors and line formatters). No behaviour moved. **F-111 residual (m) still stands: the tier matches no eslint block, so nothing enforces that cap for it.**
- Residuals:
  - **(a) An addressed milestone to a DISMISSED row of mine still raises one passive notification per post — CLOSED 2026-08-01 by F-116 item 4** (a 5-minute per-`(channel, agent)` rate limit in `task-notify`, exactly the dedupe this residual predicted, landed the same day after the review round measured the multiplier).
  - **(b) A HUMAN-typed `task_*` post from the operator's own account routes nowhere**, because `humanAuthored` is kind-gated and `myOwnAgentSpoke` therefore reads it as "my agent spoke". Nothing types those today (the composer only posts `kind:"message"`), and the direction of the failure is closed rather than open, so it is recorded rather than fixed.
  - **(c) `session-dispatch.feedLiveSession` / `maybeOpenRequesterSession` / `maybeSurfaceRequesterReply` all still gate on `kind === 'message'`** (`session-dispatch.js:45,76,136`). Out of scope here and NOT obviously wrong — those are the PAIR (ASSIST) lanes, which have no agent identity and no participant fence — but they are the remaining places a milestone is invisible to.
- Cross-references: **F-111** (this is the fourth routing lane's kind axis; its residual (h) bound is preserved, (m) is unchanged). **`docs/NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §2** — F2 (no wire-level session identity), F6 (a closed thread still accepts posts) and F3 (deferred MCP schemas) are separate and still open; §3 decision 1 ("should a task_* post wake a peer session?") is what this entry implements.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — THE FIX ITSELF IS DELETED; ONLY (c) SURVIVES.** `main/channel-agents.js` is gone with rollback §1, and with it `routeAddressedAgent`, `MILESTONE_KINDS` and the two lanes this wave built. `listener-messages.js:50-54` records the removal where the fourth route used to run. The finding is kept rather than deleted because its REASONING is the load-bearing part — *the product asked for a kind the product then refused to deliver*, found by correlating 100% of undelivered posts against their `kind`, with a 1780-test green suite saying nothing because every fixture built `kind: "message"`.
  - ~~**(b) a human-typed `task_*` from the operator's own account routes nowhere**~~ — **OBSOLETE.** It described `humanAuthored` / `myOwnAgentSpoke`, both of which lived in the deleted module; nothing types those kinds anyway.
  - **(c) STILL REAL, and now the WHOLE rule rather than a lane-priority question.** `session-dispatch.js:111` (`feedLiveSession`), `:149` (`maybeOpenRequesterSession`), `:224` (`maybeSurfaceRequesterReply`) and `:322` (`maybeReopenAddressedThread`) all gate on `kind === 'message'`. The milestone lane that used to sit underneath them is deleted, so a milestone is now invisible to EVERY session route — see F-119 residual (c), which tracks the same fact.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done)
- Status: resolved — **(a) closed, (b) obsolete 2026-08-06, (c) still real and tracked jointly with F-119 (c)**

### F-113: One agent handle, several concurrent sessions, and nothing on the wire naming which one spoke (F2)
- Location: **server** — `src/shared/auth/session-header.ts` (NEW), `src/shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.ts`, `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/features/channels/server/service-shared.ts` + `service-writes-metadata.ts`; **client** — `packages/dopl-client/src/transport.ts`; **render** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-render.ts` (`sessionIdOf` + the line suffix); **desktop** — `dopl-desktop-app/main/sdk-loader.js` (`withSessionStamp`) + `main/session-query.js`.
- Found during: the 2026-08-01 two-agent live run, analysed in `docs/NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §2 F2. Two sessions posted as `flint` and gave `cobalt` contradictory instructions 79s apart; #363 confirmed it first-person ("I AM THE SECOND SESSION").
- Severity: bug
- Description: `channel_agents` holds ONE row per handle and `as_agent` is validated per CALL against ownership alone — the server checks that the caller OWNS the claimed agent and never which session is calling. On the desktop that is by design rather than a race: `session-store.slotKey` gives a ROOM slot `(channel, agent)` and a PAIR slot `(channel, thread)` DISJOINT key spaces, so three live sessions of one handle is documented behaviour (proven from the three distinct slot keys in #341/#344/#346's `client_msg_id`s). What was missing is anything on the WIRE naming a session, so `metadata` alone could not attribute #349/#350 vs #351-#356 and "flint said X" was not a well-formed statement.
- Resolution (2026-08-01, uncommitted): **a stamp, not a lock.** `session_id` joins the reserved-key set on EXACTLY the `runtime` / `appVersion` discipline — always stripped from caller metadata, re-stamped only from the request's `X-Dopl-Session-Id` (shape-checked in the auth layer, re-narrowed in `buildChannelContext`), absent header ⇒ NO KEY. The MCP route forwards the caller's header onto the loopback the same way it forwards `runtime`. The desktop stamps `store.slotKey(s)` onto the per-session in-memory MCP entry. `read` / `await` lines render ` · session <tag>`, taking the slot key's TAIL (the channel half is identical for every session in a room).
  - **Enforcing one live session per agent id was rejected**, per `NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §3 decision 5: it would break the legitimate three-slot design and does nothing for an external CLI session passing `as_agent`.
  - **The stamp is a hint, never an authorization signal.** Any device-token holder can send the header; nothing gates on it. Same framing as `runtime-header.ts` / `app-version-header.ts`, stated in the module docblock.
- Tests: `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata-session.test.ts` (NEW, 9 — header predicate, `buildChannelContext` re-narrow, stamp/strip, and TWO sessions of one handle stamping two values); `src/shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.test.ts` (+3, the header reaching the handler ctx); `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-thread-labels.test.ts` (NEW, the render half incl. a line-forgery check); `dopl-desktop-app/test/session-id-stamp.test.mjs` (NEW, 13 — the desktop half, see residual (b)).
- Residuals:
  - **(a) Only the SDK session path stamps.** `main/mcp-config.js`'s `spawnConfigBody` writes ONE shared spawn-config file for every headless `--mcp-config` spawn, so it cannot carry a per-session value; those posts stamp nothing (which reads as "unstamped", the correct degradation). The desktop's own `api.js` / `listener-io.js` lifecycle posts likewise carry no session stamp — their slot key is already inside the `client_msg_id`.
  - **(b) No desktop test pins the new header — CLOSED 2026-08-01 (uncommitted).** `dopl-desktop-app/test/session-id-stamp.test.mjs` (NEW, 13) pins the desktop half: the stamp landing beside the bearer / pin / runtime header, in-place mutation (the call site ignores the return value), NO KEY AT ALL for an absent or malformed slot, the 128-char boundary, the `buildSdkOptions` wiring (`withSessionStamp(options.mcpServers, store.slotKey(s))`, applied AFTER the builder literal the two older suites source-scan, both still untouched and passing), and that the per-session stamp never reaches the shared `mcp-spawn.json` (residual (a)). It is a TWO-SIDED pin: the regex and the header name are read out of `src/shared/auth/session-header.ts` as source and compared, because this server DROPS SILENTLY — a widened desktop regex or a mis-cased field name costs every future attribution line with nothing else failing. Slot-key shapes (pair / room / team-without-thread / `task-<channel>-<seq>` legacy) are asserted against the SERVER's own compiled regex.
    - One-line hardening in the same change: `withSessionStamp` now takes its entry defensively (`entry.headers = entry.headers || {}`, non-object entries skipped) instead of assuming `headers` exists. Unreachable today — `buildMcpServers` always ships one — but it runs inside `buildSdkOptions`, the ONE assembly point every spawn shape uses, so a `TypeError` there would kill a session for an attribution hint. A label must never break a launch.
  - **(c) The stamp names a SLOT, not a run.** Two sequential sessions in the same slot stamp the same value. That is what the incident needed (it distinguishes concurrent slots) but it is not a run id; do not read it as one.
- Cross-references: **F-110 / F-111** (`resolvePostMetadata`'s reserved-key set grows again here; residual (a) of F-111 — `to_user_notify` still unbuilt and still unstripped — is UNCHANGED and was deliberately not bundled). **F-104** (`CallerIdentity.runtime` is the sibling observation; this adds no new identity claim). **The `to_user_notify` clause above is now out of date: it was stripped on 2026-08-06 (F-151).**
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — STILL REAL, unchanged by the rollback.** The stamp is server-side and slot-keyed, so nothing the rollback deleted touched it. (a) holds exactly as written: `mcp-config.js:122` still builds ONE shared `spawnConfigBody` for every headless `--mcp-config` spawn, carrying `Authorization` and `X-Dopl-Runtime` and no session header, so those posts stamp nothing — which is still the correct degradation, not a gap. (c) holds by design: the stamp names a SLOT, and two sequential sessions in one slot stamp the same value. **Not fixed here** because a per-spawn stamp means a per-spawn config FILE — a new surface with its own lifetime and permissions, not a guard.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done)
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **(a) and (c) re-verified still real 2026-08-06**

### F-114: A closed thread accepted posts in silence, and the close result claimed a finality the server does not enforce (F6)
- Location: **server** — `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts` + `service-writes-metadata-thread.ts` (NEW, `isThreadClosed`), `service-writes.ts`, `src/features/channels/types.ts`, `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/messages/route.ts`; **client** — `packages/dopl-client/src/channel.ts` + `channel-types.ts`; **MCP** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-post-linkage.ts` (`closedThreadNote`), `channel-ops-write.ts`, `channel-ops-threads.ts`, `channel-description.ts`.
- Found during: the 2026-08-01 two-agent live run (`NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §2 F6). flint closed thread `79ce5325` at #355 and then posted #356, #361, #362, #363, #365 into it — no refusal, no warning.
- Severity: bug
- Description: the post path resolves the thread row and gates on MEMBERSHIP, never on STATUS. `status` / `closed_at` are written on close (`service-tasks.ts`) and cleared on reopen and read NOWHERE on the write path, while the MCP close result read "Closed thread &lt;title&gt; … as &lt;outcome&gt;." full stop — finality the server does not enforce.
- Resolution (2026-08-01, uncommitted): **WARN, DO NOT REFUSE** (`NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §3 decision 2). `resolvePostMetadata` now returns `{ metadata, threadClosed }`; `postMessage` attaches `threadClosed: true` to the returned message ONLY when true; the route answers `{ message, threadClosed }` — an ADDITIVE ENVELOPE KEY beside the message, the shape `echoSeq` already uses, so nothing about the stored row or a read changes. `@dopl/client` normalizes the absent key to `false`. The MCP `post` result then carries `closedThreadNote`, and the close result says closing stops the thread ROUTING rather than stops accepting. **Same-day review round (F-116 item 1): that routing claim is now ENFORCED on the desktop** — the passive thread lane refuses a closed thread (`channel-threads.threadIsClosed`), the addressed lane deliberately still delivers, and all three copy surfaces are scoped to exactly that.
  - **A hard 403 was rejected**: it breaks the legitimate "one last word after the close echo" pattern, and its only remedy — reopen — has NO MCP counterpart (`schema.ts`'s `reopen` op is web-only), so a refusal would point an agent at something it cannot do. The copy says outright that reopening is a human's action.
  - **An IDEMPOTENT REPLAY does not carry the flag**: `replayStoredMessage` re-resolves nothing, and re-reading a thread row to describe a write that already happened would be inventing it.
- Tests: `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata-closed.test.ts` (NEW, 6 — accepted-and-flagged, metadata unchanged, open/none/legacy/inherited all silent); `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-closed-thread.test.ts` (NEW, 6 — the close copy and the post warning; `opCloseThread`'s summary describe moved here from `channel-ops.test.ts` as the §2 split below).
- **§2 SPLITS, in the same change:** `service-writes-metadata.ts` was 461 and would have gone over with F2 + F6, so the thread half (`mayWriteThread`, `isLegacyThreadParticipant`, the new `isThreadClosed`) moved to `service-writes-metadata-thread.ts` (137) leaving the fold at 435. `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops.test.ts` hit 507 and the `close_thread` describe moved to `channel-closed-thread.test.ts`, leaving it at 437.
- Residuals:
  - **(a) `reopen` still has no MCP op.** The copy now names it as a human action, which is honest but is not the same as an agent being able to take it.
  - **(b) A LEGACY thread tag can never warn** — it has no `channel_tasks` row and therefore no status. That is correct (it is not a thread, see F-115) but it means an ad-hoc exchange has no "this is over" signal at all.
- Cross-references: **F-105** (nothing ever closes a thread; this makes closing MEAN something, and does not change how rarely it happens). **F-109** (`echoSeq` is the same additive-envelope discipline).
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — BOTH RESIDUALS STILL REAL, and (a) matters more now than it did.** (a) `reopen` is still web-only: `schema.ts:288` carries the `op:"reopen"` literal and no MCP op reaches it, so the copy that names reopening as a human's action remains honest and remains a dead end for an agent. That mattered less when nothing closed threads; **F-105's propose-then-confirm now means closes actually happen**, so an agent working a thread a human closed early has no way back in and no op to point at. (b) unchanged and still correct: a legacy `task-<channel>-<seq>` tag has no `channel_tasks` row, so it has no status to warn about — which is the same reason F-115 renders it `ad-hoc`. **Not fixed here:** adding an MCP `reopen` op is a new mutating surface with its own authorization question (the close is deliberately human-only — `ThreadCloseIsHumanOnlyError`), so the reopen's authority is a product call, not a guard.
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done)
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **(a) and (b) re-verified still real 2026-08-06; (a) is now the sharper of the two**

### F-115: A synthetic `task-<channel>-<seq>` id rendered as a real thread, and the MCP schema published neither its minimums nor the agent-ref cap (F4 + F5)
- Location: **MCP** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-render-threads.ts` (NEW), `channel-render.ts`, `channel-post-linkage.ts`, `channel-schema.ts`.
- Found during: the 2026-08-01 two-agent live run (`NEXT-SESSION-FIXES.md` §2 F4 and F5).
- Severity: papercut
- Description: **F4** — a receiving desktop mints `task-<channelId>-<seq>` for an UNTAGGED request so the reply groups with it on its own card (`main/trigger.js` `taskIdFor`, mirrored in `main/legacy-threads.js`). The mechanism is correct and load-bearing, but the id rendered IDENTICALLY to a real thread in `read` / `await` lines, in the legend, and in the post's linkage note — including the instruction "continue this thread with `thread=<id>`", which the server would then SILENTLY STRIP for a non-participant. 31 such tags exist in the one observed channel. **F5** — `body` / `client_msg_id` / `title` published a maximum and no minimum against the route's `.min(1)`, and `to_agent` / `to_agents` items published no bound at all against the route's 64, so four classes of input passed the tool and died at the route as an opaque 400.
- Resolution (2026-08-01, uncommitted): **the mechanism is untouched; only the LABEL changed.** Deterministic ids, the server-side strip and the desktop's UUID gate are all exactly as they were. A non-UUID tag now renders `· ad-hoc <tag>` on the message line, gets its OWN legend line saying what it is and pointing at `create_thread` (never at posting into it), and the post's linkage note says GROUPED into the ad-hoc exchange rather than THREADED. `shortRef` prints the trailing SEQ for a synthetic id — a blind `slice(0,8)` collapses every ad-hoc exchange in a channel onto the same `task-dba` prefix, which is the opposite of a distinguishing tag. F5 adds the three `.min(1)`s and the two `.max(64)`s; **`summary` is deliberately NOT split** (§3 decision 3 stands — one param, two routes, the looser cap declared so a close summary is never refused client-side).
  - **The desktop half of F4 is a NO-OP**: `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/**` renders no thread id to a user (only `preload.js` passes one as an argument), so there was nothing to relabel and no forbidden file needed touching.
- Tests: `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-thread-labels.test.ts` (NEW, 14 — `shortRef`, the line label, the split legend, the post linkage, plus the F2 session suffix); `channel-schema-caps.test.ts` (NEW, 7 — the minimums, the agent-ref cap, and a pin that `summary` stays at the looser 2000 and the agent refs stay untrimmed); `channel-narration.test.ts` (2 assertions re-pointed — the CONTAINMENT property is unchanged, the label is not).
- **§2 SPLIT, in the same change:** `channel-render.ts` was 467 and the thread-linkage render moved to `channel-render-threads.ts` (198), leaving it at 424.
- Residuals:
  - **(a) A fabricated NON-UUID tag that is not the synthetic shape also renders `ad-hoc`.** Correct — it has no thread row either — but the label does not distinguish "the desktop minted this" from "a peer typed this". The legend prints the id verbatim, which is the tell.
  - **(b) The web UI was not audited for the same label defect.** Out of scope for this pass; `src/features/channels/lib/group-thread.ts` is where a web-side equivalent would live.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — BOTH RESIDUALS STILL REAL.** (a) `channel-render-threads.ts:147` still labels every non-UUID tag `ad-hoc`, which is correct (none of them names a thread row) and still does not distinguish "the desktop minted this" from "a peer typed this" — the legend printing the id verbatim is still the only tell. (b) still unaudited: `src/features/channels/lib/group-thread.ts` is **819 lines** and is now the fastest-growing file in the tree (ENGINEERING §2), so the web-side label audit this residual names would land in the file least able to absorb it. **Not fixed here:** an audit is a pass, not a guard, and it should ride the split that file already owes §2.
- Cross-references: **F-083 bullet 3** (the legacy-id gate this label sits on top of — closed, unchanged here). **F-114** (a legacy tag can never carry the closed warning, for the same reason it is not a thread). **F-116 items 2 and 5** (same-day review round: the linkage note now branches on WHO chose the tag — a deliberately-passed legacy id is told to KEEP the tag, the `create_thread` nudge is reserved for the untagged case, the legend's "Do NOT pass one" order is gone, and a legacy-tailed session tag renders `pair <seq>` via `sessionSlotRef`).
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done)
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **(a) and (b) re-verified still real 2026-08-06 (F-151)**

### F-116: The adversarial review round over the F1–F7 wave — copy that overpromised, a lane the wave widened without its instruction, and a notification multiplier
- Location: **desktop** — `dopl-desktop-app/main/channel-threads.js` (`threadIsClosed`), `main/channel-deliver.js` / `session-gate.js` / `session-reducer.js` / `session-engine.js` / `session-seed.js` (the per-turn thread id), `main/task-notify.js` (dedupe); **MCP** — `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-threads.ts`, `channel-post-linkage.ts`, `channel-description.ts`, `channel-render.ts`, `channel-render-threads.ts` (`sessionSlotRef`).
- Found during: an opus edge-case review of the whole uncommitted F1–F7 wave, 2026-08-01, run before commit. Verdict was "ship desktop, hold web on the closed-thread copy"; everything HIGH/MEDIUM was fixed in the same session instead.
- Severity: bug (the closed-thread copy), papercut (the rest)
- Description and resolution (all 2026-08-01, uncommitted):
  1. **Nothing stopped routing on a closed thread, and the F-114 copy said it did.** Three surfaces claimed "no session is woken for a closed thread" while `channel-threads.fetchParticipants` consumed `task.participants` and never `task.status` — and F-112 had just widened the thread lane to milestones. Fixed on BOTH sides: `fetchParticipants` now returns the established `null` ("routes nobody") sentinel when `task.status` is a non-empty string other than `'open'` (unknown lifecycles fail closed; a status-LESS task — legacy server — routes as before), and the three copy surfaces are scoped to exactly what is true. **The ADDRESSED lane deliberately still delivers inside a closed thread** — an explicit address is an explicit ask — and the ENGAGED lane is pinned unchanged (it refuses every agent author and every non-message kind already). Lag bounds: a close is honoured within the participant cache TTL (≤5 min); a reopen recovers after `FAIL_BACKOFF_MS` (30 s), because the CLOSED verdict caches like a failed read, not forever.
  2. **The ad-hoc linkage copy contradicted the desktop's own THREAD_TAG order** on the primary untagged-request path: the desktop tells a responder to keep the legacy `task-<channel>-<seq>` tag on every post, while the new ad-hoc note told the same agent "NOT a thread … open one with create_thread" — following it forks the exchange. `threadLinkageNote` now branches three ways: caller PASSED this id (`askedThread === landedThread`) → the grouping worked, KEEP passing it; caller passed NOTHING → that is where the `create_thread` suggestion lives; mismatch → the existing mismatch note. `legendAdHoc`'s "Do NOT pass one" order is replaced with what passing one actually does.
  3. **Team/room sessions could never receive the F-112-widened thread lane's F7 read instruction** — `firstActions` gates on `ctx.taskId` and the team spawn context deliberately has none (the slot must stay `(channel, agent)`). The routed thread id now rides the DELIVERY TURN, not the slot: `channel-deliver` puts `targeting.firstClassTaskId(m)` on the gate queue ITEM, the reducer carries it as an inbound effect, and `session-seed.turnContext` fills the framing's `taskId` for that one turn. An Accept frames the HEAD's thread, not whatever queued behind it; a pair/ASSIST context that already names a thread always wins; a thread-less turn produces the byte-identical effect object it always did.
  4. **Dismissed-agent notification amplification** (F-112 residual (a), now CLOSED): pre-wave, a milestone addressed to a dismissed agent produced nothing; post-wave it produced one OS banner per post, at milestone volume. `task-notify` now rate-limits per `(channel, agent)` — 5-minute window, 50-entry LRU, no new imports (the pinned dependency set is re-asserted). A rate limit, not a mute: it fires again after the window, for another agent, and for the same agent in another channel.
  5. **A PAIR slot key with a legacy thread tail rendered `· session `seq 345``** — a thread helper (`shortRef`) reused on a session id, presenting a seq as a session identity. `sessionSlotRef` renders `pair <seq>` for the synthetic-tail case and delegates to `shortRef` otherwise.
  6. **Two docblocks still described the pre-F-083 write path** ("stored VERBATIM", gate "only inside isUuid"). Corrected — since F-083 the else branch runs `isLegacyThreadParticipant` and DELETES any non-matching tag, so the render-side neutralization is defense-in-depth for pre-F-083 rows, not the live path.
- Residuals (recorded by the review, deliberately NOT fixed):
  - **(a) Release-note item, not a code item:** milestone cadence now feeds the auto-posture loop. The F-111 ping-pong BOUND is unchanged; the ARRIVAL RATE feeding it is higher by design (§3 decision 1). Two agents on auto now drive each other at milestone cadence. Say it in the release note.
  - **(b) `· no thread` prints on a page whose only tags are ad-hoc** (`channel-render.ts` `anyThreaded` counts any tag). Cosmetic; rename to `· untagged` if touched.
  - **(c) `to_agents` items measure the 64 cap pre-trim client-side vs post-trim server-side** — unreachable (handles are 2–31 chars); recorded because it is the one F5 change whose failure direction is false-NEGATIVE.
  - **(d) `@dopl/client.postMessage` returns `{ threadClosed: false }` where it once returned `undefined` for a malformed empty 2xx body** — unreachable (transport throws on non-2xx first); noted against the docblock's strict-additivity claim.
  - **(e) `session_id` and `appVersion` never co-occur on one row.** The SDK/MCP lane sends no `X-Dopl-App-Version` and `/api/mcp` does not forward one; the desktop's REST lifecycle posts carry `appVersion` but no session stamp. So "which session" and "which build" cannot be joined on a single message — the exact pairing the 2026-08-01 incident forensics wanted. Cheap follow-up: forward the version header in `/api/mcp` beside `sessionId`, add it to `buildSdkOptions`' header set.
- Tests: `dopl-desktop-app/test/channel-thread-closed.test.mjs` (NEW, 14), `test/team-turn-thread-read.test.mjs` (NEW, 19), `test/task-notify-dismissed-burst.test.mjs` (NEW, 10); MCP-side pins moved/extended in `channel-closed-thread.test.ts`, `channel-thread-labels.test.ts` (+4), `channel-narration.test.ts`. **Suites at close of the whole wave: root 1905, mcp-server 513, desktop 1853, eslint 0 errors (1 baseline warning), tsc clean, dist rebuilt.**
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-06 (F-151) — ONE RESIDUAL OBSOLETE, THREE STILL REAL, ONE A RELEASE NOTE THAT NO LONGER APPLIES.** Note also that item 1's fix (`channel-threads.threadIsClosed`) is DELETED with `main/channel-threads.js` — the desktop no longer refuses a closed thread because it no longer has a passive thread lane at all. The server-side half (F-114) is untouched and still warns.
  - ~~**(a) release-note item: two agents on auto now drive each other at milestone cadence**~~ — **NO LONGER APPLICABLE.** The lane that delivered milestones is deleted; a milestone reaches no session route (F-112 (c)). The auto-posture bound itself is unchanged and is tracked as F-111 (h).
  - ~~**(c) `to_agents` items measure the 64 cap pre-trim client-side vs post-trim server-side**~~ — **OBSOLETE.** `toAgent` / `toAgents` are `z.never()` (`schema.ts:209`); there is no agent ref to cap.
  - **(b) STILL REAL** — `channel-render.ts:299` computes `anyThreaded` from any tag at all, so a page whose only tags are ad-hoc prints `· no thread`. Cosmetic; rename to `· untagged` if that file is touched.
  - **(d) STILL REAL** — `packages/dopl-client/src/channel.ts:188` still normalizes to `{ threadClosed: false }` where a malformed empty 2xx once returned `undefined`. Unreachable (the transport throws on non-2xx first); recorded against the docblock's strict-additivity claim, not as a defect.
  - **(e) STILL REAL, and the forensic join is still missing.** `/api/mcp/route.ts` forwards no `X-Dopl-App-Version`, and `sdk-loader.js` adds no version header beside `withSessionStamp`, so `session_id` and `appVersion` still never co-occur on one row. The cheap follow-up this residual names is unchanged and unstarted. **Not fixed here:** it adds a header to two surfaces and a stamp path, which is a wire change rather than a guard.
- Cross-references: **F-112** (residual (a) closed here; the thread lane this round gates is the one that wave widened), **F-113** (residual (e) above is its missing forensic join), **F-114** (the copy this round made true), **F-115** (the label copy this round un-contradicted).
- Proposed resolution: fix-now (done)
- Status: resolved (residuals noted) — **residual list corrected 2026-08-06 (F-151): (b), (d) and (e) are live; (a) and (c) no longer apply**

### F-117: One request, two windows — an addressed milestone inside a thread wakes a second session beside the thread's own
- Location: **desktop** — `main/channel-agents.js` (the F-112 addressed lane vs the pair/ASSIST trigger path), `main/session-store.js` (disjoint ROOM `(channel, agent)` / PAIR `(channel, thread)` slot key spaces, the documented F-113 design).
- Found during: Samuel's first live 1.7.20 cross-machine test, 2026-08-02 02:27Z, DM `dba90694`, thread `8527870d`, seq #416-#423. Diagnosed from prod rows — the new F-113 `session_id` stamps are what made the two windows attributable (`…:8527870d` pair vs `…:6979e939` room), doing on day one exactly what they were built for.
- Severity: papercut (UX), by-design at the routing layer
- Description: the peer opened a thread with a `message` addressed to the OPERATOR (#416 → classic trigger → consent → PAIR session, #420) and then posted a `task_progress` addressed to the operator's AGENT (#417 → F-112 addressed lane → the agent's ROOM/team session, #419). Two lanes, two disjoint slots, two windows — both answered the same exchange (#421 vs #422/#423). Every message routed exactly once; nothing double-delivered. But a peer who mixes addressing modes about one piece of work gets the operator two windows of duplicate effort, and it reads as a bug.
- Open product question (Samuel's call, not decided): should an addressed milestone that lands INSIDE a thread prefer the thread's existing/spawnable PAIR session over waking a ROOM session for the named agent ("thread session wins when it exists")? That priority rule would collapse this to one window. Costs: the named agent's identity is lost (the pair session is not that agent), and the rule adds cross-slot coupling the disjoint design deliberately avoids.
- Also confirmed in the same rows: F-116 residual (e) is real in prod — the SDK-lane posts carry `session_id` + `runtime` but no `appVersion`, while lifecycle posts carry `appVersion` but no `session_id`. The forensic join is still missing. **(That half is STILL TRUE as of 2026-08-06 and is tracked at F-116 (e); it is the only part of this entry that survives.)**
- **OBSOLETE 2026-08-06 (F-151) — THE SECOND WINDOW CANNOT OPEN, BECAUSE THE LANE THAT OPENED IT IS DELETED.** This finding is exactly two lanes racing over one exchange: the F-112 ADDRESSED lane in `main/channel-agents.js` waking the named agent's ROOM session, beside the classic trigger's PAIR session. `channel-agents.js` is deleted whole with rollback §1 (F-141) and `listener-messages.js:50-54` records its removal at the point it used to run — there is no addressed-agent lane, no ROOM slot producer, and no `to_agent_id` to address, so a peer mixing addressing modes now reaches exactly one route. **The open product question ("should a thread session win when it exists?") is therefore MOOT, not deferred** — there is no cross-slot priority left to decide, and Samuel does not owe this a call. Kept rather than deleted because the DIAGNOSIS is the durable part: the two windows were only attributable because F-113's `session_id` stamps existed, which is that stamp doing on day one the exact job it was built for.
- Status: **obsolete** (2026-08-06) — subject deleted with the channels rollback; the product question it was waiting on no longer exists

### F-118: ATTENDED HANDOFF — "Open in Claude Code" on the desktop consent card (built 2026-08-02, uncommitted)
- Location: **desktop only** — `main/attended-prompt.js` (NEW, the prefill template, ZERO peer bytes), `main/attended-handoff.js` (NEW, URL build + handler probe + fallback ladder), `renderer/session/session-attended-ui.js` (NEW), one-line hooks in `renderer/session/session-render.js` (`makeConsent`, try-wrapped) + `session.js`/`session.html`/`session.css`, `main/session-consent.js` (entry deliberately keeps NO display strings), `main/session-ipc.js` (`session:attended-handoff`, bound by `webContents` like every other decision).
- What it is: the pre-consent window gains "Open in Claude Code" between Accept and Deny. Click → `shell.openExternal('claude-cli://open?cwd=<channel dir>&q=<prompt>')` — the operator's OWN Claude Code opens with a prefilled, UNSUBMITTED prompt teaching a fresh session to join the thread via the Dopl MCP (ToolSearch first, connector self-diagnosis, scoped read, post, await re-arm). Enter is the consent gesture. Card resolves LOCALLY as handled-attended: no spawn, no server write, Accept stays live relabeled "Start a Dopl session instead". Fallback ladder: handler bundle missing / q > 5,000 encoded chars / unencodable / openExternal rejects → clipboard + notification (click re-copies). Deep link facts (researched + live-tested 2026-08-01): `claude-cli://open` documented, prefill-never-submit, per-invocation new terminal window, handler registers only after the user's first-ever interactive prompt, RCE history fixed in CLI 2.1.118 (hence: encodeURIComponent only, ids narrowed, no shell ever carries prompt text).
- THE INVARIANT (review round, BLOCKER B-1): **zero peer bytes in the prefill.** An attended session is the operator's personal Claude — full tool set, no Dopl containment — so the template interpolates ONLY three narrowed ids; peer/channel names were deleted after the reviewer demonstrated injection via a 48-char channel rename. The session learns real names from its own scoped `read`, inside the server's fenced output. `narrowId` is pinned byte-identical to `prompt-framing.idToken` by differential test.
- Review round (opus, NO-SHIP → fixed same session): B-1 above; H-1 the await instruction omitted `workspace` (fail-closed refusal for multi-workspace callers — and the test PINNED the defective shape); H-2 the attended button was missing from the card's terminal-state `lock()` (Accept→attended double-handled; Deny→attended answered a declined request; mid-flight race re-enabled a locked button). Plus L-1 (bodyText spelling in the no-body pins), L-2 (hook try/catch — a throw costs the third button only), L-4 (notification click re-copies).
- Residuals (recorded, deliberately NOT fixed — each a consequence of "resolve locally, decide nothing on the server"):
  - **(a) M-2, the sharpest:** a handed-off card holds one of the `MAX_WINDOWS` (6) slots indefinitely — consent windows are not evictable — so a day of handoffs can silently stop later desktop cards from raising at all. Cheap fix when wanted: auto-park the consent window shortly after a successful handoff.
  - **(b) M-1:** after a handoff, Deny still reads "Deny" and still posts "Request declined" into the thread the attended session is answering.
  - **(c) M-3:** attended state is renderer-local; a window reload resurrects an enabled button, and a second click opens a second terminal (the platform has no deep-link dedupe).
  - **(d) M-4:** the web pending list and the OS notification still show a live Allow — the operator's own second surface can spawn a duplicate Dopl session against the attended thread.
  - **(e)** Later addressed messages still raise their own consent cards while an attended session runs; its `await` picks them up regardless.
- Tests: `test/attended-prompt.test.mjs` (20), `test/attended-handoff.test.mjs` (25), `test/attended-consent-dom.test.mjs` (20) — incl. the B-1 19-field peer-byte sweep, the H-1 all-ops-carry-both-ids pin, the H-2 lock matrix, the fallback truth table, and the narrowId≡idToken differential. Suites at close: desktop **1918**, eslint 0 errors; `session-render.js` 496/500, `session.js` 499/500 (both flagged split candidates).
- Spec: scratchpad ATTENDED-HANDOFF-SPEC.md (session-local). v1 scope-cuts by design: receiver-side only (no sender "attended" flag), no server/MCP changes, no claude:// desktop-chat path, no direct-spawn rung.
- **2026-08-02 LIVE INCIDENT + FIX (shipped 1.7.21, fixed for 1.7.22):** the first real click silently no-opped. Root cause, measured by bisection: **the claude-cli:// handler silently drops any URL over 4,096 TOTAL characters** (4,096 delivers, 4,097 vanishes; `openExternal` resolves either way, so there is no error to catch) — the documented 5,000-char `q` cap is unreachable. The shipped prompt encoded to ~4,057, + scheme + cwd = over. Fix: route on the BUILT URL against `URL_MAX_CHARS = 4096`, template compressed to ~3,383 encoded (worst-case 3,607, pinned at 3,650 with a cross-file test against the constant), over-budget → clipboard.
- **2026-08-02 APP ROUTE ADDED (operator's ask: "native Claude Code, not a terminal").** New PRIMARY rung: `claude://code/new?q=<compact>&folder=<dir>` opens the Claude Code DESKTOP APP with the prompt unsent in the composer — live-verified on Claude.app 1.24012.9, but UNDOCUMENTED (may change between app versions; ladder degrades, never breaks). That scheme TRUNCATES params at 1,024 (worse than dropping — half a procedure still looks whole), so both `q` and `folder` are pre-flighted and a second `buildAttendedPromptCompact` template exists for this rung: same three-narrowed-ids-only signature, same ZERO PEER BYTES invariant, all six semantic elements telegraphic (907 encoded real-world, 991 worst-case, pinned ≤1,000). Ladder: app → terminal deep link (full template, 4,096 bound) → clipboard (full template). Suites: desktop **1953**, eslint 0.
- Status: built + reviewed + fixed + app route added, uncommitted; ships as 1.7.22

### F-119: THE POSTURE WAVE — controls that were wired to nothing, a timer that wiped live postures, and the transparency + model + requester-shell slice built on the fixes (2026-08-02, uncommitted, ships as 1.7.23)
- Location: **desktop only.** New modules: `main/session-model.js`, `main/session-gate-reason.js`, `main/session-grant-keys.js` (§2 split of session-profiles), `renderer/session/session-modes-ui.js`, `session-request-ui.js`, `session-outbound-vm.js` (§2 split of the view-model). Touched: session-consent/ipc/engine/reducer/effects/team/park/dispatch/listener-messages/targeting/session-query/io/seed/store/state/replay, renderer session.js/html/css/preload/viewmodel/labels/render.
- Found during: operator-reported "bypass doesn't stick / Accept reverts my settings" (long-standing), root-caused by a read-only opus investigation, then four sequential builders + adversarial review + fix round.
- ROOT CAUSES (investigation, all verified): (1) the pop-out's posture selects were LIVE on windows with no session bound — the IPC refused via the live-session-only resolver and the preload DISCARDED the refusal, so pre-consent choices went nowhere and the spawn's manual/ask repaint read as a "revert"; the only working arm was the WEB card's channel-keyed single-use preset; (2) the 15-minute idle timer armed at TURN END only, so an open Allow card or long turn got parked mid-work, silently resetting both axes + grants; (3) a pending card is (correctly) never re-evaluated on a mode switch, but nothing said so; (4) `bypass` is a positive allow-list — unlisted names ask forever, and `BashOutput`/`KillShell` were hard-denied outright (background Bash unusable); (5) auto-send gates slug-addressed posts and non-post ops by design, unexplained. Investigation's one wrong assumption — that the SDK's `result.usage` could feed a context meter — was caught by builder C: that field is SESSION-CUMULATIVE; the meter reads the raw stream and a tripwire test fails anything that ever touches `result.usage`.
- WHAT SHIPPED: consent-ENTRY-scoped posture arm (`armModes`/`takeStartModes`) with an IPC fallback (live session → consent entry → refuse) and honest controls (`session-modes-ui.js` reverts on any refusal shape); idle timer re-arms on permission_request/decision/steer/feed/inbound-accept and a park that resets posture SAYS SO in-window; `operatorArmed` opens the parkedShell gate for the adopting launch; gate verdicts carry a closed reason-code enum (`grantDecisionDetail` — `grantDecision` itself byte-identical, proven by extraction diff) rendered on card + dock; BashOutput/KillShell follow Bash on the tool axis under `full` only; BYPASS_READS adds NotebookRead/ListMcpResources/ReadMcpResource; per-verdict + per-mode-change diag; PER-SESSION MODEL PICKER (frozen enum default/opus/sonnet/haiku/fable, coerced at five boundaries — the value becomes child argv — live `setModel`, survives park, durable-record round-trip, no silent fallbackModel); CONTEXT METER ("412k / 1M (41%)", amber ≥75% red ≥90%, frozen window map with [1m]-suffix-wins + date-strip, unknown model → tokens only, never a made-up percentage); ~~REQUESTER SHELL — a human-typed request from the app's own web view (unstamped + authorKind user + no author_agent_id, UX routing not a security gate) auto-opens a PINNED read_only shell (never starts the agent — open-session-no-query pins) with a display-only Sent → Accepted / Declined → Replied strip~~ **— SUPERSEDED BY F-140 (2026-08-05, rollback §3.4). The shell, its predicate (`requesterShellOpen`), its listener route (`maybeOpenRequesterShell`) and its park entry point (`openRequesterShell`) are DELETED, not disabled: the operator's typed request now opens the SAME full requester session a spawned session's create opens, because `main/ui-bridge.js` stamps `desktop-ui` and the evidence is server-side instead of a caller-asserted author kind. `test/operator-typed-request.test.mjs` asserts the predicate does not exist. THE STATUS STRIP SURVIVED THE SHELL and is now armed for `desktop-ui` only — see F-140.**; Q3b pinned in all three directions. Root cause of the requester gap: `targeting.requesterTaskOpen`'s runtime-stamp conjunct classified the operator's own typed create as external (the Q3b fix over-suppressed).
- REVIEW ROUND (opus, NO-SHIP → fixed same session, mutation-verified): **BLOCKER** — the consent arm was keyed to the (channel, thread) SLOT and consumed unconditionally by EVERY spawn shape, so a peer-driven parked-shell wake racing a pending armed card started at bypass/auto_both while the real Accept spawned manual/ask; fixed with `adoptsConsent` threaded from `launch()`'s own adopt test (single-setter pinned), same gate on the model arm. M1 prototype-key leak in `gateReasonText` (own-property guard; junk lists now include constructor/toString/__proto__ etc). L1 `modeChange` no longer reports {ok:false} for a drain throw after the dispatch landed. L2 a running query with no `setModel` answers `{deferred:true}` + a pending echo instead of claiming a live switch.
- Residuals (recorded, not fixed; **assessed individually 2026-08-05 under F-146 — two are MOOT, the rest are still live debt**): ~~(a) an LRU-evicted requester shell recreated by a late reply loses its status strip (needs a durable-record field)~~ **MOOT — there is no requester shell (F-140); the strip now rides a full session.**; (b) `task_finished` / non-declined `task_failed` leave the strip unchanged — v1 says nothing rather than the wrong thing **[STILL LIVE]**; (c) F-112 residual (c) untouched — session-dispatch's pair lanes still gate kind === 'message' **[STILL LIVE, and now the ONLY gate: the milestone lane F-112 built underneath it is deleted with `channel-agents.js`, so this is no longer a "lane priority" question but the whole rule — see F-146]**; ~~(d) FIX 4's team arm has NO PRODUCER yet (`wakeTeamSession` carries neither field; plumbing correct and inert — the consent arm is the only operatorArmed producer)~~ **MOOT — `session-team.js` is deleted (F-141) and `wakeTeamSession` with it; the arm it fed cannot be reached. The consent arm remains the only `operatorArmed` producer, which is now the whole picture rather than a gap.**; (e) BYPASS_READS' MCP read tools reach ANY configured server under `full`, reads only; (f) the model context-window table does not cover every id the CLI can report (`-fast`, `-v1` forms → tokens-only display, fail-safe); (g) each typed request opens a real window — self-inflicted only, evictable, N rapid requests = N windows; (h) route (4) short-circuits `classify`, so the operator's own create loses its `diag('msg')` line (matches route (2) precedent).
- Suites at close: desktop **2148**, eslint 0 errors; §2 sizes moved: session-profiles.js 500→354 (grant-keys split), session-io.js 489, session.js AND session-render.js at exactly 500 (zero headroom — next editor must split), session-engine.js 499, session-viewmodel.js 497.
- **RESIDUALS RE-VERIFIED INDIVIDUALLY 2026-08-06 (F-151) — five live, one now redundant.** This is the third assessment of the same list ((a) and (d) went MOOT on 2026-08-05; F-146 then re-recorded the rest as "still live debt" without re-reading them). Read off disk this time:
  - **(b) STILL LIVE.** `session-dispatch.js:269` — `REQUEST_MILESTONES` is `{ task_started: 'accepted', task_failed: 'declined' }`, and the comment above it states the reasoning in its own words: a `task_failed` with no `declined` flag is a real error, not a decline, and v1 has no word for it, so the strip holds rather than say the wrong thing. `task_finished` is still absent entirely.
  - **(c) STILL LIVE.** Four gates now, not three: `session-dispatch.js:111`, `:149`, `:224` and `:322` (the F-120 reopen route added the fourth). Tracked jointly with F-112 (c).
  - **(e) STILL LIVE.** `session-profiles.js:341-347` — `BYPASS_READS` is `NotebookRead` / `ListMcpResources` / `ReadMcpResource`, folded into `BYPASS_TOOLS`, so under `full` those reach any configured server, reads only.
  - **(f) STILL LIVE.** `session-model.js:92-104` — `contextWindowFor` handles the `[1m]` suffix and a date strip and then falls through to a tokens-only display for anything else, so `-fast` / `-v1` forms still land on the fail-safe rather than a number.
  - **(g) STILL LIVE.** Each typed request still opens a real window; self-inflicted, evictable, N requests = N windows.
  - ~~**(h) route (4) short-circuits `classify`, so the operator's own create loses its `diag('msg')` line**~~ — **NO LONGER A DISTINCT RESIDUAL.** Route (4) is deleted (F-140); the operator's typed create is now claimed by ROUTE (2), which is pre-classify for the same reason and short-circuits the same way. So the missing diag line still happens — but it is now literally the "matches route (2) precedent" this residual pointed at, rather than a second instance of it. Nothing to fix separately; if the diag matters, it is one line in `dispatchMessage` ahead of the routes.
- Cross-references: F-112 (the lanes the requester strip observes), F-113 (the stamp the requester predicate reads), ~~F-117 (still open — lane priority)~~ **F-117 is OBSOLETE as of 2026-08-06 — the lane it needed was deleted**, F-118 (attended handoff; its consent-window binding pattern is what the arm handlers reuse).
- Status: resolved (residuals noted); ships as 1.7.23. **Residuals re-verified 2026-08-06 (F-151): (b), (c), (e), (f), (g) live; (a), (d) moot; (h) subsumed.**

### F-120: A peer's follow-up to an answered exchange raised a second consent window instead of reopening the first (2026-08-02, uncommitted, ships as 1.7.24)
- Location: **desktop** — `main/listener-messages.js` (the `verdict === 'trigger'` branch now consults route (6) first), `main/session-dispatch.js` (`maybeReopenAddressedThread` + `exchangeTag` + `reopenableRecord`, all in the PURE block).
- Found during: Samuel's live Claude-desktop DM test on 1.7.23 (seq #440-#443, DM `dba90694`): the pair session answered #440 and settled on its own `task_finished`; the peer's correctly-tagged follow-up #443 fell through `classify → trigger` into a brand-new consent window beside the exchange it was answering. The legacy tag was doubly invisible — `firstClassTaskId` is UUID-gated (correctly) and the 1.7.15 task-scoped reopen was wired only requester-side.
- Resolution: **route (6), the one POST-classify route.** Classify's verdict was never wrong (it IS a request addressed to me wanting a decision) — only WHERE the decision was asked. At the verdict→action boundary, an addressed `message` carrying an exchange tag (first-class UUID or legacy `task-<thisChannel>-<seq>`, re-derived byte-equal via `targeting.legacyThreadId`, never pattern-matched — the channel-inline id IS the cross-channel fence) with a reopenable durable record — this channel, this tag, NO agentId (team slots share the key space and an agent id is UUID-shaped), stored counterparty === the message's author — is handed to `sessionEngine.feedInboundForTask`: live session fed directly, settled one recreated via the parked-shell machinery (fail-restrictive profile, budget + evict, agent NOT started) with the message held on the IN-WINDOW inbound gate. No consent row: the gate IS the consent surface, the operator's Messages posture decides. Every miss (no tag, foreign tag, no/expired record, wrong author, budget) answers false and today's fresh-consent path runs byte-for-byte. Also fixed the LIVE form: a legacy-tagged follow-up during a still-running pair session used to open a consent card on a different slot.
- Post-classify placement buys the no-collateral property by construction: task-reply/fyi/agent-escalation/chat/ignore are dispatched before the route can run; Q3b directions are self-authored so classify ignores them first. A recreate is NOT an adoption — the route never touches the consent-entry arm (`adoptsConsent` single-setter pin stays green).
- Tests: `test/thread-followup-reopen.test.mjs` + `test/thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs` (26, harness composing FOUR real blocks — dispatch, gate, park, plus real targeting/io/reducer), mutation-verified: seam deletion fails 8, counterparty conjunct 2, channel fence 2. Suites: desktop **2175**, eslint 0.
- Residuals: (a) F13 held-queue is memory-only — an app restart mid-hold loses the card and the cursor has moved (pre-existing, one more path reaches it); (b) F16 peer's web card says "Working…" while the gate holds (needs server lifecycle state); (c) group-channel `agent-escalation` classification still precedes this route — a peer's AGENT addressing me in a non-DM never reaches it (existing behavior, product call).
- Status: resolved (residuals noted); ships as 1.7.24

### F-122: Phase 2 scaffold — `apps/desktop-ui` + the Electron SPA seam (2026-08-02, uncommitted, branch `desktop-migration`)
- Location: **new** `apps/desktop-ui/**` (Vite + React 19 + TS SPA, npm workspace `@dopl/desktop-ui`), **new** `dopl-desktop-app/{main/spa-window.js, main/ui-bridge.js, renderer/app-preload.js, WIRING.md}`, **new** `src/shared/api/query-defaults.ts`. Touched: `src/shared/api/query-provider.tsx` (imports the extracted defaults), root `package.json` (workspaces `apps/*`, `dev:ui`/`build:ui`), root `tsconfig.json` (excludes `apps`), `eslint.config.mjs` (500-line cap extended to `apps/*/src`), `dopl-desktop-app/.gitignore` (`renderer/app/`).
- Scope: the foundation every page port copies — route table, transport, query client, test harness, conventions. **No page is ported and nothing is wired**: `main/index.js` still creates the remote main window, so the running app is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- Decisions worth knowing: (a) **hash router**, because the packaged renderer is a `file://` document where `history.pushState` to a non-existent path is a Chromium security error — one router for dev and prod; (b) `apiRequest` is a faithful port of `src/shared/api/api-client.ts` with a **pluggable transport** (`window.dopl.apiRequest` → IPC, else `fetch` + `VITE_API_BASE_URL`) and the `{ error: { code, message, details } }` envelope decoded in exactly ONE place so the two transports cannot disagree; (c) the strict production CSP (`default-src 'none'`, **no `connect-src`**) is injected at BUILD time by a Vite plugin, so the dev server keeps HMR and the shipped page keeps the session-window's policy; (d) the preload exposes four members and **no header control** — main builds every header including Authorization; (e) TanStack defaults are EXTRACTED (`query-defaults.ts`) and imported by both apps through an **exact-match** `@web/query-defaults` alias — a directory alias would let `"use client"`/next-coupled modules leak into the renderer.
- **F-074 grew a third copy.** `apps/desktop-ui/src/styles/{tokens.css,kit.css}` are hand-copies of `src/app/globals.css`'s token layers + `@layer components` kit, joining `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/session/tokens.css`. Loud headers name the source ranges and the drift risk in all of them. The close condition is unchanged and now worth more: one file imported at build time by web + SPA + session window. One intentional divergence, commented in place: `--font-mono` drops the `next/font` variable (an undefined var with no fallback poisons the declaration) for a system stack.
- Open seams for the next agents: `ui-bridge.getBearerToken()` / `getAuthState()` are stubs (auth track owns them; `broadcastAuthState(win, state)` is the push channel); ~~`img-src 'self' data: blob:` blocks remote avatars — proxy through main or add the exact origin, never widen to `https:`~~ (CLOSED by **F-124**: the main-side `data:` proxy behind `main/avatar-policy.js`'s destination allowlist; the "never widen to `https:`" half stands permanently); IPC request cancellation is best-effort (abort rejects the renderer promise, main's request completes); `ui-bridge`'s authenticated fetch and `main/api.js`'s cookie `apiFetch` should converge on ONE helper once the bearer branch lands; the app shell (AppShell → TourProvider → MyAccessProvider …) and workspace/canonical-segment resolution are stubbed placeholders.
- Verification: `apps/desktop-ui` — `tsc --noEmit` clean, vitest **9** passing (real route table under the real provider stack + the error-envelope cases), `npm run build` emits `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/app/index.html` with the CSP meta. Desktop — `node --check` clean on all three new `.js` files, eslint 0, suite **2175** unchanged. Web — vitest **1966** (120 files) unchanged, root `tsc --noEmit` clean (only the pre-existing stale `.next/types` noise), eslint 0 errors. Also load-verified in a real Electron window: the built SPA paints under `file://` with the strict CSP, zero console errors, `window.dopl` exposing exactly the four members, and the IPC round-trip against a local stub API returning 200/409/204 shapes while refusing a non-`/api` path, a protocol-relative path, and a non-UUID workspace id.
- Status: scaffold complete, unwired, uncommitted. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-123: Duplication consolidation across the desktop-migration seam — two API clients, a forked query hook, two workspace-resolution abstractions, two settings surfaces (2026-08-03, uncommitted)
- Location: **new** `src/shared/api/api-envelope.ts`, `src/shared/hooks/use-api-query-core.ts`, `src/shared/lib/open-external.ts`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/lib/{open-in-browser.ts,workspace-cache.ts}`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/hooks/use-workspace-access.ts`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/test-utils/bridge.tsx`. Touched: `src/shared/api/{api-client.ts,query-provider.tsx,idb-persister.ts}`, `src/shared/hooks/use-api-query.ts`, `src/shared/layout/settings-modal/sections/workspace-section-core.tsx`, `src/features/channels/components/channels-onboarding-core.tsx`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/lib/{api.ts,dopl-bridge.ts}`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/hooks/use-api-query.ts`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/{page-states.tsx,settings-modal/*}`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/pages/**` (7 page call sites + boot + settings), and 12 SPA test files.
- Found during: the 2026-08-03 fleet audit's duplication-quality track. Behavior-preserving throughout — no user-visible change was intended or made.
- **(a) Two API clients → one contract, two transports.** `ApiError`, `ApiRequestOpts`, `withQuery` and the `{error:{code,message,details}}` + flat plan-gate decoder now live in `src/shared/api/api-envelope.ts` (framework-free), imported by the web client and re-exported by `#/lib/api`. Kills the instanceof-incompatible `ApiError` pair that shipped in one SPA bundle (a 401 from a reused feature client could not be recognised by the SPA's `isUnauthorized`) and the verbatim decoder copy. Transports stay separate on purpose — the SPA's keeps `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and abort-reason semantics for browser dev.
- **(b) `useApiQuery` byte-fork → one core.** `useApiQueryWith(request, path, opts)` in `src/shared/hooks/use-api-query-core.ts`; both apps are a three-line binding over their own `apiRequest`. The `[path, workspaceId, query]` key contract and the TanStack-v5 "refetch ignores enabled" workaround now have one home.
- **(c) `useWorkspaceAccess` collapsed onto the shell's resolution.** It no longer runs its own `/api/workspaces/resolve`; it reads `useWorkspaceRoute()` and adds only `/api/workspaces/me`. Byte-identical-cache-key discipline across three files is now structural instead of conventional. Moved `pages/skills/use-workspace-access.ts` → `hooks/use-workspace-access.ts` and dropped its `segment` argument (every caller passed the router param it now reads itself). **Not fully sunset**: the planned `WorkspaceRouteContext` collapse still wants a shell change, which this pass did not own.
- **(d) `/settings` page and settings modal → one composition.** `WorkspaceSectionBody` (form + RemoteConnect + owner danger zone, with an `extras` slot) is exported from `workspace-section-core.tsx`; `WorkspaceSectionCore` wraps it in the modal's `SectionShell`, the page renders it directly under its own header and hangs connected-apps + trash off `extras`. Cache invalidation is one `invalidateWorkspaceReads(queryClient, segment)` in `#/lib/workspace-cache`, replacing two copies that had already drifted; `workspaceReadPath()` makes the "both surfaces share one cache entry" claim structural.
- Lows in the same pass: the two `isUnauthorized` helpers unified on the shared 401-only predicate (the boot-local `||403` branch was dead code — the desktop SPA authenticates with a session JWT and the workspace routes collapse denial to 404 — and the decision is now documented at the definition); three open-in-external-browser implementations → `openExternalUrl`/`openExternalPath` in `src/shared/lib/open-external.ts`, with `open-in-browser.ts` moved out of `components/settings-modal/` into `lib/` (burying it there is why the signed-out screen grew a copy); the dead `isDesktop()` export deleted from `dopl-bridge.ts` (it answered the opposite of `desktop.ts`'s `isDesktopApp()` inside the SPA); 11 hand-rolled `window.dopl` stubs across 9 files → `installBridge` + canonical `SEGMENT`/`WORKSPACE_ID`/`WORKSPACE`/`resolveBody`/`meBody` + `renderWithProviders` in `apps/desktop-ui/src/test-utils/bridge.tsx`.
- **Persisted-cache buster (web only).** `persistOptions` now carries `buster: QUERY_CACHE_BUSTER` from `idb-persister.ts` — `"<schema>:<build>"`, where the build comes from `NEXT_PUBLIC_QUERY_CACHE_BUSTER` / Vercel's auto-exposed `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_DEPLOYMENT_ID` / commit sha, falling back to `"dev"` locally. A deploy that reshapes any response can no longer hydrate a 24h-old snapshot in the old shape.
- Verification: root `tsc` clean, root vitest **1969** (120 files); SPA `tsc` clean, SPA vitest **104** (20 files); `npm run build -w @dopl/desktop-ui` and `next build` both clean.
- Residual (NOT introduced here, and out of this pass's scope): `apps/desktop-ui/src/components/app-shell/app-shell.test.tsx` > "rewrites a stale segment to the canonical one, keeping the page" is **intermittently failing** (~1 in 3 full-suite runs). Reproduced running only `src/components/app-shell` + `src/routes.test.tsx`, i.e. with zero files from this pass in the run — a load-sensitive `waitFor` on the resolve → needsRedirect → navigate chain.
- Status: resolved (residual noted); uncommitted. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-124: OAuth avatars unrenderable in the packaged SPA — main-side `data:` proxy behind a destination allowlist (2026-08-03, uncommitted)
- Location: **new** `dopl-desktop-app/main/avatar-policy.js`, `dopl-desktop-app/test/avatar-bridge-policy.test.mjs`, `src/shared/hooks/use-bridged-image-src.ts`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/hooks/use-bridged-image-src.test.tsx`. Touched: `dopl-desktop-app/main/ui-bridge.js`, `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/app-preload.js`, `apps/desktop-ui/src/lib/dopl-bridge.ts`, `apps/desktop-ui/vite.config.ts` (comment), `src/shared/lib/spa-bridge.ts`, `src/shared/ui/{avatar.tsx,avatar-stack.tsx}`, `src/shared/layout/{workspace-avatar.tsx,app-shell/app-rail-core.tsx,app-shell/workspace-switcher-core.tsx,settings-modal/sections/account-section-core.tsx}`.
- Found during: the packaged-SPA avatar bug — every member avatar rendered as initials. `profiles.avatar_url` is copied from the OAuth provider's `raw_user_meta_data`, so it resolves to `lh3.googleusercontent.com` / `avatars.githubusercontent.com`; the packaged CSP is `img-src 'self' data: blob: <supabase-storage>` and rightly refused them. This was the "OPEN SEAM" already documented in `apps/desktop-ui/vite.config.ts` and in the Phase-2 open-seams list at the end of this file.
- Severity: bug (feature-blocking, plus a would-be security hole if "fixed" by widening `img-src`).
- **The fix.** Main fetches the image and hands back a `data:` URI, which the CSP already allows. The fetch itself is NOT new: `main/avatar-cache.js` has done exactly this for the session window since v2.2 (https-only, `redirect:'error'`, raster `image/*` only, declared+actual ≤256KB, 4s abort, SSRF host guard, bounded positive+negative memo). It is reused verbatim — **its caching semantics are the point**: a URL is downloaded at most once per app run, so N members sharing an avatar cost one GET and a remount costs none.
- **The new part is the destination allowlist** (`main/avatar-policy.js`, sliceable `AVATAR-POLICY-PURE` block). `avatar-cache`'s `isSafeAvatarHost` is an SSRF guard — it admits every public host, which is right for an input that is a stored `avatar_url` and WRONG for an IPC handler the renderer calls with an arbitrary string (that would be the renderer's arbitrary outbound GET, the exfiltration leg out of a `connect-src 'none'` document, exactly what `ui-bridge`'s `EXTERNAL_HOST_ALLOWLIST` exists to close for `openExternal`). Policy, fail-closed: https only; no credentialed forms; hosts = `lh3.googleusercontent.com`, `avatars.githubusercontent.com`, `/^lh\d+\.googleusercontent\.com$/`, and the Supabase storage origin from `main/config.js`. **Deliberately NOT `*.googleusercontent.com`** — that is Google's general user-content host (Drive, Blogger, arbitrary uploads) and would admit attacker-chosen bytes on a Google origin. Everything else answers `null`. The answer is re-checked at the IPC boundary (`data:image/` prefix + ≤512KB), so the only thing the renderer can set as `img.src` is a bounded image; `avatar-cache`'s tighter 256KB/350KB caps still do the real work.
- **The handler** is `dopl:avatar`, registered through `ui-bridge`'s `bound(...)` wrapper like every other one (SPA window top frame only). It returns `null` rather than throwing: a refusal is not an error the page can act on, and every caller already renders initials for a missing image. Nothing about WHY crosses IPC. Preload adds ONE member, `avatarDataUri(url)`, string-coerced; the two preload invariants (no tokens, no caller-supplied headers) are unchanged and now pinned by a test.
- **The renderer half is ONE hook**, `@/shared/hooks/use-bridged-image-src`, in the web tree so both apps share it. Web (no SPA bridge, incl. SSR and the LEGACY wrapper's partial `window.dopl`) → the input is returned verbatim with no request and no state change, so web rendering is byte-identical. In the SPA, a CSP-permitted URL (relative/`data:`/`blob:`/same-origin/`*.supabase.co`) is likewise passed through; only a foreign http(s) URL goes to main, `undefined` while pending and `undefined` forever if refused — the state every caller already renders initials for, so no call site grew a branch. A module-level memo + in-flight dedupe means many members sharing an avatar produce ONE IPC call and a remount repaints synchronously.
- Converted consumers: `Avatar`, `AvatarStack`'s inner avatar (presence chips), the account section's profile picture, `WorkspaceAvatar`, the app rail's tile glyph (extracted to `RailTileGlyph` — a hook cannot be called inside the `.map()`), and `WorkspaceSwitcherCore`'s `WorkspaceGlyph`. The last three are Supabase-hosted and therefore passthrough today; they ride the shared resolver so no icon surface is special-cased and a future icon host does not silently render blank.
- Related: **F-09x** proposed "a host allowlist (googleusercontent.com + the Supabase storage host)" as one possible hardening for the avatar-cache SSRF residual. That allowlist now exists for the SPA path only — `main/avatar-cache.js`'s own callers (the session window, `resolveForSession`) are unchanged and still rely on `isSafeAvatarHost` alone, so F-09x's DNS-rebind residual stands as written.
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2292** green (10 new); SPA `tsc` clean, `vitest --no-file-parallelism` **115** in 21 files green (11 new), `npm run build -w @dopl/desktop-ui` clean; root `tsc --noEmit` clean; root vitest **1969** in 120 files green (unchanged from the F-123 baseline); `npx eslint` clean on every touched file in both trees.
- Status: resolved; uncommitted. KB sync not performed by this pass.

### F-125: The minimum-version gate — Phase 4's forced-upgrade prerequisite, and the four ways it could not leave a state (2026-08-03, branch `min-version-gate`)
- Location: **new** `src/shared/version/desktop-floor.ts` (+test), `src/app/api/version/route.ts` (+test), `dopl-desktop-app/main/{min-version.js,version-gate.js,update-required-window.js,wake.js}`, `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/update-required.{html,-preload.js}`, `dopl-desktop-app/test/{min-version,version-gate,update-required-screen}.test.mjs`. Touched: `src/proxy.ts` (+test), `src/shared/auth/app-version-header.ts` (comment only), `dopl-desktop-app/main/{config.js,index.js,shell-mode.js,tray.js,updater.js}`, `dopl-desktop-app/test/update-restart-prompt.test.mjs`.
- Found during: building the Phase-4 risk-register item ("users stuck on old shell after cutover"), then auditing the first three commits of it as an unfinished PR.
- Severity: bug (the 401 below was feature-killing), plus the design record for a net-new subsystem.
- **The design is in ENGINEERING.md** ("Desktop app — THE MINIMUM-VERSION GATE"): env-var floor read per request, `GET /api/version`, pure policy core + electron shell, fail-open on every failure, two independent anti-brick guards, one enforcement branch in the one shell factory, `DOPL_VERSION_GATE` as the dev knob. Not repeated here.
- **(1) THE GATE WAS INERT IN PRODUCTION — `/api/version` was 401'd.** `src/proxy.ts` 401s every unmatched `/api/**` path before the route runs, and the new path was in neither `PUBLIC_ROUTES` nor `SELF_AUTH_ROUTES`. The desktop's unauthenticated boot fetch got a 401, read it as "no answer", and failed open — correctly, and every single time. Endpoint, policy core, screen and window swap were all working; the floor simply never reached a client. **Caught in the field log (`version gate: floor fetch 401`), not by a test** — every unit test on both sides was green. Fixed in both lists; `proxy.test.ts` now pins the signed-out 200 and the zero claims reads. **Lesson worth keeping: "the route has no auth wrapper" is not the same claim as "the route is unauthenticated".**
- **(2) A dead download left a buttonless spinner.** `updater.js` keeps `available` true through an `error` (correctly — the release did not stop existing, and clearing it would feed GUARD 2 a false "nothing is published" and relax a real block), but the screen read `available` as "a download is running". A connection reset mid-download left a progress spinner with its only button hidden by `busy`, and the next automatic attempt up to 4h away. The failure is now its own fact (`failed`); the verdict ignores it, the screen swaps the spinner for honest copy and a retry.
- **(3) `force` dogfooded a screen no user gets.** The mode rewrote the updater for the verdict only, so a developer on an unpackaged build blocked through the real branch and was then shown "this build cannot update itself" with no button. `effectiveUpdater()` is read by both sides and rewrites exactly the two fields that matter (`supported` defeats GUARD 1, `checked` defeats GUARD 2).
- **(4) A verdict reached before `init()` ate the block.** `index.js` arms `updater.init()` first and it fires a state event synchronously, reaching `onUpdaterState` with `handlers` still empty; the gate marked itself blocked, so the first decision after wiring looked like "no change" and `onBlock` never fired. `init()` now starts from nothing. **(5)** A stale `closed` (it arrives a tick after `destroy()`, by which time a release-then-reblock can have a new window in the slot) could orphan the live window, failing `isGateSender` on every IPC call and blanking the page; the handler and the push listener are bound to the window they were created for.
- **The `x-dopl-app-version` contract is UNCHANGED.** The only edit to `app-version-header.ts` is a comment recording why the obvious-looking `426 Upgrade Required` keyed on that header was rejected: it would gate ACCESS on a value any device-token holder can set (the one thing the contract forbids), it would buy no coercion (the client that lies is the client that ignores the 426), and it would reach a population that cannot act on it (builds ≤1.8.0 would see unexplained API failures on every screen). A cooperating client PULLS the floor and blocks itself, which puts the screen, the download and the restart in the one process that can perform them.
- **Design note — the floor is an env var, not a DB row.** Both satisfy "changeable without a code change". Env wins: the endpoint is unauthenticated and hit by every desktop at boot and every 4h, so a DB read buys nothing and adds a dependency whose outage turns a floor lookup into a 500; the change cadence is one edit per forced-upgrade event, which is the cadence of a Vercel env edit; and a DB row is only genuinely code-change-free once something can WRITE it, i.e. an admin surface that does not exist.
- **RESIDUAL (RESOLVED, see F-126) — `DOPL_DESKTOP_LATEST_VERSION` is bumped BY HAND.** It is the server-side half of the anti-brick clamp and it goes stale silently: a `latest` left behind a real release makes the clamp refuse a legitimate floor, which fails toward nobody being blocked (the safe direction, and the reason it is acceptable today) but also toward the gate quietly not working. Fold it into the release step, or derive it from the GitHub release feed the updater already reads. Deliberately not built here: it is release tooling, not gate behavior.
- **RESIDUAL — the env vars live only in a gitignored `.env.example`.** `.gitignore` matches `.env*`, so the operator documentation the first pass wrote is local-only. Both vars are now documented in ENGINEERING.md, which is tracked; a tracked `.env.example` exemption would be better and is a separate call.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** server-side coercion of pre-gate clients (they cannot obey it, and a 426 reaches them as unexplained failures), a second timer (the gate rides the updater's 4h), a persisted floor (it would let a cold boot start inside a block with no network to re-check), and any dark-mode variant of the screen (every auth surface in this app is a white page).
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2376** green (up from 2367; the 401 and the four state defects each carry a test), root vitest **1994** in 122 files green, root `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx eslint` 0 errors in both trees (2 pre-existing root warnings). Screenshotted over CDP from the real Electron window in both the blocking and failed-download states. Live-verified against a running Next server: `/api/version` answers 200 signed-out while `/api/workspaces/me` still 401s.
- Status: resolved (two residuals above); committed on `min-version-gate`, unmerged, undeployed. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-126: The anti-brick clamp derives "latest published build" instead of being told it (2026-08-03, branch `min-version-gate`)

- Location: **new** `src/shared/version/latest-release.ts` (+test). Touched: `src/shared/version/desktop-floor.ts` (+test), `src/app/api/version/route.ts` (+test), `docs/ENGINEERING.md`. Nothing under `dopl-desktop-app/` changes — the client's guards were already grounded in the real feed.
- Found during: closing F-125's first residual (`DOPL_DESKTOP_LATEST_VERSION` bumped by hand, stale in silence).
- Severity: latent — the stale direction fails safe (nobody blocked) and useless (the floor can no longer be raised, and nothing says so).
- **The clamp's reference value is now read from `https://github.com/SamuelrWang/Dopl/releases/latest/download/latest-mac.yml`**, the electron-updater channel file. `DOPL_DESKTOP_MIN_VERSION` stays the one variable an operator sets; `DOPL_DESKTOP_LATEST_VERSION` survives as a cold-start fallback and manual override and **should normally be unset**. Design is in ENGINEERING.md; not repeated here.
- **Why the channel file and not `api.github.com/.../releases/latest`.** Unauthenticated REST is 60/hr **per IP** and a Vercel lambda egresses from a shared pool it does not control — the first probe written while designing this got `403 API rate limit exceeded` from an ordinary IP before it ever got a release, and the credential that would fix it is a new secret on the boot path of an unauthenticated endpoint.
