The channel file is a release ASSET (github.com 302s to the asset CDN), outside the REST quota; it is the byte-for-byte file the client's updater consumes, so the server clamp and the client's `GUARD 2` become two readings of ONE fact instead of two facts that drift; and a `tag_name` exists the moment a release is created, with or without a mac artifact behind it, while a `version:` in the channel file exists only because a build was uploaded — which is the exact question the clamp asks.
- **Why NOT a release-step bump (the rejected design B).** `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` says `1.8.2` while the newest published release is `1.7.24`. Publish-time automation copying that version would DECLARE a latest that does not exist and hand the clamp permission to serve a floor nothing can install — a **new** way to brick, which was the one prohibition. It also wants a Vercel write credential inside a manual, laptop-run release (there is no CI for the desktop app), and it fails the same silent way the hand bump does: a release run where the step errors leaves the value stale. `package.json` is a claim about what SHOULD ship; the channel file is the record of what DID.
- **Zero new dependencies on the request path.** `GET` stays a synchronous function — it cannot await a socket even by accident. It reads a per-instance cache and schedules the refresh with `after()` (the `scheduleEntryEmbedding` idiom, detached fallback outside a request scope), 10-min TTL on success, 60s backoff on failure, single-flight. A hung GitHub is pinned by a test that calls `GET()` against a never-resolving `fetch`.
- **Every failure degrades toward a LOWER latest**, which refuses MORE floors and blocks FEWER people: throw / timeout / 403 / 5xx / 200-full-of-HTML all leave the previous value in place. **Expiry schedules a refresh and never blanks the value** — a stale-low latest blocks nobody, a `null` latest disables the clamp entirely, so serving stale is the safe read and dropping to `null` is the unsafe one. The one thing the module may never do is invent a version above what the feed advertises.
- **The refusal log now names its source.** A derived latest means "publish the build"; a declared one means "your env var is stale" — opposite fixes, so a line naming the wrong knob is worse than one naming none. `DesktopVersionFloor` grew `latestSource: "release-feed" | "env" | null` for it. **The wire contract is unchanged**: `/api/version` still answers exactly `{ minSupported, latest }`, and `resolveDesktopFloor` stays pure (the derived value arrives as an argument, never by import).
- **Drift alarm:** a test reads `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` and asserts `build.publish[0]`'s owner/repo match the URL constants. A rename would otherwise 404 the derivation forever and silently fall back to the stale env var — the exact failure this closes.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** an authenticated GitHub client (a secret on an unauthenticated endpoint's path, to raise a limit the chosen source is not subject to); a second remote source as backup (the env fallback already covers a dead feed, and a second socket doubles the failure surface for a value nothing waits on); a cross-instance cache (Next's data cache would own a second TTL on top of this one, untestably); and any change to `dopl-desktop-app/` (the client's GUARD 1/GUARD 2 already read the real feed).
- Verification: root vitest **2030** in 123 files green (up from 1994/122 — 36 new), root `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx eslint src/` 0 errors / 2 pre-existing warnings. Both sources probed live against the real public repo: channel file 200 in 0.45s through 2 redirects carrying `version: 1.7.24`; REST API 403 then 200 from the same IP minutes apart. Desktop suite untouched and unrun by this change (no file under `dopl-desktop-app/` was modified).
- Status: resolved; committed on `min-version-gate`, unmerged, undeployed, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-127: The landing page's Download button had never worked (2026-08-03, branch `min-version-gate`)

- Location: **new** `src/app/download/route.ts`, `src/shared/version/mac-download.ts` (+test). Touched: `src/features/marketing/constants.ts`, `components/hero.tsx`, `components/site-nav.tsx`, `src/proxy.ts`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md`.
- Found during: consolidating the landing page to download-only for the public desktop launch (Samuel's directive; retirement plan Stage A).
- Severity: **bug**, in production, on the one control the page exists for.
- **The link 404'd, and had since it was written.** `DOWNLOAD_URL` was `https://github.com/SamuelrWang/Dopl/releases/latest/download/Dopl-arm64.dmg`, above a comment claiming "the version-less asset name keeps this link valid across builds". electron-builder's default `artifactName` stamps the version in, so the newest release carries `Dopl-1.7.24-arm64.dmg` and no release has ever carried the other. Verified live: that URL resolves to `…/releases/download/v1.7.24/Dopl-arm64.dmg` → **404**. `/releases/latest/download/:asset` is evergreen in the RELEASE — GitHub resolves "latest" for you — and then demands the exact file name, which moves every release. A hardcoded URL has no failure mode a test can observe, which is why nothing noticed. The second consumer, `channels-onboarding-core.tsx`'s "Download for macOS", was broken by the same constant and is fixed by the same change.
- **The asset name is now READ from `latest-mac.yml`**, the electron-updater channel file — the same source, for the same reasons, as F-126's anti-brick clamp (release asset, not the rate-limited REST API; the byte-for-byte file the updater consumes). `GET /download` resolves and 307s; the name comes back current by construction, because it was just read out of the release GitHub itself calls latest. `/download` is also a stable public name for a README or a DM, and it survives the website retirement, which keeps this page.
- **Why not pin `artifactName` to a version-less name** (one line, and it makes the original constant true): it renames the artifacts in **1.8.3**, the min-version gate's first release and the one release whose auto-update path must not acquire a new variable; it leaves the button 404ing until that release ships, which is after this branch merges; and it drops the version out of the downloaded file name, which is the first thing a support conversation asks for. Nothing forecloses it — the resolver never assumed a name, so a rename costs it nothing.
- **The button can never show a stack trace.** Timeout, 5xx, HTML from a captive portal, a channel file naming no dmg → the releases page. A test asserts that every input, hostile or broken, still yields a `https://github.com/SamuelrWang/Dopl/releases/…` URL, and that a name carrying `/`, a scheme, `%`, `?`, `#` or whitespace is refused before it can be interpolated.
- **The landing consolidation itself:** the nav's dark Login button and the hero's Login/Download pair became one Download each, in the existing `.lp-btn--3d` surface — no new component, no new CSS. `/login` the ROUTE is untouched (KEEP-listed; the desktop OAuth handoff and password reset land there). `/pricing`'s subscribe flows still push `/login` deliberately: they need a session and are not landing CTAs. `/terms` and `/privacy` keep their "← Back" links to `/login`, which is where they are reached FROM.
- Verification: root vitest **2052** in 124 files green (up from 2032/123 — 20 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` 0 errors / 2 pre-existing warnings. `/download` exercised end-to-end against the live public repo: 307 → `…/releases/latest/download/Dopl-1.7.24-arm64.dmg` → 200 from the asset CDN with `Content-Disposition: attachment`. Screenshots at 1440×900 and 375×812, before and after.
- Status: resolved; committed on `min-version-gate`, unmerged, undeployed. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-128: `dopl://open` — the general-purpose deep link the retirement needs (2026-08-03, branch `min-version-gate`)

- Location: **new** `dopl-desktop-app/main/deep-link-target.js`, `main/deep-link.js`, `test/deep-link-target.test.mjs`. Touched: `main/index.js` (the deep-link section moved out), `test/shell-mode.test.mjs`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md`.
- Found during: retirement plan Stage A — "ship a `dopl://open` verb in 1.8.3 so post-retirement web URLs can hand off into the desktop app" (§2.5).
- Severity: gap. The handler adopted auth fragments and **nothing else**, and a rejected fragment returned before `showMainWindow()` — so a plain `dopl://` link launched the app and then did nothing. The plan calls that "an accidental no-op rather than a first-class one".
- **Grammar, hardening, mapping and the cold-start send are documented in ENGINEERING.md** (§18, "THE `dopl://open` VERB"); not repeated here.
- **The auth handoff moved VERBATIM.** `index.js` was at 499/500 lines, so both halves were carved out at the cap (the `wake.js` / `app-menu.js` idiom). Every comment on the M4 CSRF gate, the C15 token re-arm and the remote-mode completion load travelled with the code unedited; `shell-mode.test.mjs`'s park/order pins were repointed at the new file rather than relaxed, and gained the two refusals the new verb adds.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** deferred navigation across a sign-in (plan §2.5 accepts landing home, and there is no queue anywhere in this app to reuse); a renderer-side ack for the cold-start send (it would want a bridge change in a security-sensitive file to save one 300ms re-send of an idempotent route); routing in remote mode (`DOPL_UI=remote` is the rollback path, and teaching it new tricks is how a rollback path stops being one); and any change to `apps/desktop-ui` — the SPA half already exists (`dopl:navigate` → `router.navigate`).
- **Residual:** `WORKSPACE_PAGES` is a hand copy of the SPA's route table. The drift test makes it loud rather than silent, but the real fix is generating one from the other at build time — worth doing only if the table starts moving.
- **THE SEAM BUG, AND HOW IT WAS FOUND.** `parseDeepLink` handed the mapper `parsed.pathname`, which `URL` has ALREADY normalized: `dopl://open/{seg}/../../etc` reads back as `/etc`, so the mapper's dot-segment refusal never fired and the link opened `/etc/canvas` — in-app and unable to escape, but a link that says one thing and resolves to another, which is the exact property the refusal exists for. **Every unit case passed**, because each was handed a raw string directly; the bug lived only in the seam between the two halves. It was caught by a throwaway pass that `require`d `deep-link.js` under a stubbed `electron` and drove `openDeepLink` — the desktop convention for electron-requiring modules is source extraction, which pattern-matches the code rather than running it. The path is now cut out of the URL string (`rawPathOf`), and the seam has its own end-to-end table.
- Verification: desktop suite **2446** green (up from 2376 — 69 new + 1), `npm run lint` 0 errors / 0 warnings. Fourteen dispatcher cases driven at runtime (both verbs, both malformed shapes, signed-out, remote mode, the park and its flush, `arm`). Not exercised against a real running app: the verb ships in 1.8.3 and wants a click test on Samuel's machine (`open 'dopl://open/<segment>/channels'` cold, warm, and signed out) before the release.
- Status: resolved; committed on `min-version-gate`, unmerged, undeployed, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-129: The SPA shell's preload never grew `sessions` — "Open thread" was gone from every DM and channel in 1.8.x (2026-08-04, master, ships as 1.8.4)

- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/renderer/app-preload.js`. **new** `dopl-desktop-app/test/preload-parity.test.mjs`. Touched: `apps/desktop-ui/src/lib/dopl-bridge.ts`, `dopl-desktop-app/package.json` (1.8.4), `docs/ENGINEERING.md`.
- Found during: Samuel reporting the button missing after the first public SPA-shell release. Nothing had been deleted.
- Severity: **bug**, in production, on the only control that opens a session window from a thread.
- **The button hid itself, correctly, forever.** `session-card.tsx:365` is `if (!bridge) return null`, where `bridge` is `getDesktopSessions()` — feature-detected on `window.dopl.sessions.reopen` (`src/shared/lib/desktop.ts:86`). That self-hiding is right for a plain browser and for an older desktop build, and it is precisely why this shipped: **a self-hiding surface cannot report its own absence.** No error, no console line, no failing test. `renderer/preload.js` (the REMOTE shell) has exposed `sessions.reopen` all along; `renderer/app-preload.js` (the SPA shell, default since 1.8.0) exposed fourteen namespaces and the string "sessions" **zero times**. Confirmed against the shipped artifact: `/Applications/Dopl.app/Contents/Resources/app.asar` → `appOrigin, apiRequest, onNavigate, signOut, beginSignIn, passwordSignIn, sendMagicLink, getAuthState, onAuthState, channels, syncWatch, onSyncEvent, openExternal, avatarDataUri`.
- **The main half was never broken, and the sender gate was never the second half of the bug.** `sessions:reopen` is registered in `main/channel-dir-ipc.js:192` inside `mainOnly(...)`, which resolves the live main window and demands its TOP frame. In `DOPL_UI=spa` mode `shell-mode.createShellWindow` calls `setMainWindow(win)` with the SPA window, and `index.js` hands `channel-dir-ipc` the lazy `() => mainWindow` accessor — so the gate's window IS the SPA window, and the SPA's `file:` page is its own top frame. **Verified at runtime rather than argued**: `channels.setPermissionPreset(uuid, {tools,messages})` answered `{ ok: true }` from the SPA window, and only `mainOnly`'s admitted branch can produce that write. Nothing was loosened.
- **THE THIRD OF THESE, WHICH IS WHY THE FIX IS A TEST.** Avatars (F-124), the consent card's folder row, now this — three shipped gaps in the same seam, each found by a human noticing something missing. `test/preload-parity.test.mjs` EXECUTES both preloads against a fake `electron` (the surface and the wire names are the real ones, not a grep over text a rename would walk past) and pins four things: every CALLABLE op the remote preload exposes exists in the SPA preload; each shared op invokes the same channel with the same payload (a re-added namespace on a different wire is the same outage with a passing presence check); every capability the web tree feature-detects is provided, driven off a table re-checked against the real detection source; and every member `SpaBridgeSurface` declares exists — the direction that would have caught F-124. Restricting parity to CALLABLE members is what makes the legacy markers fall out for free.
- **`isDesktop` stays absent from the SPA preload, and that is now pinned as a DELIBERATE divergence** rather than left implicit: `isDesktopApp()` routes the web login into the browser-OAuth + `dopl://` handoff, which the SPA must not take (it signs in through main via `use-login-core` + bridge actions). Adding it to "fix parity" would re-route sign-in.
- Audit of the rest of the seam: no other gap. `channels.chooseFolder`, `channels.get/setPermissionPreset` and every `getSpaBridge()` member (`apiRequest`, `getAuthState`, `onAuthState`, `openExternal`, `avatarDataUri`, `appOrigin`, `syncWatch`, `onSyncEvent`) are all present.
- Verification: desktop suite **2457** green (up from 2446 — 11 new), desktop lint 0 errors / 0 warnings; root vitest **2052** in 124 files, `tsc --noEmit` clean (root and SPA), root eslint 0 errors / 2 pre-existing warnings; SPA suite **115**. Red/green proved by deleting the `sessions` block: 5 of 11 fail, restored, green. END TO END, **partial and labelled as such**: the app was run from source in `DOPL_UI=spa` mode and driven over CDP — `window.dopl.sessions.reopen` is a function in the real SPA window (the exact predicate `getDesktopSessions()` uses, and the shipped bundle's compiled copy of it), the sender gate admits that window, and `sessions.reopen(uuid, taskId)` returned `{ ok:false, reason:'no-thread' }` — a shape produced only by `session-park.js:226`, i.e. deep inside the engine, unreachable unless the gate admitted — with **zero** `channel-dir ipc: refused` lines in the run's diag log. **NOT verified: the button rendered next to a real thread.** The dev binary is unsigned, so reading the signed app's `safeStorage` blob raised a macOS Keychain prompt; rather than grant an unsigned binary access to Samuel's credential, the run used an isolated `--user-data-dir`, which is signed out and cannot reach a channel. The render half rests on the runtime predicate plus `session-card.test.tsx`.
- **Residual:** no test mounts `ReopenWindowButton` WITH a bridge and asserts the button appears — the root suite has no DOM environment (`renderToStaticMarkup` only, and the detection is a `useEffect`), so the two halves are pinned separately. A signed-in click test on Samuel's machine after the 1.8.4 release would close both this and F-128's residual.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, unpushed, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-130: The landing CTA goes through the account — "Get Started" → `/login` → `/get-started` (2026-08-04, master)

- Location: **new** `src/shared/lib/url/post-auth-landing.ts` (+ test), **new** `src/app/get-started/page.tsx`, **new** `src/features/get-started/` (screen, install animation, stylesheet), **new** `src/app/auth/callback/route.test.ts`. Touched: `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts`, `src/features/auth/hooks/use-login.ts`, `src/features/auth/components/google-one-tap.tsx`, `src/features/marketing/{constants.ts,components/hero.tsx,components/site-nav.tsx}`, `src/shared/version/mac-download.ts` (+ test), `src/shared/layout/layout-shell.tsx`, `src/config/index.ts`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md`.
- Found during: Samuel's ask for the Wispr Flow pattern — capture the account BEFORE the download, so install drop-off is recoverable and the funnel (signup → download → first-open) is countable instead of being one anonymous click into a dmg.
- Severity: feature. F-127 made the landing download-only and made its URL resolve; this puts an account in front of it without adding a second button.
- **The rules, the redirect decision and the animation's construction are documented in ENGINEERING.md** ("The download goes THROUGH the account now"); not repeated here.
- **THE SURGICAL PART WAS NOT THE PAGE, IT WAS THE ONE-LINE SIGNAL.** `use-login.ts` set `redirectTo=/canvas` on EVERY callback URL it built, because it read the param through `safeRedirect`'s default. So `/auth/callback` could not tell a plain signup from a deep link, and its onboarding detour — the branch that reads "did this sign-in owe somewhere a return trip" — fired for everybody. Moving the landing without fixing that would have routed every new account into the web `/onboarding` it is supposed to skip. The param is now threaded only when the visitor's URL carried one, and "carried one" excludes hostile values, so a crafted `redirectTo` cannot flip the branch by being rejected.
- **`safeRedirect`'s default was left alone on purpose.** Two of its five call sites (`/onboarding`, `POST /api/onboarding/complete`) ask "where does the app live", not "where does a sign-in end". Those answers diverge at the retirement — `/canvas` retires, `/get-started` replaces it — so they are separate functions rather than one moved default.
- **`/get-started` MAY NOT BE NAMED `/download-anything`.** `PUBLIC_ROUTES` matches by `startsWith`, so `/download` opens every `/download*` path to signed-out visitors. A test pins the landing constant against that prefix. (Found while building a throwaway preview harness at `/download-preview`, which was public for free — the hazard is real, not hypothetical.)
- **The auto-start mechanism was CHANGED MID-BUILD by a live failure**, see F-131: it began as `location.assign("/download")` and became a zero-sized same-origin iframe the moment a stale asset name produced a GitHub 404 and threw the browser off the page. Same download on the happy path; the page survives the sad one.
- **`resolveMacDownloadAsset()` is an extraction, not a second read.** The page prints the real dmg name in step 2, and an install instruction naming the wrong file is worse than one naming none — so the name comes from the same `latest-mac.yml` fetch, under the same TTL, as the URL. `resolveMacDownloadUrl` is now two lines over it. `null` is a first-class answer (the copy drops the file name); the URL still cannot be null.
- Verification: root vitest **2090** in 126 files green (up from 2052/124 — 38 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` 0 errors / 2 pre-existing warnings. Driven in a real browser against the dev server: landing "Get Started" → `/login` (which carries "Sign up"); signed-out `/get-started` → 307 `/login?redirectTo=%2Fget-started`; the page issues `GET /download` by itself → GitHub → `release-assets.githubusercontent.com` with `content-disposition: attachment`, and the top-level location is STILL the page afterwards. The four OAuth entry points' `redirect_to` values captured off the wire (plain, deep-linked, `desktop=1`, `desktop=1&provider=github`). Screenshots at 1440×900 and 375×812 plus four frozen animation frames and the reduced-motion frame.
- **NOT verified: a completed sign-in.** Doing that end to end needs credentials or a new account in the live Supabase project, neither of which an agent should manufacture. The hop from a finished auth to `/get-started` rests on `route.test.ts`'s matrix and the captured `redirect_to` values instead.
- **Residual:** the escape-hatch decision (no "just download" link on the landing) is a funnel judgement, not a technical one, and is worth revisiting against real drop-off. `/pricing`'s signed-out subscribe pushes `/login?redirect=/pricing` — the param is `redirect`, which nothing has ever read (see F-131's second half).
- **THE LAYOUT WAS REBUILT THE NEXT DAY to the reference Samuel supplied** (Wispr Flow's post-login download page): two full-height fields replacing the centred `1fr 1fr` row — a 43% `--ink` column (brand · serif display heading · three steps · "Not working? Try again." · the live status line in the reference's bottom-left ornament slot) and a 57% `--rail-tile`→`--rail` field holding the illustration, cropped by the field instead of centred in a box. **BEHAVIOUR IS UNTOUCHED** — the auth gate, the iframe auto-start, the retry link, the server-resolved dmg name in step 2 (and its null degrade), the animation's phase logic and its reduced-motion last frame all survive verbatim; only positions, colours and the step copy (four steps compressed to the three the heading promises) moved. **No new colour was invented:** both field colours are existing tokens, `--gs-accent` was consolidated onto `--link` (globals.css's only blue, which retired the hand-picked `#6b7cff` and the folder icon's hardcoded `#7c828f`), and the drop-target tints are `color-mix` over that same token. The serif is `--font-playfair` (Newsreader), the token the fleet audit pinned. **The stylesheet split three ways** on the way through — `get-started.css` 784 → 293 (page + copy), `install-animation.css` 401 (composition), `install-animation-motion.css` 277 (timeline) — so no file in the feature is over the §2 cap; it was one file doing three jobs, which is why the layout rebuild touched all of it. The animation's coordinate space grew 400×320 → 640×660 and its scale factor now reads the FIELD through container units rather than stepping through width breakpoints. Verified: root vitest **2092** in 126 files green (unchanged — the feature has no tests of its own; behaviour is pinned by `route.test.ts` and `mac-download.test.ts`, both untouched), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` 0 errors / 2 pre-existing warnings, `npm run build` green with `/get-started` still `ƒ`. **NOT verified in a browser, deliberately** — Samuel reviews the rendered page himself after deploy; the modern syntax the layout leans on (`container-type: size`, `100cqw`/`100cqh`, length÷length in `calc`, `color-mix`) was instead checked in the BUILT stylesheet, where all four survive the pipeline unrewritten.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, unpushed, undeployed. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-131: `/download` 404s for the ten minutes after every release, and `/pricing`'s return param is dead (2026-08-04, master) — FIRST HALF RESOLVED, second half open

- Location: `src/shared/version/mac-download.ts` (`macDownloadUrlFor` + `MAC_DOWNLOAD_TTL_S`); `src/features/marketing/components/pricing-content.tsx:115,132,139`.
- Found during: F-130, in the least deniable way possible — the browser navigated to `https://github.com/SamuelrWang/Dopl/releases/download/v1.8.4/Dopl-1.7.24-arm64.dmg` and got a **404 page**, live.
- Severity: **bug** (first half), in production, on the download button. Small blast radius, sharp edges.
- **THE STALE WINDOW DEGRADES TO A 404, NOT TO THE PREVIOUS BUILD.** The resolver caches the asset NAME for `MAC_DOWNLOAD_TTL_S` (600s) and builds `…/releases/latest/download/<name>`. `latest` is resolved by GitHub AT REQUEST TIME. So in the window after a release, the cached name is yesterday's and the release is today's — and GitHub answers 404, because that asset does not exist in that release. The module's own comment says the cost is "at most ten minutes of the previous build being served"; the previous build is not served, nothing is. Reproduced end to end today (1.8.4 published 07:28Z; a cached `Dopl-1.7.24-arm64.dmg` 404'd against tag `v1.8.4`).
- **The fix is to stop mixing the two coordinates.** The channel file carries `version:` AND the asset name in one body; `latest-release.ts` already parses the version. Build `…/releases/download/v<version>/<asset>` from ONE read and the name can never be paired with a release it does not belong to — a stale answer then serves the genuinely previous build, which is what the comment claims and what auto-update fixes on first run. Not done in F-130: it changes `macDownloadUrlFor`'s contract and its "evergreen /releases/latest" test, which is its own change with its own proof.
- **Mitigated, not fixed, in the meantime.** `/get-started` starts the download in an iframe, so this 404 no longer throws a visitor off the install instructions. The landing page no longer links `/download` at all. A direct visit to `usedopl.com/download` inside the window still 404s.
- **SECOND HALF, unrelated except by proximity: `/pricing` pushes `/login?redirect=…` and nothing reads `redirect`.** The login page reads `redirectTo`. So a signed-out visitor who clicks Subscribe has never returned to `/pricing` — they landed on the default post-auth destination, which was `/canvas` and is now `/get-started`. Left alone in F-130 deliberately: making the param work is a behaviour change nobody asked for, and there is no way to preserve the accidental `/canvas` landing that is worth preserving. Decide it explicitly — rename to `redirectTo` (subscribe resumes where it left off) or drop the param (admit the download page is the right answer now that billing is moving in-app).
- Status: **first half RESOLVED** (2026-08-04, on master with F-130): `readChannel()` reads asset name AND version from one body; `macDownloadUrlFor(asset, version)` builds `…/releases/download/v<version>/<asset>` — tag-pinned, so a stale answer serves the genuinely previous build (which auto-updates on first run) instead of a 404. The version-less `latest` form survives only as the fallback when the channel file names a dmg but no parseable version — the same window as before, never worse. Test suite updated: the "evergreen /releases/latest" pin became its own fallback test, plus a regression pin that a resolved URL never contains `/latest/`. **Second half (pricing `redirect` param) still open** — decide rename-to-`redirectTo` vs drop once the D1 billing-surface decision lands; billing moving in-app argues for drop.

### F-132: Channels was silently dead on 1.8.x — the listener's fetch copy never got the 401 repair, and its only failure branch logged nothing (2026-08-04, master)

- Location: **new** `dopl-desktop-app/main/api-repair.js`, **new** `dopl-desktop-app/test/listener-auth-repair.test.mjs`. Touched: `dopl-desktop-app/main/{api.js,listener-io.js,listener-heal.js,channel-listener.js}`, `dopl-desktop-app/test/{auth-tokens.test.mjs,listener-heal.test.mjs}`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md`.
- Found during: Samuel mid-test with a real peer — he showed as NOT online to them while the app was running, and "Open thread" answered *"This channel isn't available on this machine."*
- Severity: **bug**, in production, taking out the whole Channels feature — the lead product.
- **ONE CAUSE, BOTH SYMPTOMS.** `reconcileInner` calls `io.listWorkspaces()` FIRST and returns on its `null`. That return is upstream of `presence.setWorkspaces` (~L265), `realtime.setWorkspaces` (~L267), identity resolution and every channel-loop start — so no heartbeat rows (symptom 1: offline to the peer) and no loops (symptom 2: no channel is available on this machine). The self-heal then re-ran the same doomed pass every 30s, forever.
- **WHY THE CALL 401'd — the actual regression.** `api.js:41-53` already documented the mechanism: auth rides the Electron session's Supabase COOKIE jar; the pre-1.8 remote page kept that jar fresh with supabase-js, "the bundled SPA removes the page and with it the refresher, and `getAuthCookie()` only repairs an EMPTY jar, never a STALE one." api.js was given a Phase-2 401 repair for exactly this. **`listener-io.js` kept its own `apiFetch` — api.js:2 says so in as many words, "The Channels listener keeps its own copy (E2E-verified — not refactored here)" — and never received it.** So `listWorkspaces`, `listChannels`, every `/await` long-poll, `channel-post`'s lifecycle posts, roster, threads and consent all died on a stale jar and recovered only BY LUCK, when some other api.js caller happened to 401 and repair the shared jar first.
- **THE DEADLOCK THAT MADE IT PERMANENT.** The listener is not repair-less: `channelLoop`'s 401 branch and `listChannelsWithRetry`'s auth-shaped retry both call `auth.ensureFresh()` + `writeSessionCookies`. But both are reachable only AFTER a workspace list succeeds. A cold start with a stale jar therefore had zero loops, zero enumerations, and no path to the two repairs it did have. Confirmed in the field log: a full app relaunch at 00:06:37 did not clear it — the self-heal line reappeared 800ms later.
- **AND IT WAS INVISIBLE.** `listWorkspaces`'s `if (res.status === 401) { notifyStale(); return null; }` was the ONLY silent `null` in the listener. Field log (2026-08-04, ~3h): **293** × `reconcile self-heal: retrying 1 unenumerated workspace(s)`, and **zero** `listWorkspaces` / `reconcile: listWorkspaces error` / `enumeration FAILED` / `namecache` / `identity tier` lines — which is what proves it was the silent 401 branch rather than a throw or a non-OK status. Independently corroborated: `auth-tokens: refreshed ( 401-repair )` × 4 at one instant (api.js's repair firing and briefly rescuing the shared jar), and a presence row ~32h stale against a peer's seconds-fresh one.
- **THE FIX IS SHARED, NOT COPIED.** A third divergent copy is how this happened, so the repair moved to `main/api-repair.js` and BOTH seams call it; each keeps only its own `sendOnce`, because the SEND is what legitimately differs (the listener's wires the caller's abort signal into the controller for the wake kick and carries the long-poll timeouts `awaitOrCheap` depends on; api.js's carries `no-store`). A thunk, not a merged transport — deliberately, under time pressure, and stated as such. Rule and boundedness in ENGINEERING.md. Nothing about either REQUEST changed; only what happens on a 401 did, and the request shape (URL, method, headers, version stamp, body, abort wiring) plus both abort paths are pinned byte-for-byte by the new suite.
- **THE LOG COPY WAS ACTIVELY MISLEADING and cost hours.** `reconcile` reported a total failure to list workspaces as `healer.onEnumerationFailure(1)` — "retrying 1 unenumerated workspace(s)" — naming a per-workspace channel enumeration that was never attempted and implying one workspace was affected when NONE had been listed. Now `healer.onWorkspaceListFailure()`, sharing the same single retry timer (so the two paths still cannot stack), with copy that says what actually failed. `listWorkspaces`'s 401 branch now logs the cause AND the blast radius.
- **STEP 4, the ordering question, answered NO CHANGE — with the reason, because "leave it" needs proof too.** The early return does not starve presence or realtime: it never CLEARS either, and `lastGoodWorkspaceIds` is filled in lockstep with `presence.setWorkspaces(wsIds)` on every successful pass and emptied with it on sign-out, so both keep the last good set across a transient failure. Realtime additionally has the `shouldReapplyWorkspaces` want=0 repair, which runs BEFORE the list. The one starved state is a cold start whose very FIRST list fails, and persisting the set to disk would not fix it — the heartbeat POSTs the same origin with the same credential, so whatever killed `/api/workspaces` kills `/api/channels/presence`. The credential was the bug. The lockstep invariant is now pinned, so a future edit that clears one without the other fails.
- **OPERATOR RECOVERY WITHOUT A NEW BUILD (verified from the code, not run): sign out, then sign back in.** `auth-store.persist()` writes the encrypted blob ONLY — never the jar — so proactive rotations refresh the bearer and leave the jar carrying the old JWT, and a relaunch changes nothing (the cookie has a 400-day expiry). `signOut()` calls `cookies.clearSessionCookies()`, which makes the jar EMPTY, which is the one state `getAuthCookie()` will repair; the next call rebuilds it from the fresh session. Quitting the app, waiting, or reconnecting the network do not.
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2480** green (baseline 2457; +23, and the 2 pre-existing guards that pinned the old shapes were updated, not deleted), desktop `npm run lint` 0 errors / 0 warnings. Root suite untouched — no file under `src/` was modified. Red/green measured on all three halves, not asserted: reverting `listener-io.apiFetch` to the bare `sendOnce` fails **8** (6 in the new suite + the two shared-seam guards in `auth-tokens.test.mjs`); restoring `healer.onEnumerationFailure(1)` in `reconcileInner` fails **2** (the copy guard + the S8 shape pin in `listener-heal.test.mjs`); deleting the new `listWorkspaces` 401 `diag` fails **2**. Each restored to green before the next.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live. The evidence base is the shipped source plus `~/Library/Application Support/dopl-desktop/listener.log`, read as read-only evidence.
- **Residual:** the same log carries an UNRELATED storm — `ui-sync auth MISSING — rotate` interleaved 1:1 with `auth-tokens: refresh failed — transient (… attempt 39316)`, tens of thousands of attempts in seconds while offline. `ui-sync.js` re-asks for a credential in a tight loop with no bound of its own; the failure counter is global, so it also inflates every other caller's `attempts` and hence `retryDelayMs`. Not touched here (different subsystem, and this was an urgent single-cause fix), but it is a real F-072 violation and should be its own finding.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, unpushed, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-133: an agent's answer was structurally undeliverable, and the fresh-install trigger gate dropped every request (2026-08-04, master)

- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/{prompt-framing.js,claude-runtime.js,trigger.js,channel-listener.js,channel-post.js,targeting.js,session-effects.js,session-window.js,session-dispatch.js}`; `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/{channel-schema.ts,channel-description.ts,channel-ops-write.ts,channel-ops-threads.ts,channel-ops-read.ts,channel-errors.ts,channel.ts}`; `src/features/channels/{lib/group-thread.ts,server/*,components/session-card*.tsx}`; `src/app/api/cron/stale-threads/route.ts`.
- Found during: a live two-agent DM. The requester's agent asked, the responder's agent did the work and posted its findings, and **the answer appeared nowhere on the requester's side**. No error, no partial render, nothing.
- Severity: **bug**, in production, on the lead product, during distribution. Plus one LAUNCH-CRITICAL defect found in the same pass (below).

**THE VANISHING ANSWER — four layers, each locally coherent.**
- The responder posted its whole answer as `kind:"task_finished"`. `lib/group-thread.ts` folds a terminal marker into `draft.endEvent` and NEVER pushes it to `draft.entries`, so its body has no render path at all. Stored, delivered, shown nowhere.
- **The runtime was innocent.** `prompt-framing.deliveryCall` emits no `kind`, so the MCP default is `message`. The AGENT chose the kind — because `milestoneGuidance` framed a milestone as progress "without waiting for the final reply", which puts a milestone and the final answer on ONE AXIS and leaves the agent to complete it, and because VOCABULARY listed all four `task_*` names side by side and said "use each as given".
- The header made it worse rather than better: `computeSummary` scanned the START event first, and a `task_started`'s `metadata.summary` is the requester's ORIGINAL ASK. So the card described the question for the whole life of the exchange, including after it was answered. Summary-before-body is why *a well-behaved agent that writes a good summary hides its own answer harder than a sloppy one* was literally true.
- Nothing linked "the responder is finished" to `channel_tasks` — no trigger, nothing on the write path — so the thread never closed either. Two are open in production since the feature shipped.

**FIXES, and the seams they turn on.**
- **Prompt (P0-1).** The progress-vs-final axis is gone; a milestone is an opt-in one-line marker on its own op that says outright that content never travels in one. VOCABULARY states the split of AUTHORITY instead of a list of names. Every delivery branch — all four — carries the invariant WITH the reason: a `task_finished` body is not shown on the other member's card.
- **Enforcement (P0-2).** `task_started`/`task_finished`/`task_failed` are refused from an AGENT-TOKEN caller. **The seam is the CREDENTIAL, confirmed rather than assumed:** MCP `op="post"` arrives on a bearer token (`source:"agent"`); the desktop runtime's own echoes arrive on the Electron session's Supabase COOKIES (`source:"user"`) and are untouched; the close route's echo is the one real overlap and is exempted at its call site by an option no HTTP caller can set. `task_progress` stays writable — the one `task_*` body a renderer shows, claiming nothing about a lifecycle.
- **The milestone gets its own OP (P0-3)**, not a sharpened `kind`. With `op="milestone"` there is no kind to pick on either call, so "mark a milestone" and "send a reply" cannot be confused by choosing wrongly between adjacent enum values. `kind` keeps all five values deliberately: narrowing the enum turns the mistake into an opaque zod `-32602` at exactly the moment the agent needs telling what to do instead.
- **The renderer belt (P0-4)** is needed regardless of the guard — rows like this already exist, the desktop's `bodyOverride` ends are content, and a human's close summary rides out as the echo's body. The line between status and content is drawn by calm FLAG before string, so a later build's re-wording cannot leak a status line into the reply lane.
- **DELIVERY FILTERS KEPT.** `session-dispatch.feedLiveSession` and `targeting.classify` keep their `kind !== 'message'` drop. They were dangerous only because prose could ride in a `task_*` kind; with that closed, what reaches them is a runtime lifecycle marker (a machine's status line, not a turn) or a milestone (one turn spent per milestone, on a stream agents are told to post freely). Milestones meant for an agent are claimed earlier by `channel-agents.routeAddressedAgent`, which is their deliberate lane. Written down at both sites.

**LAUNCH-CRITICAL, found in the same pass: a fresh install could not answer anything, and nothing said so.**
- `trigger.handleTrigger` gated every inbound request on `spawner.claudeAvailable()`, which is `claude-resolve.resolveClaude()` = `probeStaticPath()` || `probeLoginShell()` — an **EXTERNAL** `claude` on PATH. The binary a session runs ships INSIDE the app bundle (`sdk-loader.resolveClaudeExecutable`), which `session-auth-detect.js` already documented in as many words.
- So anyone who downloaded Dopl without separately installing the Claude Code CLI received **nothing**: the gate returned before the consent row, before the notification, before the window. Invisible from a dev machine, where the startup diag reads `claudeAvailable: true`.
- The startup notice was the visible half of the same conflation — same probe, and it told those installs "Channel auto-responses stay off until it is installed", which was true only because of the bug it was describing.
- **Two questions, two names, kept apart on purpose:** `claudeAvailable()` is the external CLI the auxiliary commands need (`claude mcp …`, `claude setup-token`); `claude-runtime.sessionSpawnAvailable()` is "can a session run at all" — bundled first, external as the headless fallback. Collapsing them is the tidy-up that would re-open this.

**F3b, also found in this pass: every session's FIRST ORDER was a denied tool.** `prompt-framing.firstActions` opened with `ToolSearch("select:mcp__dopl__dopl_channel")`, and `ToolSearch` sits in `tool-profiles.DENIED_BUILTINS` under capability escalation — hard-denied on ALL THREE session profiles (read_only/dopl_only via `DENIED_BUILTINS`, `full` via `SESSION_HARD_DENY`). So `grantDecision` answered `deny` before any button appeared. F3 was written for `attended-prompt.js`, which runs in the operator's own unconstrained Claude Code and keeps it correctly; copying it here made the prompt order what the containment table forbids. **Fixed on the PROMPT side, not the profile side:** a deny list cannot scope `ToolSearch` to one argument, and the lookup is unnecessary because `dopl_channel` is named in every profile's `doplToolsPolicy`. `prompt-profile-drift.test.mjs` now joins the prompt to the REAL deny lists — the same drift class as everything above.

**Product decisions implemented (Samuel, settled).**
- **Close is PROPOSE-then-CONFIRM.** `closeTask` refuses an agent-token caller; `op="propose_close"` posts a marked, NON-TERMINAL `task_progress` the human's surfaces render as a confirmable prompt, and writes nothing to the thread row. The marker keys are reserved and re-stamped server-side only. **Consequence to know: closing over MCP is no longer possible at all** — the human closes in the app (web thread card, SPA, or the desktop session window, whose control is restored).
- **A local session end posts a NON-TERMINAL `session_ended`.** It rides on `task_progress`, which can never be an `endEvent`, so it is non-terminal BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by flag. **A metadata marker rather than a new `kind` because `channel_messages.kind` carries a CHECK constraint** (verified against the live DB) — a first-class kind is a schema change deployed ahead of every desktop that would write it, for a render hint. The caps stay terminal: a cap is this machine refusing to continue, not a window being tidied away.

**Also fixed.**
- **P1-5, the loop brake was void in every DM.** `targeting.js`'s LOOP BRAKE comment said an unaddressed agent reply lands as FYI; `resolvePostMetadata` addresses it anyway (`?? peerUserId`), so a courtesy no-op ("please resend") arrived as a trigger and could spawn a phantom session under a synthetic `task-<channel>-<seq>` id. Two agents can ping-pong that way. `intent:"chat"` via a named `postCourtesy` helper; both false invariant comments corrected where they were written.
- **P1-8, the read tool's own advice lost messages.** A thread-scoped read printed a thread-local seq, warned that it was thread-local, and then interpolated it into `op="await", since=N`. Now carries the channel-wide max, and FAILS SOFT — no number rather than the wrong one.
- **P2-9, five terminal rows for one failure.** Terminal echoes on a first-class thread key on `(kind, taskId)`; `task_started` keeps its cycle (a resume IS a new start) and legacy ids keep their shape.
- **A staleness sweep that PROPOSES, never closes** (`/api/cron/stale-threads`, 14d idle, one prompt per thread forever via a colliding `client_msg_id`, authored `system`).

- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2510** (baseline 2480), root `npx vitest run` **2155** (baseline 2092), `packages/mcp-server` **545** (baseline 531), SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **115** (unchanged), `tsc --noEmit` clean, root eslint 0 errors / 1 pre-existing warning (`proxy.ts:143`), desktop eslint 0/0. Red/green measured, not asserted, on: the P0-2 refusal at both layers (8 server, 5 MCP), the renderer belt (5), the F3b drift join (3), the spawn-availability gate (7), the P1-5 brake (3), the P1-8 cursor (2), the P2-9 dedupe (2). Each restored to green before the next.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- **FLAG FOR SAMUEL: `CRON_SECRET` is unset in Vercel**, so every `/api/cron/*` route answers 503 and the new sweep — like `purge-trash`, `oauth-cleanup`, `reconcile-seats` — is inert until it is set.
- **Residual / needs a decision:** (1) closing over MCP is gone, so an operator driving an external CLI session must close in the app — deliberate, but it is a workflow change; (2) the two threads open since 1.7.20 (`35a12201…`, `8527870d…`) will get a sweep prompt on the first run after `CRON_SECRET` is set, not before; (3) `@dopl/client.closeChannelThread` is now reachable from no MCP op — kept as the route binding for the human lane.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, unpushed, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-134: the billing surface becomes a page of its own, and `?redirectTo=` learns to survive the trip (2026-08-05, master)

- Location: `src/features/billing/{url.ts,components/billing-page-screen.tsx}`; `src/app/billing/{page.tsx,[segment]/page.tsx}`; `src/features/billing/server/{entitlements.ts,stripe.ts}`; `src/app/api/billing/{checkout,portal}/route.ts`; `src/features/billing/components/upgrade-modal.tsx`; `src/features/marketing/components/pricing-content.tsx`; `src/shared/layout/{layout-shell.tsx,app-shell/app-shell.tsx,settings-modal/sections/plans-billing.tsx}`; `apps/desktop-ui/src/{lib/open-in-browser.ts,components/settings-modal/{account-actions,billing-pane}.tsx}`; `src/proxy.ts`.
- Found during: executing decision **D1(a)** of `docs/migration-research/website-retirement-plan.md` — Samuel decided to KEEP embedded checkout, so billing lives on a web page and that is fine long-term.
- Severity: **blocker cleared** (the website retirement's last product-shaped tether) plus **two production bugs** in the auth middleware.

**WHY BILLING COULD NOT FOLLOW THE PRODUCT INTO THE APP.** Checkout is `ui_mode: "elements"`: the payment form is our own React (`components/embedded-checkout.tsx` + `checkout-appearance.ts`) mounting Stripe's script and talking to Stripe's origin. The packaged renderer is a `file://` document under `script-src 'self'` / `connect-src 'none'`. Not a porting effort — a contradiction. So `/billing/{segment}` exists, and it is assembly rather than product: the shipped `PlansBilling` pane and the shipped `DeleteAccount` danger zone, rendered as page sections.

**THE IMPORT GRAPH IS THE LOAD-BEARING PROPERTY, NOT THE MARKUP.** One `AppShell` import drags the rail, sidebar, workspaces fetch, tour and graph engine back into the KEEP set and Stage D stops being a deletion. A test reads the three source files and refuses `@/shared/layout/app-shell`, `@/features/tour`, the onboarding components and the join-request notices. Verified independently from the shipped side: a `vite build` of the SPA into a scratch dir has **no** app-tree module and no `billing-page-screen` in its sourcemap `sources`, while `features/billing/url.ts` IS compiled in — which is exactly the split intended.

**SIX HAND-WRITTEN URLS BECOME ONE BUILDER.** They all named the retiring `/canvas`, so they were going to die on the same day, in the one flow where a dead link costs revenue rather than a 404. `features/billing/url.ts` is pure on purpose (no `next/*`, no `server-only`, no browser globals) so the SPA over `@/`, the Stripe modules under `server-only`, and the RSCs share one definition. Two details that are not cosmetic: the query is CONCATENATED because Stripe substitutes `{CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}` and does not substitute `%7B…%7D`; and `parseBillingReturn` refuses `upgrade`, because that value must not arm a poll for a purchase nobody has made.

**TWO DEFECTS FIXED IN PASSING, both of which predate this work.**
- **The Stripe returns did not know their workspace.** `withWorkspaceAuth` was already handing both billing routes `workspaceSlug` + `workspacePublicId`; nothing composed them, so a multi-workspace admin who upgraded their SECOND workspace was returned to the FIRST one's billing state and told nothing had happened.
- **The stale comment at `entitlements.ts`** claimed `/canvas` was on the retirement keep list and `/pricing` was being deleted. The plan says the opposite on both. It mattered more there than in most comments: those envelopes are followed literally by API-first clients, MCP agents included.

**`?redirectTo=` — TWO PRODUCTION BUGS IN `proxy.ts`, one of which the new page turns into lost revenue.**
- **The bounce dropped the query.** `url.searchParams.set("redirectTo", pathname)`. A signed-out `/billing/{segment}?billing=upgrade` came back a bare page and the checkout never opened — hitting FIRST-TIME PAYERS, whose browser has by definition never signed in. Now `pathname + search`, still validated on the way out by the same `safeRedirect` (which reassembles `pathname + search + hash` from a parsed URL and rejects anything whose origin moved, so a query cannot make a same-origin path off-origin). Consequence taken deliberately: a QUERY promotes even `/canvas` to a real destination, so a Stripe return landing in a signed-out tab survives the sign-in instead of being discarded as "that's just the default landing". And the login URL now carries exactly ONE param — the page's own query lives inside `redirectTo`, and a second copy beside it would be a second, ignored source of truth.
- **An already-signed-in visitor's `redirectTo` was ignored.** `/login?redirectTo=%2Finvite%2Ftok_x` with a live session 307'd to `/canvas` with the param left on the URL as decoration; the invite was never reached. Every producer of that URL is stating a destination, and having a session already is the reason the sign-in step can be SKIPPED, not a reason to discard where they were going. Validated through `explicitPostAuthTarget` — the same decision module the login form and `/auth/callback` use, never a re-implementation, because a value honoured by one layer and rejected by the next is how an open redirect gets built one helper at a time.
- **AND THE Q4 LOOP BREAKER HAD TO LEARN ITS OWN MIDPOINT.** The counter is disarmed by the first healthy authenticated page view, and the one page that must not disarm it is the page the bounce just chose. That was a hardcoded `/canvas`, correct only while `/canvas` was the sole possible destination. Honouring `redirectTo` makes it any page — and `/billing/{segment}` and `/get-started` both bounce back to `/login` NAMING THEMSELVES, which is the loop verbatim. The cookie now carries `"<count>|<pathname>"`; a legacy bare `"<count>"` still reads as `/canvas`, so cookies in flight across the deploy keep their old meaning. Without this, honouring `redirectTo` would have shipped an unbounded redirect loop reachable during exactly the GoTrue degradation the breaker exists for.

- Verification: root `npx vitest run` **2250** (baseline 2155; +95), SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **121** (baseline 115), desktop `npm test` **2510** (unchanged), `packages/mcp-server` **545** (unchanged), `npm run typecheck` clean, `tsc --noEmit -w @dopl/desktop-ui` clean, `npx next build` clean (`/billing` and `/billing/[segment]` both register as dynamic), SPA `vite build` clean, root eslint 0 errors / **2** pre-existing warnings (`proxy.ts` unused `options`, `use-boot-state.ts` unused disable directive — both measured at `a68c7d4` too; F-133's note of "1 warning" was already one short), desktop eslint 0/0.
- Red/green measured, not asserted, three times, each restored to green before the next: reverting the query carry fails **4**; reverting the signed-in honouring fails **10**; reverting ONLY the cookie's destination memory fails **2**, including the walk that otherwise never terminates.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live. Stripe is in LIVE mode and was not called at all: the one Stripe test fakes the SDK at the module boundary, so no session, customer or charge was created.
- **Deferred, with reasons.** (1) The workspace ICON UPLOADER (GAP-21, the other half of D4) stays out: it is workspace branding, not billing or account, and putting a branding editor on a payment page is scope the task did not ask for. It is now the only remaining reason to open web settings, so it needs its own decision — fold it in, port multipart over the bridge, or accept the loss. (2) No profile editor and no sign-out link on the page; the desktop app owns both, and §2.2's "Sign out for shared machines" belongs to the retired-page copy, not here. (3) `upgradeUrl()` stays workspace-AGNOSTIC (`/billing`, default-workspace forwarder) — its three callers hold an id, not a segment; the Stripe returns, which do hold one, now pass it.
- **Residual, now CLEARED by F-135: `src/proxy.ts` was 486 lines against the 500-line eslint cap.** The seam named here — the login-bounce breaker (cookie read/write/clear + `applyTarget`) into `shared/auth/login-bounce.ts` — was performed as its own commit ahead of the retirement branch, taking the file to 392 before it grew again. The reason for deferring it (the file's comments carry the argument for the loop the helpers implement) held: the whole Q4 block moved with the code.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, **unpushed**, unreleased. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-135: the website retires by redirect, behind a flag that defaults ON (2026-08-05, master)

- Location: `src/shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts` (new); `src/shared/auth/login-bounce.ts` (new, F-134's seam); `src/proxy.ts`; `src/proxy-retirement{,-billing,-keep}.test.ts` (new); `src/proxy{,-loop,-redirect-to}.test.ts`; `docs/ENGINEERING.md` §9.3.
- Found during: executing **Stage B** of `docs/migration-research/website-retirement-plan.md` §2.1 — the soft cutover.
- Severity: **feature + one latent redirect loop the flag would have created**.

**WHAT RETIRES, AND WHAT "RETIRE" MEANS HERE.** Every `[workspaceSlug]/(app)` page, `/canvas` and `/onboarding` 302 to `/get-started` when `WEBSITE_RETIRED` is on. **Nothing is deleted** — the pages are still in the tree, still in `next build`, and render again the moment the flag is off, which is what makes the rollback an env change rather than a revert. Stage D is a separate, saturation-gated PR and stays out of scope.

**THE FLAG DEFAULTS ON BECAUSE THE OFF SWITCH IS THE ONE THAT MUST BE DELIBERATE.** `WEBSITE_RETIRED` is only changeable in the Vercel dashboard, so the state an operator reaches by DELETING the variable has to be the safe one: a lost env var, a fresh preview deployment or a restored project must not quietly un-retire the website. `0`, `false` and `off` all turn it off — this is an incident lever typed under pressure, and a rollback that silently does nothing because someone wrote `false` is worse than one that is slightly loose. Read per request (`resolveDesktopFloor`'s injectable-`env` pattern, verbatim).

**THE MAP NAMES ITS PAGES, AND A TEST READS THE DIRECTORY TO PROVE IT.** `[workspaceSlug]` is a ROOT-level dynamic segment, so `/pricing` is `/{segment}` as far as a pattern can tell; "retire any second segment" would have taken the KEEP list down with the app tree. `APP_PAGES` is the `(app)` route table verbatim, `RESERVED_TOP_LEVEL` is the KEEP list as a guard, and `proxy-retirement.test.ts` readdir's `src/app/[workspaceSlug]/(app)/` and fails if a page exists that the map does not name. The bare `/{segment}` rule additionally requires the canonical `{slug}-{publicId}` shape — a legacy slug-only URL is indistinguishable from a top-level route nobody has written yet, so it renders as today and retires one hop later when its own redirect lands on `/{canonical}/canvas`.

**THE LEGACY BILLING INBOUND IS THE ONLY PART THAT COSTS MONEY WHEN IT IS WRONG.** `/{segment}/canvas?billing=…` is still produced by three things no deploy can reach: shipped desktop builds ≤ 1.8.5 (`open-in-browser.ts` built it until the D1 repoint), Stripe `return_url`s baked into sessions created before that repoint, and bookmarks. Those rewrite to `/billing/{segment}` with the query forwarded VERBATIM — byte for byte, because Stripe substitutes `{CHECKOUT_SESSION_ID}` and does not substitute `%7B…%7D` — BEFORE the generic redirect gets a look. The bare `/canvas?billing=…` form goes to the segment-less `/billing`, which resolves the default workspace, exactly the job `/canvas` was doing for it. A segment failing the charset check falls back to `/billing` rather than being refused: a mangled bookmark still reaches a payment surface. **Presence of `billing` is the test, not its value** — `parseBillingIntent` on the page is the authority, and a bookmark carrying junk after `billing=` is still someone trying to pay.

**A DEFECT THE FLAG WOULD HAVE SHIPPED: AN UNBOUNDED REDIRECT LOOP.** The signed-in default landing was `/canvas`, which is now retired, so `/login → /canvas → /get-started` made every lap of the Q4 cycle three hops instead of two. The bounce cookie records the midpoint the bounce CHOSE (`/canvas`), so arriving at `/get-started` read as "a healthy authenticated page view somewhere else" and DISARMED the counter — on every lap, forever. A page whose own `getUser()` then fails sends the browser back to `/login` with the counter at zero: the exact cycle the breaker exists to stop, re-created by a redirect that looked harmless. The default landing now follows the flag (`/get-started` while retired), which puts the midpoint back under the cookie. This is §9.2 rule 3 restated: any hop the middleware inserts between `/login` and the page the browser reaches moves the midpoint.

**THE BRANCH IS LAST AND SIGNED-IN ONLY**, so the flag cannot reach a KEEP route even if the map were wrong about one, and the `?redirectTo=` interaction composes instead of needing a second rule: a signed-out visitor to a retired page still bounces to `/login?redirectTo=<the page they asked for>`, the login form returns them to it, and THAT request is the one retired — including a first-time payer's `?billing=upgrade`, which arrives at checkout.

**`/join/` JOINED `PUBLIC_ROUTES`** (plan Stage A, GAP-6). The app copies `${getAppOrigin()}/join/{token}` to clipboards TODAY, so those URLs are in the wild permanently and their audience has no account by definition. `JoinLinkCard`'s `needsAuth` branch — which names the workspace and the inviter before asking anyone to sign in — was DEAD IN PRODUCTION because the session gate bounced every visitor to a login screen that could not say what they were joining. The join request itself is still auth-gated by its own route.

- Verification: root `npx vitest run` **2490/140** (baseline 2250/137; +240), desktop `npm test` **2510** (unchanged), SPA **121/22** (unchanged), `packages/mcp-server` **545** (unchanged), `npm run typecheck` clean, `npx next build` clean (all retired pages still register — `/canvas`, `/onboarding` and the whole `[workspaceSlug]` tree build exactly as before, which is the point), eslint 0 errors / **2** known warnings.
- Red/green measured, not asserted, twice, each restored to green: flipping the flag's DEFAULT to off fails **61** across 5 files, and `proxy-retirement-keep.test.ts` fails **0** of its 90 — the KEEP list is flag-independent by construction, which is the result that matters most. Reverting ONLY the default-destination line fails **41**, including four walks that report "did not terminate".
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live. No dev server, no browser, no Stripe call.
- **Known, accepted consequences.** (1) Desktop builds ≤ 1.8.5 open `/{segment}/canvas` with no query for "Delete account in browser" (plan §2.3); that is a plain retired page by the Stage B map, so those builds lose the browser account pane until they auto-update. Money was the flow that had to be protected, and it is. (2) `/{segment}/settings?billing=…` is NOT rewritten — no builder ever produced it; only `canvas` and `canvas2` are in the inbound set, per plan §2.1. (3) Pre-1.8 remote wrappers render `/get-started` inside their Electron window (plan §2.2's dual audience, risk R8) — copy review is Samuel's, not this session's.
- **Left alone deliberately.** `safeRedirect`'s `/canvas` fallback and `/onboarding`'s `redirect("/canvas")` (plan §2.4) still name a retired page. Both now resolve through the middleware in one extra hop and neither can loop, so repointing them is a code change with no behavioural gain tonight; it belongs with Stage D, when `/canvas` stops existing. `/invite/**` and `/admin/**` stay (D2/D3 undecided). Nothing was deleted anywhere.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, **unpushed**, unreleased, **flag not yet set in Vercel** (the code default is ON, so the flip happens at deploy — see the report). KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-136: the retirement ate the deep link the onboarding detour was carrying (2026-08-05, master)

- Location: `src/app/auth/callback/route.ts` (the detour, deleted); `src/shared/lib/url/website-retirement.ts` (`onboardingDestination`, new); `src/shared/lib/url/post-auth-landing.ts`, `src/features/auth/hooks/use-login.ts` (comments only); `src/first-run-deep-link.test.ts` (new); `src/app/auth/callback/route.test.ts`, `src/shared/lib/url/post-auth-landing.test.ts`, `src/proxy-retirement.test.ts`; `docs/ENGINEERING.md` §"The download goes THROUGH the account now".
- Found during: verifying F-135 live, signed in, against the shapes a first-time user actually arrives on.
- Severity: **bug — silent, unrecoverable, and it hit the MCP OAuth dance.**

**THE DEFECT WAS IN THE SEAM, NOT IN EITHER COMMIT.** F-130 kept the web-onboarding detour for deep-link arrivals (`!isDesktop && explicitTarget && !isOnboarded` → `/onboarding?redirectTo=<target>`), which was correct while `/onboarding` served. F-135 retired `/onboarding`, and the retirement hop REPLACES the query (`applyTarget`) — so the detour deleted the destination it existed to carry:

```
/auth/callback?code=…&redirectTo=%2Fjoin%2Ftok_x
  → 302 /onboarding?redirectTo=%2Fjoin%2Ftok_x   (callback)
  → 302 /get-started                              (middleware)
```

Verified live in production on all three shapes. Neither commit was wrong on its own; each was read against the other's behaviour instead of against the walk, and no test followed a browser across both layers. **That is the lasting lesson and the reason the new test file drives `GET` and `proxy()` in one file**: a handler-shaped assertion cannot see a seam-shaped bug.

**WHAT IT COST.** A never-onboarded arrival via an invite, a join link, an MCP OAuth consent screen or a password-reset link lost its destination. The OAuth case is the worst: the MCP client never receives its code, and **the retry fails identically**, because `onboarded_at` is only stamped inside the desktop app now — there was no state reachable from the web that could make a second attempt behave differently. A user could not recover by trying harder.

**THE FIX IS DELETION, NOT REPOINTING, AND THAT IS THE PRODUCT DECISION.** Web onboarding is retired; the desktop app owns onboarding. A detour into a retired page is a pointless hop by construction, so a valid `explicitTarget` now wins regardless of onboarding state. Safe because of WHAT these arrivals target: `/oauth/authorize`, `/invite/*`, `/join/*`, `/auth/reset-password`, `/billing/*` are all on the retirement KEEP list and **not one of them reads onboarding state**. The `isOnboarded` READ went with the branch — the callback no longer has an opinion about who has onboarded, which is the only way its destination can stop depending on a column the web cannot write (and it takes a query off every sign-in). Plain signups are untouched (`/get-started`), desktop is untouched (`?desktop=1` branches first), and a hostile `redirectTo` still reads as `null` and falls to the default — the validation was not touched, only the branch that used to hang off it.

**BELT AND SUSPENDERS, IMPLEMENTED: THE `/onboarding` RETIREMENT NOW CARRIES A VALIDATED `redirectTo`.** A search of the tree found the callback was that URL's ONLY producer — every other `/onboarding` push is bare (`canvas/page.tsx`, `[workspaceSlug]/(app)/page.tsx`, the two workspace-delete handlers), and no email or template emits one. So this is insurance, not a route: a 302 already in flight when the deploy lands, or a bookmark, still arrives, and the difference between honouring it and dropping it is one line in the map against one lost arrival. It reuses `explicitPostAuthTarget`, so a crafted value is not a destination; it cannot loop, because the value handed back was encoded INSIDE the URL it came from and the string strictly shortens every hop. **`/canvas` deliberately does not get it** — no producer ever put a `redirectTo` on it, and inventing a destination contract for a page on the RETIRE list would be new behaviour rather than the recovery of a lost one. It is also the only place in that map where a generic redirect reads the query, so `proxy-retirement.test.ts` names the exception where it states the rule.

- Red/green measured, not asserted: `src/first-run-deep-link.test.ts` fails **13 of 23** at `2838058` — all four arrival shapes at the callback layer, at the middleware layer, and end to end, plus the "never reads onboarding state" pin — and passes 23/23 after. The 10 that were green before and after are the ones that had to be: the hostile matrix, the plain signup, the desktop handoff, the loop proof.
- Verification: root `npx vitest run` **2517/141** (baseline 2490/140; +27), `npm run typecheck` clean, `npx next build` clean (`/onboarding` still registers — Stage B deletes nothing, and this changes who is SENT there, not what is served), `npx eslint` 0 errors / **2** known warnings. Desktop, SPA and `packages/mcp-server` not run — untouched.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- Status: resolved; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-137: P1-8 shipped the await-cursor fix INVERTED, losing more messages than the bug it fixed (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

- Location: `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-ops-read.ts` (the thread-scoped read's watch hint) + `channel-thread-scope.test.ts`.
- Found by: a **counterparty's agent**, in production, during the very exchange that was testing channels — it read the emitted hint against the `await` schema and refuted it. Not by any test, review pass, or verification agent, all of which had accepted P1-8.
- **The inversion.** `await` is `gt("seq", since)`, so a LARGER `since` returns FEWER rows. The complaint P1-8 answered was real (the hint handed back the THREAD max, so rows below it in other exchanges were never delivered), but the remedy went the wrong way: it suggested the CHANNEL max instead. The rows in `(threadMax, channelMax]` are exactly the other exchanges this reader has not seen — awaiting from the thread max DELIVERS them, awaiting from the channel max DROPS them, permanently. P1-8 therefore caused the loss its own docblock existed to prevent, and its hint asserted the claim inverted ("passing the thread-local N would skip everything between the two").
- **Why it passed review.** The symptom was verified (prose warns, then interpolates the warned-about number) but the DIRECTION of the remedy was never checked against the comparison operator. Two agents and one adversarial verification pass reproduced the reasoning without re-deriving it. A test was written that pinned the inverted behavior as correct, which converted the mistake into a defended invariant.
- **The fix (P1-8b).** Neither number is a cursor: a safe `since` is the highest seq below which the reader has seen EVERYTHING channel-wide, and a filtered page cannot establish that bound by construction. The hint now offers NO number, states that the read did not advance a channel-wide cursor, and names the unscoped read that can establish one. The channel-head round-trip P1-8 added is removed with it.
- **The real elevation this argues for:** a `thread` filter on `await` (or an opaque resume token), so "watch MY exchange" is expressible instead of approximated by an integer whose scoping the agent cannot see. Open.
- Status: **resolved**; the inverted tests were replaced with ones asserting no number is emitted.

### F-138: "I set bypass/automatic and it still asks me" — THREE mechanisms, one symptom, and why every previous fix failed (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/session-state.js` (the timer bounds + `idleTimeout`), `session-reducer.js` (`idle_timeout`, the new `abandon_timeout`), `session-effects.js` (`parkEffects` options), `session-engine.js` (`scheduleIdle`), `session-profiles.js` (`isOwnChannelRead` + the Axis-B read branch), `session-gate-reason.js` (three codes), `session-io.js` (the diag line's `op=`), `renderer/session/session-labels.js` + `session-render.js` (the copy, §2 split).
- Found by: **Samuel, from his own production diag log**, after three previous attempts to fix this had each landed, been verified, and not stuck.

**WHY IT KEPT COMING BACK.** Three independent mechanisms produce the identical operator experience. Each previous fix diagnosed one, removed it, and shipped — and the symptom survived, so the fix read as ineffective and the next session started from scratch on a different one. Naming all three at once is the actual finding.

- **M1 — THE ROOT CAUSE, and Samuel identified it himself: a session waiting on the peer was treated as idle and parked.** Every turn end emitted `scheduleIdle`, including the turn that had just posted — the one the reducer labels `activity: 'awaiting_peer'` two lines earlier. `DEFAULT_IDLE_MS` is 15 minutes; a counterparty's agent doing real work routinely takes longer. So the exchange parked MID-FLIGHT, the park reset the posture, the peer's reply then woke it, and the next tool call asked. The state distinction already existed and simply was not consulted where the timer was scheduled, and `nextIdleMs` had carried the comment "a hook for phase-aware backoff later" since it was written.
  - **Decision: a far longer bound, not suppression.** Suppressing while `awaiting_peer` leaves a live SDK query, its push iterator and its awaited `canUseTool` promises alive FOREVER when the reply never comes (peer offline, their agent crashed, thread abandoned) — an unbounded resource commitment with nobody watching, which is the class of thing the idle timer exists to bound. `AWAITING_PEER_IDLE_MS` = **4 h**: ~16x the TTL and one to two orders of magnitude above a real agent turn, so it cannot fire inside a live exchange, while "nothing runs unattended forever" still holds.
- **M2 — THE POSTURE LIFETIME CONTRACT.** Samuel: "if I change it mid task, or I set it, for the rest of the session it shouldn't revert back. It needs to behave as I have set it" — confirmed to include the for-task grants. The idle park no longer clears `toolMode`, `messageMode`, `inboundForTask` or `allowForTask`. This REVERSES v2.3 FIX #3, MEDIUM-3/C9 and v2.9 FIX F1. Their reasoning was not wrong, it was mis-sited: all three answer an AWAY threat, and the 15-minute park is a poor proxy for away.
  - **The threat is answered twice, better.** (1) **A genuinely abandoned session ENDS.** The park arms `ABANDONED_MS` (**12 h** — 48x the park TTL, 3x the peer bound, so a session parked over lunch is still there and one parked at end of day ends overnight); `abandon_timeout` on a parked session sets `phase: 'ended'`, which is terminal at the top of the reducer. Terminal beats disarmed: an ended session cannot be woken by a peer at all, where the old downgrade left it dormant AND wakeable. A later peer reply recreates a dormant shell from the durable record at manual/ask, exactly like every other spawn nobody approved (FIX 1b). (2) **The real boundary was always the PROFILE** — the hard-deny set is checked first in `grantDecision` and no posture, no grant and not `bypass` can widen it. F1's actual defect was that the header and the state DISAGREED (reset posture beside a surviving grant); consistency fixes that as well as clearing did, and honestly.
  - **The AUTH HOLD still resets, and is now the only park that does** — a session with no credential relaunches through `startQuery` on sign-in rather than resuming in place, so its arm belongs to the run that ended. It arms no abandonment timer: ending it would destroy the window carrying the Sign in button.
- **M3 — read-only channel ops gated in every posture, which is incoherent.** Axis B auto-allowed exactly one shape, an own-channel POST, so posting into the session's own channel ran with no card while READING that same channel asked — the more dangerous op was the permitted one. Samuel's log, with both axes wide open: `06:47:05 session gate: dopl_channel gate channel-op-approval-required tool=bypass msg=auto_both`. The own-channel READ-ONLY ops (`read`, `await`, `list_threads`, `get_thread`, `members`, `agents`) now follow the INBOUND half of Axis B — a read sends nothing; it brings the peer's words into this agent's context, which is what `auto_inbound` consents to. `auto_outbound` alone does not cover them. Everything that changes the room or reaches past it still gates in every posture (`open`, `invite`, `create_thread`, `propose_close`/`close_thread`, `set_thread_mode`, `join_thread`/`leave_thread`, `milestone`, the agent-lifecycle ops, any cross-channel post or read including a slug-addressed one, and `list` — read-only but not own-channel-scoped).
- **DIAGNOSABILITY (why this took archaeology).** The gate line named the tool and the reason but never the OP, so `dopl_channel gate channel-op-approval-required` read identically for a read, an invite and a DM open. It now carries `op=<name>` on channel calls only — the op name, sanitized, never a body/recipient/channel — plus three new reason codes (`read-approval-required`, `cross-channel-read`, `auto-inbound-read`) so "reads are never auto-run" stopped being asserted once it stopped being true.

- **§2 splits this forced** (all three files were at or one line under the 500 cap): the gate-reason COPY moved `session-render.js` → `session-labels.js` (re-exported verbatim, every caller unchanged); the timer truth table moved `session-posture-sticks.test.mjs` → `test/session-idle-bounds.test.mjs`; the channel read half moved `session-permission-axes.test.mjs` → `test/session-channel-read.test.mjs`. `session-state.js` absorbed the whole timer decision (`idleTimeout(state) -> {ms, type}`) so `session-engine.scheduleIdle` grew by one line rather than gaining a second timer — one handle means every existing teardown path is still correct.
- **Existing tests changed deliberately, never deleted** (9 were red on the behaviour change, all of them pinning park-resets-everything): `session-reducer-park`, `session-permission-axes`, `session-permission-hardening` (the F1 SOURCE pin, inverted — the same defect class pointed the other way), `session-preset-start` (a pin that would have matched the auth-hold branch and passed vacuously), `inbound-gate`, `session-posture-sticks`, `session-replay-modes`. Each carries a comment naming the requirement change.
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2538** (baseline 2510; +28), desktop eslint **0/0**; root `npx vitest run` **2518** (unchanged), `npm run typecheck` clean, root eslint 0 errors / 2 known warnings; `packages/mcp-server` **545** (unchanged). Red/green proved per mechanism by reverting each fix in isolation: M1 → 2 fail, M2 → 13 fail, M3 → 6 fail (+1 hang, the `no dispatch` await pattern that file already uses), the diag `op=` → 2 fail.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- **Open, for Samuel to decide** (each is a judgement call, not a defect): the two bounds (4 h / 12 h) are first picks; an abandonment end DESTROYS the session window (`settle`), which is the honest state but is visible; an abandonment posts NO channel lifecycle, so the peer's card keeps pulsing exactly as a park already leaves it; and `await` was included in the read set on the argument that gating "wait for the peer" is the same incoherence M3 removes.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing).

- **M2b (2026-08-05, Samuel's call): an abandonment KEEPS its window.** The end is what stops a peer waking a session; the window is UI. Every other end is the tail of something the operator watched happen (they clicked End; a cap fired while they were there), so tidying its window away completes an action they took. An abandonment fires hours later with nobody present — destroying that window makes a transcript vanish from the desktop of someone who only stepped away, indistinguishable from a crash, and it is the one end they never saw. `endEffects` stamps `keepWindow` on the settle effect for `reason === 'abandoned'` and `session-engine.settle` guards only the `win.destroy()`; the phase, the permission denials, the iterator close, the abort and the map delete are untouched. Pinned both ways in `test/session-idle-bounds.test.mjs` (abandonment keeps, operator End still closes).

- **M3 AMENDED by F-139 M4 (2026-08-05):** `propose_close` and `milestone` left the always-gated list and follow the OUTBOUND half of Axis B. The M3 reasoning is intact; those two ops were on the wrong side of it. See F-139.

### F-139: the session gate hardcoded ONE MCP server name, so on a different client every Dopl tool was unclassified — and a hard-denied one was merely clickable (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

- Location: `dopl-desktop-app/main/mcp-tool-names.js` (**new**), `main/session-profiles.js` (`isChannelTool`, `grantDecision`, `toolModeAllows`, the new `isOwnChannelMarker`), `main/session-gate-reason.js` (the canonical name + the marker codes), `main/session-io.js` (`shortToolLabel`). Tests: `test/session-tool-name-prefix.test.mjs` (**new**, split out of `session-permission-hardening.test.mjs` at the §2 cap), plus `session-channel-read`, `session-gate-reason`, `session-permission-hardening`, `session-permission-axes`, and the two source-extraction injectors (`session-profiles`, `sdk-grant`).
- Found by: **Samuel, from production diag logs on TWO machines** — the fourth mechanism behind one symptom, and the first one visible only by comparing a working machine to a broken one.

**THE DIAGNOSIS, CONFIRMED.** Two machines on 1.8.6, identical and fully permissive postures, opposite behaviour:

```
working  session gate: dopl_channel op=read allow auto-inbound-read tool=bypass msg=auto_both
```
