```
broken   session gate: mcp__claude_ai_Dopl__dopl_channel gate unclassified-tool tool=bypass msg=auto_both
```

The only difference is the MCP **server segment**. `mcp__dopl__` is what OUR registration produces (`sdk-loader` keys the in-memory server `dopl`); the claude.ai connector exposes the SAME server as `mcp__claude_ai_Dopl__`, and other clients use a UUID (`mcp__6a12c8bd-4187-40eb-9b21-eb230264f726__`, observed live). `isChannelTool` stripped exactly one hardcoded prefix, so for any other server name `short` stayed the full dotted name, neither test hit, the call skipped Axis B, fell to Axis A, was in no positive allow-list, and gated as `unclassified-tool` **in every posture, `bypass` included**. No setting the operator could reach would ever fix it, which is why it presented exactly like a posture that did not stick and survived several rounds of "fix the permissions".

**THE FULL AUDIT — every prefix-dependent matcher, what was broken and what was already fine.** The coordinator's prediction was right and understated it: it is one bug in four places, and the fourth is the dangerous one.

| Matcher | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| `isChannelTool` (Axis-B branch, grant keys, `isOutboundPost`, the diag `op=`) | **BROKEN** — the reported defect. Every channel call under a foreign prefix. |
| `AUTO_TOOLS` / `BYPASS_TOOLS` via `toolModeAllows` (built from `DOPL_READ_TOOLS`/`DOPL_WRITE_TOOLS`) | **BROKEN** — exact `indexOf` on `mcp__dopl__…` literals, so `auto`/`bypass` covered no Dopl tool on that machine; all of them gated as `unclassified-tool` too. |
| `cfg.preApproved` (`dopl_only`'s pre-approved read half) | **BROKEN** — a connector-named `dopl_search` was not pre-approved, so `dopl_only`'s whole point (headless-usable reads) missed. |
| `cfg.disallowedTools` — **the deny direction, answered explicitly** | **BROKEN, and this is the SECURITY half.** `SESSION_HARD_DENY` names the six `dopl_*_admin` tools as `mcp__dopl__…` literals. Measured before the fix: `mcp__claude_ai_Dopl__dopl_kb_admin` → `{"decision":"gate","reason":"unclassified-tool"}` where `mcp__dopl__dopl_kb_admin` → `deny`. **A denied tool CAN be renamed past its deny entry by a different server prefix.** It degrades `deny` → `gate`, not `deny` → `allow`, so nothing runs silently — but the hard-deny set exists precisely so that no click, no `allowForTask` and no `bypass` can open it ("checked FIRST so a denied tool can never be opened"), and one operator Allow on `dopl_kb_admin` runs `delete_base`/`delete_folder`/`delete_file`. `read_only`'s containment lost the same way (it denies every Dopl tool by name). Both are `deny` under every prefix now, pinned across all three profiles even with a matching grant key held. |
| `session-gate-reason.toolReason` (`BYPASS_TOOLS.indexOf`) | **BROKEN** — would have reported `unclassified-tool` for a tool the fixed gate covers. The explanation and the decision were wrong in lockstep; both read the canonical name now. |
| `session-io.shortToolLabel` (diag label) | **BROKEN** — the regex was `/^mcp__[a-z0-9-]+?__/i`, a server class with **no underscores**, so `claude_ai_Dopl` stripped to nothing and the log printed the full dotted name. Exactly what the broken machine's line shows. |
| `session-grant-keys.grantKeyFor` (`Bash` / `WEB_SCOPED` / `EDIT_TOOLS` arms) | **ALREADY FINE** — built-ins, never prefixed. Its channel arm rode `isChannelTool` and is fixed with it. The key deliberately keeps the RAW tool name: a grant is scoped to the shape the operator saw, server included. |
| `tool-profiles.js` — `buildAllowedTools` / `buildDeniedTools` / `buildBuiltinTools`, the HEADLESS path | **ALREADY FINE, and for a reason worth keeping.** Those lists are CLI argv for a spawn that also passes `--strict-mcp-config` with our own `--mcp-config`, so only the server WE named as `dopl` loads and no foreign prefix can exist. (The `full` profile emits no restriction flags at all, hence no `--strict-mcp-config` — but headless `full` also passes no `--allowedTools`, and a TTY-less CLI auto-denies anything not pre-approved, so there is no hole there either.) |
| `doplToolsPolicy` / `shortDoplName` | **ALREADY FINE** — they transform OUR OWN constants into server-local names for the MCP `tools` policy; they never classify an incoming name. |

**THE FIX.** `main/mcp-tool-names.js` (new, pure, 75 lines) owns two functions and the whole argument. `mcpShortName` = the segment after the **LAST** `__` (MCP tool names do not contain `__`; server names can, so greedy yields the tool half for every real name and over-matches only a pathological one). `canonicalDoplName` rewrites the **known Dopl vocabulary** onto `mcp__dopl__<short>` and returns everything else untouched. `grantDecision` normalizes ONCE at the top and hard-deny, pre-approval and Axis A all use that one value; `isChannelTool` matches by short name under any server; the explainer and the diag label read the same helpers.

- **Why over-matching stays safe.** Axis B is the STRICTER axis — only an own-channel post (M3's read set, M4's markers) auto-allows and every other shape gates in every posture — and hard-deny is checked BEFORE the channel branch either way, so nothing can be demoted out of `deny` by landing in Axis B. Misrouting an unrelated tool INTO Axis B makes it ask; the shipped behaviour misrouted the real tool OUT of Axis B into "unclassified", which also asks. Neither direction is unsafe; only one lets the operator's posture work at all.
- **The residual risk, assessed.** A third-party MCP server exposing a tool whose short name is literally one of ours (`dopl_channel`, `dopl_kb`, or the two generic-sounding workspace metas) inherits this table's classification for that name. **The trade is right because it hands an attacker nothing they did not already have:** registering an MCP server on this machine is a config write or code execution, and anyone with either can simply register their server UNDER THE NAME `dopl`, which the old literal matcher trusted just as completely. A server name was never a secret and never a bound. Every tighter bound considered fails the case that caused this — an allow-list of known server names re-breaks on the next client, and requiring the server segment to look Dopl-ish re-breaks the UUID form already seen in the field.
- **The diagnostic hole had the same root, and resolves with it (confirmed).** The gate line prints `op=` only for a call that CLASSIFIES as the channel tool, so on the broken machine the op was absent from every line — the bug hid its own diagnosis. Pinned: under all three server names the line is now byte-identical to the working machine's.

**M4 — THE SECOND, INDEPENDENT DEFECT: the operator was asked twice for one decision.** On Samuel's machine (where the prefix matching works), `bypass` + `auto_both`: `09:28:44 session gate: dopl_channel op=propose_close gate channel-op-approval-required`. Once to permit the agent's CALL, then again to confirm the close. **The second prompt is the feature and is untouched.** The first bought nothing: `propose_close` closes nothing at all — it posts a marked note and surfaces a confirmable prompt, the thread stays open, routing and live until the human acts, and the server refuses an agent-token `close_thread` outright. It is strictly LESS powerful than the own-channel `post` that `auto_both` already auto-allows.

- **`milestone` decided the same way, deliberately and not silently.** It is a one-line marker into this session's own thread; it addresses nobody, notifies nobody and carries no deliverable. Its only effect is agent-authored text in a channel where an auto-allowed `post` already puts arbitrary text with more reach, so the marginal exfil surface over `post` is **zero** — and `prompt-framing.js` actively instructs the agent to log milestones, so gating it is the product asking permission for what it just ordered. Deciding it the other way would have left M3's incoherence one op along: the more powerful op permitted, the weaker one asking.
- **The axis is OUTBOUND** (`auto_outbound`/`auto_both`), because both put CONTENT into the shared channel — statements that leave this machine. `auto_inbound` alone does not reach them; M3's read set went to the inbound half for the mirror-image reason.
- **Scope: channel, on the same footing as `isOwnChannelPost`** — absent/empty means this session's own, and a `channel` naming anything else (a slug included) gates. The THREAD is not scoped: `grantArgs` carries `channelId` and not `taskId`, and the blast radius of a wrong thread id is a confirm prompt the operator reads, inside the channel they are already bound to. **Flagged for Samuel rather than widened or silently tightened.**
- **`close_thread` gates unconditionally**, pinned across every `toolMode` × `messageMode` pair, and is never conflated with its proposal.
- Reason codes: the two marker ops SHARE the post codes on the GATE side (`message-approval-required`, `cross-channel-post`) because the fact and the operator's fix are identical, and get their own `auto-outbound-marker` on the ALLOW side, where the audit question is "what left this machine with no click?" and "the agent proposed a close" is not the same answer as "the agent sent a message".

- **§2 splits this forced:** `main/mcp-tool-names.js` lifted the normalizers out of `session-profiles.js` (which would otherwise have hit 534), and `test/session-tool-name-prefix.test.mjs` lifted the F-139 truth table out of `session-permission-hardening.test.mjs` (518 → 441). The two source-extraction tests inject the two normalizers exactly as they already inject `makeGrantKeyFor`.
- **Existing tests changed deliberately, never deleted** (3 were red on the behaviour change, each carrying a comment naming the requirement change): `session-permission-hardening`'s F3 matcher test asserted `mcp__other__dopl_channel` is NOT the channel tool — it pinned the mistake, and F3's own comment already argued the opposite rule; `session-channel-read`'s ALWAYS_GATED list lost `propose_close`/`milestone`; `session-permission-axes`' source pin follows the canonical name.
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2551** (baseline 2539; +12), root `npx vitest run` **2518** (unchanged), `npm run typecheck` clean, root eslint 0 errors / 2 known warnings, `packages/mcp-server` **545** (unchanged). Red/green proved per fix by reverting each in isolation: `isChannelTool` → 3 fail, the `grantDecision` canonicalization → 4 fail, the M4 marker branch → 2 fail, the diag label → 2 fail.
- **Desktop eslint is 1 error, and it is NOT this work:** `main/session-engine.js` is 503 lines against the 500 cap, having gone 497 → 503 in commit a6d9122 with lint not re-run. Every file this change touches is at or under the cap. Left for a split of its own rather than trimmed inside a security fix.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- **Open, for Samuel to decide:** (1) the marker ops are CHANNEL-scoped, not thread-scoped (above); (2) the session path does not set the SDK's `strictMcpConfig`, so the operator's own MCP servers — including a claude.ai Dopl connector — load into every session window alongside ours, which is the mechanism that produced the two Dopl servers in the first place. Everything foreign gates, and the gate is now correct either way, so this is a product question (does a session window want the operator's other servers?) rather than a defect; turning it on would also remove the duplicate-Dopl-tool confusion at the source.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-140: three sender-side outcomes for one action, because the app could not prove its own UI posted (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

Phase 1 of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§3.4 + sequencing item 1). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "ONE INITIATING BEHAVIOUR, and the `desktop-ui` stamp"**; the F-119 requester-shell bullet is struck through and points here.

**THE DEFECT, which was one missing fact and not three product decisions.** Posting a request produced three different outcomes on the SENDER'S machine depending on how it was posted: a desktop-spawned session's `create_thread` opened a full requester session; the operator TYPING the same request in the app's own composer opened a dormant SHELL (window, agent not started); an external MCP session's create opened nothing. Only the first and third were designed. The middle one existed because the app's UI posted from a browser context — cookies, no `X-Dopl-Runtime` header — so the server stamped no `metadata.runtime` and the post was indistinguishable on the wire from an external agent's create. `requesterShellOpen` had to route on `authorKind`, which is CALLER-ASSERTED, and something inert was the most that weak evidence could safely buy. The code said so itself (`targeting.js:355+`): *"only a shell — which starts nothing, consumes nothing and can be evicted — is safe to open on that evidence."*

**THE FIX WAS TO MAKE THE EVIDENCE TRUSTWORTHY, NOT TO LOOSEN THE PREDICATE.**

- **The stamp.** `main/ui-bridge.js` `sendApiRequest` attaches `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-ui`. That is the ONE transport the bundled SPA has: `renderer/app-preload.js` takes a path and a small typed options object and exposes NO header surface, main builds every header, and every handler is bound to that window's own top frame — so the stamp cannot ride a request the renderer did not originate, and a new call site cannot forget it. `main/api.js` / `listener-io.js` / `channel-post.js` are deliberately untouched: those are the listener and session lanes, not the operator typing.
- **The honesty bound, and what the claim is actually worth.** A header cannot attest who called; the API is public and the retired website is not the only thing that can hold a cookie. What the server CAN do is refuse a claim the credential does not support. `desktop-ui` claims a PERSON typing in the app, which is a first-party SESSION credential by construction, so `narrowRuntime(value, {agentCredential})` refuses it for any `dopl_at_*` token — every remote-MCP caller, every external Claude Code session, every device-token script — while leaving `desktop-session` credential-agnostic, because a spawned session authenticates with exactly that device token and always has. Applied once in `with-workspace-auth.ts` (so every consumer of `ctx.runtime` inherits it) and re-applied in `buildChannelContext` through the same predicate; `readRuntimeHeader` deliberately does NOT apply it, because it reports the CLAIM and that is what lets `/api/mcp` forward a caller's value onto the loopback where the token is known. **Stated exactly: the stamp is worth "a first-party session credential declaring its own surface", and no more.** It stays a ROUTING HINT — the security gate is unchanged and is the identity pair (`authorUserId === me` AND `taskCreatedBy === me`), which no peer can forge.
  - **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 (F-145): the bound is on the `desktop-ui` VALUE, not on the window.** This bullet originally read as though the bound closed the requester window to agent credentials, and the source docblock said so outright ("an external MCP post can never acquire this stamp no matter what header it sends"). That is true of `desktop-ui` and false of the WINDOW: `targeting.requesterTaskOpen` accepts EITHER stamp (`DESKTOP_RUNTIMES`), and `desktop-session` is credential-agnostic by design — so a `dopl_at_*` holder sending `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-session` on a `create_thread` of its own clears the stamp conjunct and, with the identity pair, opens a window and starts an agent on that account's Mac. **Pre-existing and NOT a regression** — before this change there was no credential bound at all, so Phase 1 tightened it — and **not a peer's path**, because the identity pair holds. It is the account's own credential acting as the account. **The real boundary is TOKEN CUSTODY**, and it is documented rather than "fixed": bounding `desktop-session` would refuse the exact caller it was invented for (a desktop-spawned session authenticates with that device token). Corrected in `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts`, `with-workspace-auth.test.ts`'s docblock and its `desktop-session` case, and in F-144's CASE 3 / consent-posture paragraphs.
- **The launch.** `targeting.requesterTaskOpen` accepts either stamp (`DESKTOP_RUNTIMES`), so the operator's typed request takes the SAME route to `launchRequesterSession` — window plus running agent, `goal` = the operator's own words.

**THE SHELL IS DELETED, NOT DISABLED — and that was checked, not assumed.** `requesterShellOpen`, listener route (4) `maybeOpenRequesterShell` and `session-park.openRequesterShell` had exactly one caller each, in a chain that started at the deleted predicate. The pinned-shell machinery they BORROWED (`parkedShell: true` and the `recreateParkedShell` / `openFromChannel` / `startResume` / `session-team` paths behind the session-engine PINNED SHELL comments) is untouched and still has its own callers, so nothing about reopen or adoption changed. **The one piece kept: the request lifecycle strip.** It was the shell's, but the question it answers outlives the shell — Accept and Decline reach this machine as `task_started` / `task_failed` MILESTONES and every listener route gates on `kind === 'message'`, so the running agent never sees either. `armRequestStatus` became slot-keyed (its caller is the route, which holds the slot, not the registry entry) and idempotent (a message read twice must not walk `accepted` back to `sent`), and it is armed for `desktop-ui` ONLY, so the desktop-session path is byte-for-byte what it was.

**A SIDE EFFECT WORTH NAMING: `authorKind` now decides nothing here.** The deleted predicate was its only reader on this path. An unstamped create opens nothing whether it declares `user` or `agent` — strictly stricter than before, and the point.

- Location: `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts` (rewritten: `DESKTOP_UI_RUNTIME`, `narrowRuntime`), `src/shared/auth/with-workspace-auth.ts:215`, `src/features/channels/server/service-shared.ts:73`, `src/features/channels/server/service-writes-metadata.ts:378`, `dopl-desktop-app/main/ui-bridge.js:59`/`:189`, `main/targeting.js:302-390`, `main/session-dispatch.js:103-183`, `main/listener-messages.js:70-82`, `main/session-park.js:244-300`, `main/session-engine.js:491`.
- Found during: rollback plan Phase 1.
- Severity: bug (three outcomes for one user action) + smell (a routing decision resting on a caller-asserted field).
- Verification: desktop `npm test` **2552** (baseline 2551) + desktop eslint clean; root `npx vitest run` **2532** (baseline 2518; +14) + `tsc --noEmit` clean + root eslint 0 errors / 2 known warnings; `packages/mcp-server` **545** (unchanged). Red/green: `test/operator-typed-request.test.mjs` (the rewritten shell suite) fails on the old code at "the operator's typed request opens a full requester session" and at "the shell predicate is GONE"; `test/thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs` Q3b DIRECTION 3 was inverted from "still opens the requester shell" to "takes that same route, plus the strip"; the forged-stamp bound is `with-workspace-auth.test.ts` "an AGENT-TOKEN caller sending the same header gets NO stamp".
- **CASE 3 IS PINNED ON PURPOSE.** An unstamped external create must still open nothing until rollback §3.5 lands an explicit handoff op that declares ownership. `operator-typed-request.test.mjs` and `thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs` both fail if that drifts.
- **Known gaps, both dev-only, both fail-safe.** `DOPL_UI=remote` (the legacy wrapper, which loads the retired website through Chromium's cookie jar) and `npm run dev` in a plain browser (fetch transport, no bridge) send no header, so a request typed there opens nothing rather than a shell. Neither is a shipping surface; the failure is "no window", never a wrong one.
- **Desktop version NOT bumped** — the rollback phases release together.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- **Waiting on Phase 2:** nothing in this change blocks the named-agent / engagement / `to_agent` / breakout-room deletions, and none of those surfaces was invested in here. ### F-141: named agents, engagement and breakout rooms — a whole model deleted rather than fixed (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

Phase 2 of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§1 + sequencing item 2). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "PHASE 2 OF THE ROLLBACK"**. Follows F-140 (Phase 1); §3.3–§3.5 are the replacement and are NOT in this change.

**WHY A RIP-OUT AND NOT A DEPRECATION.** Three defects were queued as fixes against this surface and every one of them evaporates with it: engagement died silently on restart (the "still engaged" state lived in an in-process map, so a restart collapsed it to a stale server floor and untagged messages were dropped with no signal); two machines disagreed about whether an agent was engaged (same root); the web chip read Idle while the desktop worked (it read the server floor only, and the chip is being replaced). `dopl_channel` is a public surface, but Samuel and one peer are its only real users, so a deprecation window bought nothing.

**WHAT WENT, in one sentence per lane.** SERVER: `service-agents.ts`, `service-participants.ts`, `repository-participants.ts`, `service-thread-handshake.ts`, `service-writes-agents.ts`, `schema-agents.ts`, the `agents/[agentId]` and `tasks/[taskId]/participants` routes, six error classes and their HTTP arms. MCP: seven ops, five params, `channel-ops-agents.ts`, `channel-agent-refs.ts`, `channel-render-agents.ts`, `channel-handshake-key.ts`, the `_callerAgent` locus plumbing, and ~2.5k characters of law. `@dopl/client`: `channel-agents.ts`, `ChannelAgentsClient`, `ChannelThreadDetail`. WEB: the chips bar, the `@`-mention machinery, `/new-agent` and the whole slash-command surface, `agent-engagement.ts`, `thread-agents.ts`, `rooms.ts`. DESKTOP: eight modules, two `classify` rules, one realtime channel, `THE_LAW`, `buildTeamTurn`, the session window's `@` picker, and `test/live/` in full.

**FOUR DECISIONS WORTH THE INK.**

1. **The removed PARAMS are refused; the removed OPS are absent.** Deleting `toAgent` / `toAgents` / `authorAgentId` / `participants` from the route schemas would have been SILENT — zod strips unknown keys — so an old client's `to_agent` would post successfully and address nobody, which is precisely the invisible-delivery failure the addressing contract existed to prevent. They are declared `z.never()` with a message naming the replacement (`schema.ts#removedParam`), so absence stamps nothing and presence is a 400 on the field. The MCP tool does the opposite and drops its seven ops from the enum, because a capability that is genuinely gone should answer "invalid enum value"; `close_thread` stays in the enum for exactly the contrasting reason — its capability MOVED to `propose_close` and its refusal names where.
2. **No destructive migration. Writes stopped, rows did not move.** `channel_agents` and `channel_task_participants` keep every row and every column. The three agent metadata keys stay in `resolvePostMetadata`'s reserved STRIP list with NO writer left, and that is load-bearing rather than tidy: `author_agent_id` on a stored row is what the web transcript reads to render "quartz · Ada's agent", so a caller who could set it on a NEW post could attribute their own words to somebody's retired agent.
3. **Historical attribution splits by SURFACE, and the split is deliberate.** The WEB keeps it: `GET /api/channels/[channelId]/agents` survives GET-only, `listAgents` moved into `service-reads.ts` (it is no longer a lifecycle question), `repository-agents.ts` is one read and no writes, and dismissed rows are included because the agents most likely to own old messages are the retired ones. The DESKTOP does not: `channel-roster.js` is deleted and `session-dispatch.authorLabel` degrades from "quartz (Ada's agent)" to "Ada's agent". That string feeds an AGENT'S PROMPT, not a human reading history, and the distinction it exists for — person or machine — survives intact; the cost it was paying was a roster GET per watched channel per reconcile pass.
4. **`test/live/` is deleted rather than rewritten.** All nine contract checks were built on summoning two agents into a throwaway channel and addressing them. A harness that 404s on its first call reads as a broken product rather than a removed feature, and a rewritten-but-unrunnable one (it needs a credential and the network, which this session had neither of) would be worse. **Phase 3 must rebuild it**; ENGINEERING §18 keeps the record of its design so the rebuild inherits rather than re-derives it.

**THE LATER-CLEANUP MIGRATION LIST — nothing here was run.** In dependency order, once the rows are genuinely not wanted: (a) `channel_agents.engaged_at` / `engaged_by` and the index on them — no reader anywhere, and the first safe drop; (b) `channel_task_participants` in full (table, its workspace-consistency trigger and its RLS policies) — nothing reads it now that `mayWriteThread` is gone; (c) `channel_agents` from the `supabase_realtime` publication — the doorbell that consumed it is deleted, so it publishes to nobody; (d) `channel_agents.status` — the DTO stopped mapping it, only attribution reads the row; (e) `channel_agents` itself and `channel_messages.metadata.author_agent_id`, LAST and only once historical attribution stops mattering, because dropping the table is what finally makes an old agent-authored message anonymous. Each is a separate migration and each is Samuel's to run.

**AMBIGUOUS, AND DELIBERATELY LEFT.**
- **The room-vs-pair SESSION SLOT SHAPE** — `agentId` beside `taskId` in a session key, `bind: 'room'` accepted and ignored — is inert (nothing sets `agentId`) and survives in `session-store` / `session-park` / `session-engine` / `session-pool`. Removing it is surgery on the park/abandon machinery this rollback was under orders not to disturb; §3.3's session pills may want a slot dimension anyway.
- **`address-picker.tsx` had no agent half to delete.** It is a MEMBER picker end to end. The scope named it, and the honest answer is that the agent half lived in `mention.ts` / `mention-popup.tsx`, which are deleted. The picker stays whole until Phase 4 replaces the composer control.
- **`bridge.sendToPeer` stays wired** in the session preload with no renderer caller, because §3.2 brings the capability back as a pill in the same window.
- **`agent_presence` is untouched**, per scope — its retirement is sequencing item 7 and the plan says to MEASURE the delta rather than assume it.

- Location: **server** — deleted `src/features/channels/schema-agents.ts`, `server/service-agents.ts`, `server/service-participants.ts`, `server/repository-participants.ts`, `server/service-thread-handshake.ts`, `server/service-writes-agents.ts`, `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/agents/[agentId]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/channels/[channelId]/tasks/[taskId]/participants/route.ts`; rewritten `schema.ts` (`removedParam`), `server/service-writes.ts` (one write; `assertChatIsUnaddressed` re-homed), `server/service-writes-metadata.ts`, `server/service-writes-metadata-thread.ts` (`mayWriteThread` → sync `isThreadParticipant`), `server/service-tasks.ts`, `server/service-reads.ts` (`listAgents`), `server/service-writes-members.ts`, `server/repository-agents.ts`, `server/agents-dto.ts`, `server/errors.ts`, `server/http-mapping.ts`, `types.ts`, `constants.ts`. **web** — deleted `components/agent-chips-bar.tsx`, `components/mention-popup.tsx`, `lib/agent-engagement.ts`, `lib/mention.ts`, `lib/thread-agents.ts`, `lib/composer-commands.ts`, `lib/rooms.ts`; ### F-142: session pills replace agent chips — and the session engine could not be required at all (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

Phase 3 of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§3.3 + sequencing item 3). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "PHASE 3 OF THE ROLLBACK"**. Follows F-141 (Phase 2); §3.2, §3.4's composer half and §3.5 are NOT in this change.

**THE BLOCKER FOUND ON THE WAY IN, and it is the most important thing in this entry.** F-141 deleted `main/session-team.js` and its `require`, but left one line in session-engine.js's export object: `acceptsInboundFrom: sessionTeam.acceptsInboundFrom`. `module.exports = { … }` is EVALUATED, so requiring the session engine threw `ReferenceError: sessionTeam is not defined` — **no session engine, no session windows, no sessions at all**, on every launch since `5bf55ff`. The export had zero consumers anywhere in the tree. Nothing caught it and nothing could have: every session test in that suite reads the file as SOURCE and slices a pure block out of it (the extraction idiom that exists because the module is electron-bound), so no test ever `require`d it, and the desktop eslint config carries `max-lines` and nothing else. A green 2219-test suite over an app that could not start.

`test/main-exports-defined.test.mjs` is the general guard, not a one-off assertion: for every `main/*.js` it reads the `module.exports` literal and checks each name it EVALUATES against what the file actually binds. The export block is the one region that runs at require time on every path, which is what makes an unbound name there always fatal and always worth policing, and what makes the check cheap (no parser, no module loading, no electron). Two details are load-bearing — it carries a POSITIVE CONTROL, because a filter that quietly stops matching reports a clean tree forever; and it blanks comments and string literals with a SCANNER rather than two regexes, because this tree writes glob paths in both (`src/features/*` in prose, `'~/.claude/**'` in code) and a naive block-comment pass swallowed hundreds of real lines and reported twelve phantom failures.

**THE MAPPING, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE MODULE.** Plan §2 lists "the web chip shows Idle while the desktop works" as one of three defects the rollback deletes rather than fixes. Its root was two readers deriving one fact two ways, so `main/session-summary.js` derives it ONCE and hands out the result; phase 5 lifts the same `list()` to MCP and adds no second derivation. Three decisions inside it:

1. **`awaiting_permission` reads as WORKING.** The turn has not ended — the SDK is blocked on a `canUseTool` promise and the session is one click from continuing. Calling that "idle" is the same lie the old chip told, in a new place.
2. **An unknown activity falls to IDLE, never working.** Both directions lie; they do not cost the same. "Working" over a dead session makes the operator wait for something with no end and no feedback, where "idle" over a working one makes them re-ask. The table is also re-derived from the engine's own source by the test, so a NEW activity added to the reducer fails rather than landing silently on that default.
3. **PHASE-FIRST for the terminal answers, ACTIVITY-SECOND for the rest**, because the two fields deliberately disagree in normal running (FIX #6 holds a phase while the activity moves; a park lands on `phase: 'awaiting_inbound'` when a message is held). Reading one field is how the old chip got it wrong.

**THE ENDED-SESSION RETENTION RULE, stated once so it is not re-litigated.** An ended pill lives exactly as long as its WINDOW. `settle` destroys the window on every end but the abandonment (M2b keeps it, because that end fires hours later with nobody present and a vanishing transcript is indistinguishable from a crash), so the abandoned session is the one with something left to open and the only one whose pill survives. **A pill is a HANDLE, not a tombstone** — one for a session with no window would answer its own click by recreating a fresh parked shell wearing a dead one's name, over the top of the transcript the operator was trying to read. The channel transcript already carries the record of what happened. So: no TTL, no ring, no persistence; the retained set is bounded by the window budget, the operator closing the window is the removal, and a destroyed window is swept on the next projection.

**THE GENERATOR SEAM.** `main/agent-names.js` is a port, not an import: the main process is CommonJS with no build step (`dopl-desktop-app/` is inert for the Vercel build) and cannot reach web-tree TypeScript. The alternatives were each worse — naming in the RENDERER makes a handle a property of one window (different per surface, re-rolled on reload, unavailable to §3.5's MCP read, which answers from main); naming on the SERVER needs a write this phase is under orders not to make; a build step for one 60-element array is not a trade. The copies are pinned from the tree that owns the canonical one (`agent-names-desktop-parity.test.ts`): same pool, same ORDER, and both pickers run over one corpus including deep exhaustion — the suffix rule sweeps the whole pool per round and that is exactly what a re-implementation gets wrong, invisibly to a pool diff.

**DELIBERATE, so a later pass does not "fix" them.**
- **Main pushes EVERY session and each consumer filters.** The list is bounded by the desktop's window budget, so there is nothing to page; a per-channel watch handshake would be a second thing to keep in step for no gain. `sessionsForChannel` is the only filter and it lives in the renderer.
- **`sessions.summaries` / `onSummaries` are SPA-only.** The remote preload's window loads the retired website; it does not grow capabilities. Parity is remote ⊆ SPA so nothing is violated, but the divergence is now an assertion naming both members rather than an inference from a check that happens to pass — the three silent outages `preload-parity.test.mjs` exists for all looked exactly like a legal asymmetry.
- **The engine diff is four statements** (`touch` on dispatch, `noteEnded` on settle, the bind, the require). session-engine.js is in the §2 zero-headroom cluster; the room was paid for by deleting the session-team history F-141 already carries in full, not by trimming live comments.
- **`agent_presence` is untouched** — sequencing item 7, and the plan says MEASURE.
- **No `thinking` state.** ~~It needs `includePartialMessages`, which is off; plan §3.3's own dependency note says so.~~ **Corrected by F-146 (2026-08-05): that reason was wrong.** The session window already renders a Thinking chip with no stream (`session-chrome.js#thinkingVisible`). The pill is blocked by its INPUT — `pillState` sees only `{ phase, activity, parked }`, never the transcript — not by the SDK. See F-146 and plan §3.3's corrected dependency note.

**STILL OPEN.** `test/live/` remains deleted (F-141 item 4 assigned its rebuild to this phase; it was not done, and the desktop still has no live-contract tier). §3.2's two composer pills, §3.4's composer half and §3.5's MCP ops are unstarted.

- Location: **desktop** — added `main/session-summary.js`, `main/agent-names.js`, `test/session-summary.test.mjs`, `test/main-exports-defined.test.mjs`; rewritten `main/session-engine.js`, `main/session-reopen.js`, `main/ui-bridge.js`, `main/shell-mode.js`, `main/index.js`, `renderer/app-preload.js`, `test/preload-parity.test.mjs`, `test/session-reopen.test.mjs`, `test/session-crash-teardown.test.mjs`, `test/session-decision-truth.test.mjs`. **web** — added `src/features/channels/components/session-pills-bar.tsx` + its test, `src/features/channels/server/agent-names-desktop-parity.test.ts`; rewritten `src/features/channels/components/channel-pane.tsx`, `src/shared/lib/spa-bridge.ts`. **SPA** — added `apps/desktop-ui/src/features/channels/session-pills-bar.test.tsx`; rewritten `apps/desktop-ui/src/lib/dopl-bridge.ts`.
- Found during: rollback plan Phase 3.
- Severity: bug (a load-time ReferenceError that disabled the whole session engine) + the §3.3 feature work.
- **Suite deltas.** Desktop `npm test` **2219 → 2254** (+35: the projection's mapping / naming / retention / push cases, the export-block guard, and the reopen branch). Root `npx vitest run` **2086 → 2100** (+14: the pills bar's web half and the generator parity). SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **121 → 138** (+17: the pills bar's DOM half). `packages/mcp-server` **445**, unchanged — no MCP surface moved this phase. Root typecheck clean, SPA typecheck clean, root eslint 0 errors / 2 known warnings, desktop eslint clean, `npx next build` clean.
- **Desktop version NOT bumped** — the rollback phases release together.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing).

### F-143: two composers, two pills, and the syntax neither of them needs any more (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

Phase 4 of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§3.2, with §3.1 as the semantics behind the first pill). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "PHASE 4 OF THE ROLLBACK"**. Follows F-142 (Phase 3); §3.5's MCP ops are NOT in this change.

**WHAT §3.2 IS ACTUALLY ABOUT, because "move the toggle into the input" undersells it.** Both composers had a second destination that only existed as TYPING: `/new-agent` in the channel, a leading `@their-agent` in the session window. Both were deleted with named agents (F-141), and the thing they had in common is the thing this phase fixes — an option nobody can discover is an option nobody has, and in both composers the undiscoverable one is the one that starts somebody else's machine. So both controls are **dropdowns showing every row with its consequence**, not two-state toggles: a toggle has the same defect in miniature, since the state you are not in is invisible until you click it. That reasoning is the reason to refuse a future "simplification" back to a toggle.

**THE MAIN COMPOSER'S DECISION DID NOT MOVE.** `lib/composer-mode.ts` keeps `buildComposerPayload` as the single decision — chat mode still cannot produce a thread, request mode still refuses rather than guessing a subject — and the diff there is copy plus a `hint` per slot. `SegmentedControl` left the row above the input for `ComposerIntentPill` inside the concave bubble, and **the row above went with it**: it was already request-only (addressee picker + subject), so message mode is now one field with two controls in it and no furniture. The chat slot reads **Message** (§3.2 names the states "message ↔ request", and the session pill beside it says "Message `<name>`"; two pills that are one control should not use two words for one half) while the wire value stays `chat`, because renaming a protocol field to improve a label is not a trade.

**THE SESSION WINDOW'S PILL IS THE `@` PICKER'S CAPABILITY MINUS ITS MACHINERY, and the subtraction is most of the entry.** Gone with the syntax: the tokenizer, the caret-position popup state machine, the prefix matcher, the alias table, the slug derivation with its self-collision guard, the "an unrecognized tag is not a tag" rule, the `aria-activedescendant` bookkeeping, and — the one that was a real hazard — **the keydown contention**. The `@` popup had to be delegated to FIRST inside the composer's one keydown handler so Enter-to-accept and Enter-to-send could not both fire, and its IME guard existed because a peer-tagged half-composed draft leaves the machine and cannot be recalled. A picked target is a variable: Enter means send in every state, the menu takes only Escape, and the delivered bytes are exactly what was typed rather than what survived a tag strip. `session-mention.js` + `session-mention-ui.js` were 544 lines with 943 of tests; `session-address.js` + `session-address-ui.js` are 442 with 488, and the tests that went away are the ones that were about the syntax rather than about the destination.

**THE NAME SEAM, and the alternative that was rejected.** The resting row names the window, and the handle is F-142's — `session:agent-name` → `session-summary.nameForSession(s)` — so the window and the channel pane's pill cannot disagree about what a session is called, which is the exact defect (two readers, two derivations) that module exists to prevent. Riding the `init` payload was the obvious alternative and is worse three ways: three emit sites build an `init` (`session-io`, `session-auth`, `session-park`) and each would have had to remember; `session-io.js` is AT the 500-line cap, so it was a split for one field; and a handle is a live fact main can always answer, not a fact that needs pinning in the replay ring. The handler is passive by construction — no `dispatch`, and deliberately **no `touch()`**, because a window asking its own name at mount is not the operator using it and must not defer a park.

**THE SAFETY RULE WORTH KEEPING.** `resolveTarget` is never a passthrough: a held `peer` collapses to the steer the moment the peer row leaves the menu. The peer name rides `init`, so a park's recreate or an auth hold's synthesized init can land a picked-on window in a session with no counterparty, and a stale `peer` would call `sendToPeer`, which posts with **no addressee at all**. Falling back keeps the words on this machine, which is the safe direction of the two.

**DELIBERATE, so a later pass does not "fix" them.**
- **The peer bubble came back with the peer half.** A post that paints nothing is a post the operator cannot tell happened. `peer_message` is its own stream kind (never a `turn` — my agent never saw it; never an `outbound` — my agent did not draft it), and its status line fails closed on anything but `ok === true`.
- **FIX F8 moved rather than being re-derived.** The no-`sendToPeer` refusal lives in the module that owns the send, so the version-skewed case says so in the stream and leaves the draft in the field instead of clearing it silently.
- **`session.js` stayed under the cap by giving the module MORE.** The first cut kept the refusal path and the async name read in the controller and hit 505. `createComposer` owns the control, its copy, the target state, the name read and the refusal — the arrangement `session-modes-ui.js` and `session-close-ui.js` already have.
- **The addressable set was not widened.** The renderer still sends no addressee (`sendToPeer(text)`), so a session reaches `{my agent} ∪ {the bound counterparty}` and `note()` states that limit out loud rather than leaving a one-row menu looking broken. Adding roster rows without widening the IPC would deliver every pick to the bound counterparty, which is a worse lie than the silence.

**A CONCURRENT WRITER WAS IN THIS TREE, AND IT COST ONE COMMIT.** A second agent session was implementing the same phase in the same working directory (`session-composer-pill.js` / `session-composer-pill-ui.js`, an `agentName` field added to `session-viewmodel.js`'s `init` case — the ride-on-init approach rejected above — and its own `nameForSession` in `session-summary.js`). It withdrew everything afterwards, but its `nameForSession` was on disk when `e12b5c9` staged that file, so **that commit shipped the function declared twice**; `c9911be` removes the withdrawn copy.

**Nothing in this tree could have caught the duplicate, and that is the reusable half.** Hoisting made the later declaration (the one with the null guard) live, so the behaviour was never wrong and all 2290 desktop tests passed over it. Three reasons it was invisible: `dopl-desktop-app/eslint.config.js` is deliberately light — `max-lines` and nothing else — so **`no-redeclare` never ran**; every `session-summary` test reads the module through the BEGIN-sentinel source extraction, which evaluates both declarations and returns whichever won; and `test/main-exports-defined.test.mjs` policies the EXPORT block, where an unbound name is fatal at require time (F-142's blocker). A duplicate BINDING is the other side of that same coin and has no guard at all. **If a future pass wants one rule in that config beyond `max-lines`, `no-redeclare` is the candidate with a real incident behind it.**

- Location: **desktop** — added `renderer/session/session-address.js`, `renderer/session/session-address-ui.js`, `test/session-address.test.mjs`, `test/session-address-dom.test.mjs`; rewritten `main/session-ipc.js`, `main/session-summary.js`, `renderer/session/session-preload.js`, `renderer/session/session.html`, `renderer/session/session.css`, `renderer/session/session.js`, `test/session-summary.test.mjs`. **web** — added `src/features/channels/components/composer-intent-pill.tsx`; rewritten `src/features/channels/components/message-composer.tsx` + its test, `src/features/channels/lib/composer-mode.ts`. **SPA** — added `apps/desktop-ui/src/features/channels/composer-intent-pill.test.tsx`.
- Found during: rollback plan Phase 4.
- Severity: feature work (§3.2), no bug found on the way in.
- **Suite deltas.** Desktop `npm test` **2254 → 2290** (+36: the pure address layer, the boot-DOM wiring, the markup/CSS/IPC pins, the name-seam agreement case). Root `npx vitest run` **2100 → 2101** (the composer's pinned surface was rewritten in place for the pill). SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **138 → 143** (+5: the pill's DOM half, the F-142 split). `packages/mcp-server` unchanged — **no MCP surface moved this phase** (§3.5 is next). Root typecheck clean, SPA typecheck clean, desktop eslint clean, root eslint 0 errors / 2 known pre-existing warnings (`use-boot-state.ts`, `proxy.ts`).
- **Desktop version NOT bumped** — the rollback phases release together.
- **NOT verified: a running app.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; Samuel reviews live.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master` (`9d7b874`, `e12b5c9`, `2d82faf`, `c9911be`), **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing, as F-142).

### F-144: the three session capabilities that replace summon/to_agent — one shipped whole, one exposed, one shipped as a contract with a flagged delivery gap (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

Phase 5 (final) of `docs/CHANNELS-ROLLBACK-PLAN.md` (§3.5 + sequencing item 5). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "PHASE 5 OF THE ROLLBACK"**. Follows F-143 (Phase 4). `summon_agent` / `to_agent` were deleted in F-141; this restores their *capabilities* in the session-centric model, for an EXTERNAL agent (Claude Desktop / Claude Code over MCP). **HONESTY OVER COMPLETENESS was the governing instruction, and two of the three capabilities have a deliberately-flagged remainder rather than a fabricated whole.**

**1. SPAWN-WITH-HANDOFF — shipped end to end, server → MCP → desktop, and it is the one that inverts a pinned rule.** F-140 pinned CASE 3: an external UNSTAMPED `create_thread` opens NOTHING on the operator's machine, because that external session awaits the reply itself and "a window would steal it". Handoff is the operator explicitly asking for exactly that steal, so it is correct — but only when DECLARED.
- **CASE 3 IS ABOUT AN UNSTAMPED CREATE, and F-145 corrects the framing that grew around it.** It was read (here and in `runtime-header.ts`) as "an external caller cannot open a window", which is not what the predicate says. `requesterTaskOpen` accepts EITHER stamp, and `desktop-session` is credential-agnostic on purpose, so an external MCP caller holding the account's own `dopl_at_*` token could ALREADY open a window by sending `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-session` — before handoff existed and independent of it. **So handoff did not widen a boundary; it gave a name and an audit trail to something already reachable**, and CASE 3 remains exactly true as written: no stamp and no declaration opens nothing. Pre-existing, not a regression, and the boundary that actually holds is the identity pair (against a peer) plus token custody (against anyone holding this account's credential).
- **The seam is `targeting.requesterTaskOpen`'s stamp conjunct**, which went from `if (!desktopRuntime(m)) return false;` to `if (!desktopRuntime(m) && !declaresHandoff(m)) return false;`. So handoff substitutes for the RUNTIME STAMP and nothing else — the identity pair (`authorUserId === me` AND `taskCreatedBy === me`) is untouched and still required, which is what makes a handoff unable to open a window on a peer's machine (a peer's create fails those two whatever it stamps). `declaresHandoff(m)` reads `metadata.handoff === true` strictly.
- **`handoff` is a reserved server-stamped metadata key**, on the runtime stamp's exact terms: `resolvePostMetadata` strips any caller copy and re-stamps `true` only from `PostMessageOptions.handoff`, forwarded by `service-tasks.postOpeningMessage` from the validated `TaskCreateSchema.handoff` field. A caller cannot forge it in raw metadata — pinned in `service-writes-metadata.test.ts`. It is stamped onto the OPENING MESSAGE (which always carries the thread tag the stamp requires).
- **The two P1 pins invert under the flag and hold without it.** `operator-typed-request.test.mjs` and `thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs` gain: a declared-handoff external create OPENS (predicate + dispatch), read strictly (only literal `true`), never substituting for the identity pair; the no-handoff external create still opens nothing (CASE 3 / Q3b DIRECTION 1, unchanged). A handoff session arms NO request strip (`requesterTypedByOperator` keys on `desktop-ui`), and `session-dispatch.diagRuntimeGateSkip` treats a declared handoff as clearing the stamp conjunct so it never mis-reports a handoff open as a version-skew skip.
- **The MCP `create_thread` result under handoff tells the external agent to STOP** — do not arm `await`, the operator's window carries the reply now — because a second watcher on one thread races the window for the reply.

**2. MESSAGE-A-SESSION — the peer direction ALREADY EXISTED and was exposed, not rebuilt; steer-my-own is a flagged desktop gap.** Messaging the peer's running session is a plain `op="post"` REQUEST addressed to the peer (or threaded into the exchange), which triggers their listener — Phase 1 / §3.1 semantics, already pinned in the post/create suites. Verified it still holds and pointed the description at it; **no new code.** The genuinely new bit — an EXTERNAL agent steering its OWN desktop session window — needs desktop IPC that does not exist (an external MCP post reaches the server, not a specific renderer window), so it is **FLAGGED, not half-built.**

**3. READ-SESSION-STATE — server READ CONTRACT + MCP op shipped and tested; the desktop-push DELIVERY is the flagged gap.** New op `dopl_channel(op="read_sessions")` → `client.listChannelSessions` (GET `/api/channels/sessions`) → `session-state-service.listSessionStates` → `repository-collab.listSessionStates` over the new `channel_sessions` table, OWN-SCOPED to the caller (`ctx.userId` + a RLS owner-select policy). Returns the `ChannelSessionState` shape — the SAME projection the pills show (F-142's "no second derivation"), `working`/`idle`/`ended`, NO `thinking`.
- **DELIVERY DECISION: push-on-state-change (plan §5 option a), not a heartbeat.** The desktop would write a row only when `session-summary.js`'s derived digest actually changes (it already coalesces to exactly that) — a handful of writes per session lifetime vs `agent_presence`'s ~120/hr. So this is the CHEAP side of §5's tradeoff and is what makes item 7 (retiring `agent_presence`) a net DB-load reduction rather than an increase. Option (b) — read an existing server signal — was rejected: `agent_presence` only knows "listener online", not per-session name/state/thread, so it cannot honestly answer "what is flint doing?".
- **THE WRITE (the desktop push) IS NOT WIRED THIS PHASE, and that is the honest boundary.** `session-summary.js` is deliberately network-free above its source-extraction sentinel, and the summary shape carries no `workspaceId` / session-key context the upsert needs, so giving it an authenticated writer + multi-workspace grouping is desktop architecture beyond a clean server+MCP phase. Per the scope's instruction, the server op + schema ship returning what's available (empty until the push lands) and the gap is FLAGGED loudly rather than faked: the op says "no live sessions being reported", never invents state, and when the push lands the same op renders it with no server change. The migration is written but **UNAPPLIED** — Samuel's gauntlet applies it and verifies it live.

**CONSENT POSTURE (documented for Samuel; restated 2026-08-05 by F-145).** Spawn-with-handoff opening a window+agent on the operator's machine from a remote trigger is a real capability, but the caller is the operator's OWN credential, the machine is the operator's own, and the opened session's tool use still gates through the untouched inbound consent card (§3.6). **The bound is the IDENTITY PAIR *plus* TOKEN CUSTODY, and it has to be stated as both.** The identity pair (`authorUserId === me` AND `taskCreatedBy === me`) is what a peer cannot forge, and it is unconditional. What it does not bound is the account itself: anyone holding a `dopl_at_*` for this account can act as this account, and that includes clearing the stamp conjunct by claiming `desktop-session` — so **gating the handoff behind a card would buy nothing, because the same window is reachable by claiming the other stamp** (see the CASE 3 correction above). **Call made: "I asked Claude to do this" is sufficient — no extra card for the window-open itself**, with the diag as the observable signal. The honest security statement is that a leaked device token is the threat, not a declared handoff. Flagged for Samuel to confirm before 1.9.0.

- Location: **server (app)** — added `src/features/channels/server/session-state-service.ts` + its test, `src/app/api/channels/sessions/route.ts`, `supabase/migrations/20260805120000_channel_sessions.sql`; rewritten `src/features/channels/schema.ts` (`handoff`), `types.ts` (`SessionPillState` / `ChannelSessionState`), `server/service-writes-metadata.ts` (+ test), `server/service-writes-lifecycle.ts`, `server/service-writes.ts`, `server/service-tasks.ts`, `server/collab-dto.ts`, `server/repository-collab.ts`, `server/service.ts`. **client** — rewritten `packages/dopl-client/src/channel-types.ts`, `channel.ts`, `client.ts`, `index.ts` (+ dist). **MCP** — added `packages/mcp-server/src/tools/channel-session-ops.test.ts`; rewritten `channel-schema.ts`, `channel.ts`, `channel-ops-read.ts` (`opReadSessions`), `channel-ops-threads.ts`, `channel-description.ts`, `parity.test.ts` (+ dist). **desktop** — rewritten `main/targeting.js` (`declaresHandoff`, the stamp conjunct), `main/session-dispatch.js` (the diag mirror), `test/operator-typed-request.test.mjs`, `test/thread-followup-predicate.test.mjs`.
- Found during: rollback plan Phase 5.
- Severity: feature work (§3.5); no bug found on the way in.
- **Suite deltas.** Desktop `npm test` **2290 → 2293** (+3: the handoff predicate + dispatch inversions). Root `npx vitest run` **2101 → 2109** (+8: the handoff metadata stamp/strip, the session-state service round-trip). `packages/mcp-server` **445 → 454** (+9: the read_sessions shape/empty/filter/neutralize, the handoff pass-through + result branch). SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **143** (unchanged — no SPA surface moved). Root typecheck clean, desktop eslint clean, root eslint 0 errors / 2 known pre-existing warnings (`use-boot-state.ts`, `proxy.ts`), `npx next build` + `vite build` clean. Both committed dist trees (`@dopl/client`, `@dopl/mcp-server`) rebuilt.
- **FLAGGED (not shipped this phase), in priority order** — ~~(a)~~ **CLOSED by F-147 (2026-08-05)**; (c) is still Samuel's gate and (b)/(d) are still open: (a) the read-session-state desktop PUSH — `session-summary.js` gaining an authenticated writer on state change + the workspace/session-key context the summary shape lacks; without it `read_sessions` returns empty live. (b) message-a-session's STEER-MY-OWN — an external agent posting into a specific open desktop session window, which needs new desktop IPC. ~~(c) the `channel_sessions` migration is UNAPPLIED~~ **APPLIED 2026-08-06** — the table exists and carries live rows, and `read_sessions` answered 200 with real state in the live harness. F-145's `PGRST205` degrade in `repository-collab.listSessionStates` is now dead code and can be deleted. (d) `thinking` is still unbuilt — ~~waits on streaming (item 6)~~ **corrected by F-146**: it waits on `pillState` gaining an input it does not have, not on the SDK. `agent_presence` retirement (item 7) is now unblocked to MEASURE against this store.
- **Desktop version NOT bumped** — Samuel bumps to 1.9.0 at ship; the rollback phases release together.
- **NOT verified: a running app / a live DB.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off, and the migration is unapplied; Samuel reviews live and runs the final gauntlet.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing, as F-142).

### F-145: the rollback review — an MCP layer that stripped removed params, a read that 500'd where it claimed `[]`, and four surviving behaviours whose guards were deleted with their features (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

A three-lens review of the whole channels rollback (F-140…F-144, all unpushed). Architecture rules now in **ENGINEERING §18 "THE ROLLBACK REVIEW FIXES"**. Several findings were proven by MUTATION rather than by reading — the code was deleted and the suite stayed green — and that is the shape of almost every item here: **the rollback deleted features correctly and deleted their guards along with them, including the guards on behaviour that survived.**

**1. THE MCP LAYER ACCEPTED-AND-DROPPED REMOVED PARAMS, AND A SHIPPED STRING TAUGHT ONE.** `closedThreadNote` — emitted on EVERY post into a closed thread — still read `to_agent="<handle>" starts that agent, to="<member>" triggers their machine`, contradicting the tool's own law two files over ("There is no way to address an agent by name"). It was not stale copy over a dead surface: the MCP SDK parses arguments with `safeParseAsync` on a NON-STRICT `z.object` (`server/mcp.js` `validateToolInput`), so `{op:'post',body:'hi',to_agent:'quartz'}` was ACCEPTED with `to_agent` silently dropped, the post landed UNADDRESSED, and `opPost` narrated success. The route layer already refuses that exact shape — `schema.ts#removedParam` declares the deleted params as `z.never()` because "a plain deletion is SILENT: zod strips unknown keys … the invisible-delivery failure the addressing contract existed to prevent" — so the MCP layer in front of it had the hole the route had closed, and the tool's own output taught the argument its own parser then swallowed. A test at `channel-closed-thread.test.ts:231` POSITIVELY ASSERTED the sentence, holding it in place through four phases.
- **STRICT SCHEMA — the decision, with the SDK probed rather than assumed.** `server.ts`'s `registerTool` / `registerMetaTool` now go through `server.registerTool(name, {description, inputSchema: strictInput(shape)}, cb)` with `strictInput = z.strictObject`. **The positional `server.tool()` overload cannot do this**: `isZodRawShapeCompat` returns false for a schema INSTANCE and the arm below reads the object as annotations, so `tool()` + a ZodObject throws "expected a Zod schema or ToolAnnotations". The config-object form takes a built schema and `normalizeObjectSchema` passes an object schema through untouched. Probed against the pinned SDK (1.29.x, zod 4.3.6): the published JSON Schema is **byte-identical apart from a gained `additionalProperties: false`** — no property, description, cap or `required` entry moves — and an unknown key becomes `-32602 … Unrecognized key: "to_agent"`, which NAMES the field, so a calling agent can correct itself instead of believing a delivery that never happened. **Cost, stated: a caller that today sends a stray key gets an error where it used to get a silent strip. That is the point, and it is the same trade the route made.** Applied to ALL tools, not just `dopl_channel` — removed vocabulary is one way a model reaches a param that does not exist; a stale cached tool list and plain invention are others.
- **`tool()` is the regression to watch for**, so all four `McpServer` test mocks (`server.test.ts`, `factory.test.ts`, `narration.test.ts`, and the new one) expose `registerTool` and deliberately NOT `tool` — a revert is a TypeError naming the method, not a silent re-registration.
- **The vocabulary guard now scans every SHIPPED STRING.** `channel-law.test.ts` read `CHANNEL_DESCRIPTION` and `channel-discovery.test.ts` read `buildInstructions`; neither could see a result line. It now parses every non-test `channel-*.ts` with the TypeScript compiler API and tests every string/template LITERAL (comments are not literals, so a comment may still discuss `to_agent`). No allowlist, deliberately — the ad-hoc legend's "no participants" became "no recorded parties" rather than earning an exemption.

**2. `read_sessions` 500'd where four places said it returned `[]`.** `repository-collab.listSessionStates` selected from `channel_sessions` and rethrew; the table is UNAPPLIED (F-144 flagged it, both reviewers confirmed it live), PostgREST answered `PGRST205`, and `mapChannelError` has no arm for a raw driver error → `INTERNAL_ERROR` 500. The op was broken in exactly the state its own documentation called normal. It now degrades that ONE code to `[]` — matched on `error.code`, never on the message prose — and **rethrows everything else**, because permission denied / a moved column / a dead connection mean the answer is UNKNOWN and an empty list is a CLAIM (F-144's own "honesty over completeness"). Delete the degrade once the table is applied and the push has landed.

**3. THE AGENT-ATTRIBUTION STRIP HAD NO TEST AT ALL.** `service-writes-metadata.ts` deletes `to_agent_id` / `to_agent_ids` / `author_agent_id`; **deleting each line individually left all 2109 root tests green.** Every sibling reserved key (`intent`, `handoff`, `to_user_id`, `session_id`, `summary`, `runtime`, `appVersion`) has a firing SECURITY test; these three lost theirs when `service-writes-metadata-agents.test.ts` was deleted whole with the feature. **The feature went and the reason did not:** the WRITE path is gone, the READ path is deliberately alive (stored rows still render "quartz · Ada's agent"), so these are display credentials with a live reader and NO legitimate writer — which makes the strip the entire boundary between a caller and attributing their own words to somebody's retired agent. `service-writes-metadata-attribution.test.ts` is the guard; each of the three lines is individually mutation-proven, and it covers the type-blind cases (a `delete` must not become a "valid-looking id" filter) and the `source: "user"` lane.

**4. THREE SURVIVING BEHAVIOURS LOST THEIR GUARDS (each mutation-proven green at 2293).**
- **DM recipient naming (H2)** — whether a permission card says "To: David" or names nobody. Its only test was a MIXED file, `session-addressee-truth.test.mjs`, deleted whole because its composer-mention section covered a deleted syntax; the N-party half survives elsewhere, the H2 half survived NOWHERE (not one desktop test mentioned `directChannel`). **The rule that saved `service-writes-members.test.ts` applies: a mixed file whose feature is deleted is REWRITTEN down to what survives.** Restored as `test/session-dm-addressee-truth.test.mjs`, minus the genuinely-gone mention section. Red/green: dropping the `directChannel` conjunct in `session-labels.js:185` fails 4, in `session-render.js:146` fails 3, and dropping main's stamp at `session-io.js:386` fails 1.
- **Declined-body scrubbing** — `session-gate.js:260`'s `io.noteGatedBody(s, head.message)` was removable with nothing failing, while its twin at `:176` has seven tests. It is written as a belt, and **a belt with no test is a line a future reader deletes as dead** — after which a refused message body reaches the next session's history seed. `test/session-decline-scrub.test.mjs` pins the FUNCTION's contract (whatever queued the card, DECLINE records the body before the decline dispatches), the head-not-latest rule, the fail-closed verdict mapping, and the source ORDER. Red: 4 of 6 fail without the line.
- **FIX F8 version-skew refusal** — `session-address-ui.js:230` answers false and paints a notice when the preload has no `sendToPeer`; `session-address-dom.test.mjs` always installed a working stub, so the path was never driven. Added there on the real booted controller by taking the bridge member away (the shape a skewed preload presents) and putting it back. Red: removing the guard fails the case.

**5. A NEW TEST ADVERTISED A GUARANTEE IT DID NOT CHECK.** `operator-typed-request.test.mjs`'s "main attaches X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-ui" pinned the SYMBOLIC form `/\[RUNTIME_HEADER\]: DESKTOP_UI_RUNTIME,/`, so rewriting either constant's VALUE stayed green while the entire Phase-1 stamp stopped matching `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts`. There is **no shared module across that join** (main is CommonJS Electron), so both sides agree by literal or not at all. Both literals are now pinned on both sides and COMPARED against the web tree's own source, so a change from either direction fails. **The file was at EXACTLY 500 lines** (the §2 cap) doing two jobs, so the stamp block moved to `test/runtime-stamp-literals.test.mjs`, which also closes the gap `sdk-grant.test.mjs` could not see: whether the `desktop-session` literal — from BOTH spawn paths, including the headless CLI one nothing pinned — is still what the server recognizes.

**6. THE HANDOFF CREATE OVER-PROMISED.** `channel-ops-threads.ts` told the external agent "A full session is opening on your operator's Dopl app … Do NOT arm op=await … You are done", unconditionally, off nothing but the caller's own flag. The server has no evidence of any of it: `session-dispatch.maybeOpenRequesterSession` returns false SILENTLY when window mode is off, when `requesterTaskOpen` refuses, when the window budget is spent, and when the desktop is not running at all. **"You are done" on a handoff nobody picked up leaves the exchange with NO watcher** — the failure the whole wake-guidance module exists to prevent, and the NON-handoff branch is careful about exactly this. The copy now states the REQUEST as a request, keeps the do-not-race default (a real handoff is the common case and two watchers on one thread is a genuine failure), and restores the fallback: notice with `op="get_thread"`, and the real `since=` cursor if nothing picks it up.

**7. STALE ERROR GUIDANCE ON THE HTTP LANE.** `ChannelChatAddressedError` told callers "Mention an agent with toAgents to have it act" — correct for the four days between the 2026-07-31 narrowing and rollback §1, after which `toAgents` is a `z.never()`, so **following the error produced a second 400 naming the param the first error had just recommended**. Its docblock still recorded the narrowing as current. The MCP twin was rewritten at the time and this one was missed because nothing pinned the sentence; it is pinned now, including a cross-lane check that both refusals offer the same decision.

**8. MIGRATION HARDENING, before the desktop writer lands.** `20260805120000_channel_sessions.sql` (UNAPPLIED, so edited in place rather than followed by a patch migration).
- **The index rule, and the two precedents that looked opposed.** `20260731150000` dropped an index with no query behind it; `20260802180000` added a covering index for every unindexed FK. **They do not conflict — a foreign key IS a query.** The engaged index was a PARTIAL index on a non-FK column; an FK cover is named by every cascading delete. So `channel_sessions_workspace_idx` STAYS (it is the `workspaces` cascade's cover, not a dead read index — the reviewer's "no query behind it" is right about reads and misses the cascade), `(user_id, channel_id)` GOES and `(channel_id)` takes its place (the old one served the narrowed read but covered no FK, and `user_id` is already led by the unique upsert key), and `task_id` gains the cover it never had. Four indexes, one per reason, all four FK columns covered as a LEADING column — which is what `unindexed_foreign_keys` checks.
- **`channel_name` / `thread_title` are CHECK-bounded** on `20260731100000_channels_name_topic_bounds.sql`'s class, at the bounds of what they mirror (120 / 200). The comment that justified leaving them open cited a desktop neutralizer — **a claim about a writer that does not exist yet**, and the first code to write these columns will be written by someone reading this file.
- **`updated_at` is stamped by a table-scoped trigger**, not by the writer. The read is `ORDER BY updated_at DESC`; an upsert that omits the column freezes the ordering with no error and no empty result — a plausible, stale, wrongly-ordered list.
- **`?channelId=` is validated** (`SessionStateQuerySchema`) — it went straight into `.eq()`, so a non-uuid was a Postgres cast error and a 500 for a malformed request.
- **`state` renders through a closed-set test.** It was the one field on `read_sessions`' line spliced RAW into server narration, guarded only by a CHECK in an unapplied migration plus an unchecked cast; `formatSessionLine` now says `(unrecognized state)` for anything outside the three. Unreachable today, defended anyway — the writer lands later and this is the layer that has to hold.

**9. THE SECURITY PROSE WAS MATERIALLY WRONG IN FOUR PLACES, AND THE UNDERLYING ISSUE IS DELIBERATELY NOT "FIXED".** `narrowRuntime` bounds ONLY `desktop-ui`; `desktop-session` returns unconditionally for ANY credential and `targeting.requesterTaskOpen` accepts either — so a `dopl_at_*` holder sending `X-Dopl-Runtime: desktop-session` on a create of its own CAN open a window and start an agent on that account's Mac. **Pre-existing and NOT a regression** (there was no credential bound at all before Phase 1, so the rollback tightened this) and **not a peer's path** (the identity pair holds unconditionally). But `runtime-header.ts` said "an external MCP post can never acquire this stamp no matter what header it sends", and `with-workspace-auth.test.ts`'s docblock, F-140 and F-144's CASE 3 all read the same way — a future reviewer would have leaned on it. All four now say the bound is on the `desktop-ui` VALUE, not on the window, and **F-144's consent posture is restated as "the identity pair PLUS token custody"**, recording that gating handoff behind a card would buy nothing while the other stamp is reachable. **Not fixed by bounding `desktop-session`**: a legitimate desktop session authenticates with exactly that device token, so the value must stay credential-agnostic. **The real boundary is token custody, and that is flagged for Samuel rather than papered over.**

- Location: **MCP** — added `packages/mcp-server/src/strict-args.test.ts`; rewritten `src/server.ts` (`strictInput`, both registrars), `src/server.test.ts`, `src/factory.test.ts`, `src/tools/narration.test.ts`, `channel-post-linkage.ts`, `channel-render-threads.ts`, `channel-ops-threads.ts`, `channel-ops-read.ts`, `channel-law.test.ts`, `channel-closed-thread.test.ts`, `channel-session-ops.test.ts` (+ dist). **server (app)** — added `src/features/channels/server/repository-collab.test.ts`, `service-writes-metadata-attribution.test.ts`; rewritten `repository-collab.ts`, `session-state-service.ts`, `collab-dto.ts`, `errors.ts`, `schema.ts` (`SessionStateQuerySchema`), `schema-query.test.ts`, `service-writes-intent.test.ts`, `src/app/api/channels/sessions/route.ts`, `src/shared/auth/runtime-header.ts`, `with-workspace-auth.test.ts`. **desktop** — added `test/session-dm-addressee-truth.test.mjs`, `test/session-decline-scrub.test.mjs`, `test/runtime-stamp-literals.test.mjs`; rewritten `test/operator-typed-request.test.mjs` (trimmed off the cap), `test/session-address-dom.test.mjs`. **db** — `supabase/migrations/20260805120000_channel_sessions.sql`.
- Found during: a three-lens review of the rollback, before 1.9.0.
- Severity: bug (a silently-dropped addressing param; a 500 on a documented empty read; a 500 on a malformed query) + missing guards (one SECURITY test and three behavioural ones, all mutation-proven absent) + materially false security prose.
- **Suite deltas.** Desktop `npm test` **2293 → 2313** (+20: H2 restored 9, decline scrub 6, the F8 refusal 3, the literal join +2 net after the split) + desktop eslint clean. Root `npx vitest run` **2109 → 2133** (+24: the attribution SECURITY set 7, the PGRST205 repository set 11, the query schema 2, the refusal copy 4) + `tsc --noEmit` clean + root eslint 0 errors / 2 known pre-existing warnings (`use-boot-state.ts`, `proxy.ts`). `packages/mcp-server` **454 → 483** (+29: the strict-args end-to-end set 6, the per-file shipped-string scan 19, the state closed-set 2, the handoff hedge +2) + mcp `tsc` clean. SPA `npm test -w @dopl/desktop-ui` **143** (unchanged — no SPA surface moved). `npx next build` + `vite build` clean. `@dopl/mcp-server`'s committed dist rebuilt; `@dopl/client`'s is untouched because no source of its moved.
- **NOT FIXED, with reasoning.** (a) The `desktop-session` credential bound — see item 9: bounding it refuses the caller it exists for, and the reachable window is the account's own credential acting as the account. Documented; token custody flagged as the real boundary. (b) The `channel_sessions` desktop PUSH — still F-144's flagged gap; this change makes the empty answer HONEST rather than building the writer. (c) `mapChannelError` gained no generic arm for raw PostgREST errors — a blanket mapping would turn every unmodelled DB fault into a shaped 4xx, which is the opposite of what the 500 is for; the degrade is one code, at the one call site that can honestly answer empty.
- **Desktop version NOT bumped** — Samuel bumps to 1.9.0 at ship.
- **NOT verified: a running app / a live DB.** Standing instruction — no self-screenshot sign-off; the migration is still unapplied, so the PGRST205 degrade is proven by the exact PostgREST envelope in unit tests and not against a live database. Samuel reviews live and runs the final gauntlet.
- Status: **resolved**; committed on `master`, **unpushed**. KB sync skipped (standing, as F-142).

### F-146: the residue pass — nine dead symbols, a doc claiming a correction it never made, and 4 963 tests CI has never run (2026-08-05, master) — RESOLVED

The three-lens review of the channels rollback found the ship-blockers (fixed as F-145) and, underneath them, a layer of residue: code with no caller, prose that survived the code it described, guards the incidents had earned but nobody added, and one coverage hole with a live consumer. This is that layer. **No behaviour changed anywhere in this finding** except the two lint rules and the CI workflow.

**THE PATTERN, because it is what a future rollback should design against: PARTIAL-FILE UPDATES.** Almost every stale comment sat two paragraphs from a CORRECT rollback annotation in the same file. `targeting.js:160` and `:176` say the module is gone while `:68` and `:168` describe it acting in the present tense. `channel-render.ts:253` says the agent-address clause went while `:230-241` documents its output format. `service-writes-members.ts:27` records the engagement sweep as deleted while `:95` `{@link}`s the deleted helper. The rollback swept by SYMBOL and the residue is wherever a symbol was named in prose without being named in code — which is exactly the set a grep for the symbol finds, and exactly why item 4b below turns that grep into a test.

**1. DEAD CODE, each verified before removal.**
